         39.1.4 The WordStar Diamond & Cursor Navigation (^E, ^X, ^S, ^D, ^A, ^F, ^R, ^C, ^W, ^Z, ^Q commands)
         39.1.5 Text Insertion, Deletion & Word Wrap (^G, ^H, ^T, ^Y, ^QY, ^V, ^OW)
         39.1.6 Block Operations (^KB, ^KK, ^KC, ^KV, ^KY, ^KW, ^KR)
         39.1.7 Search and Replace Operations (^QF, ^QA, ^L)
         39.1.8 Page Formatting, Margins & Paragraph Reform (^OC, ^OL, ^OR, ^B)
         39.1.9 Dot Commands Reference (.he, .fo, .pa, .op, .pn, .lm, .rm, .pl, .mt, .mb)
         39.1.10 File Saving, Document Exiting & Printing (^KS, ^KD, ^KX, ^KQ, P command)
         39.1.11 Practical Tutorial: Writing and Printing a Professional Business Document
     39.2 CalcStar 1.4 (CS.COM) Financial Spreadsheet User Guide
         39.2.1 Overview & Architecture: 26 Columns (A..Z) x 255 Rows (1..255)

         39.2.4 Data Entry: Labels, Numeric Constants, and Formulas
         39.2.5 Formula Syntax, Operators (+, -, *, /, ^) & Relative/Absolute Cell References
         39.2.6 Built-in Mathematical & Statistical Functions Reference (SUM, AVG, CNT, MIN, MAX, ABS, INT, SQRT, ROUND, IF, NPV)
         39.2.7 The Slash (/) Command System Reference (/F Format, /C Copy, /M Move, /I Insert, /D Delete, /W Width, /B Blank, /A Auto-Calc, /R Recalc, /S Save, /L Load, /P Print, /Q Quit)
         39.2.8 Practical Tutorial: Constructing an Annual Business Budget Model
     39.3 DataStar 1.4 (DS.COM & FORMSORT.COM) Forms Database Management User Guide

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 1. SYSTEM OVERVIEW & ENVIRONMENT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

BasinPy Extended is a high-performance, cycle-authentic Sinclair ZX Spectrum 48K
and Beta BASIC interpreter engineered in Python utilizing pygame-ce for real-time
CRT audio/video rendering. BasinPy combines the classic feel of Sinclair BASIC
with powerful professional extensions, full peripheral emulation, and a modern
dual-panel IDE.

1.1 System Architecture & Display Engine
-----------------------------------------
The display subsystem reproduces the authentic 256x192 pixel Sinclair ZX
Spectrum graphic bitmap surrounded by a 32-pixel border, producing an emulated
320x256 frame buffer with a 1.25 (5:4) aspect ratio. Colours are rendered using
the official Sinclair color palette across Normal (0-7) and Bright (8-15) levels:
  0: Black   1: Blue   2: Red   3: Magenta   4: Green   5: Cyan   6: Yellow   7: White
Each 8x8 character cell maintains independent INK, PAPER, BRIGHT, and FLASH
attribute bytes exactly matching Sinclair hardware architecture.

1.2 Dual-Pane IDE & Screen Modes
---------------------------------
BasinPy features an integrated dual-panel workspace:
  - LEFT PANEL:  Interactive BASIC Program Listing and Editor
  - RIGHT PANEL: Simulated ZX Spectrum CRT Canvas with Border and Status
BasinPy also supports a dedicated Fullscreen CRT mode (toggled via F11 or RUN/FS),
which centers the authentic Spectrum CRT canvas on any display.

1.3 Startup Display & Splash Screen
------------------------------------
When BasinPy loads, it presents an authentic Sinclair ZX Spectrum splash:
  - The classic Sinclair ZX Spectrum logo with 4 vibrant diagonal/horizontal
    colour stripes (Red, Yellow, Green, Cyan) on a deep black border/paper.
  - Centered formal credits:
      BasinPy Extended
      Produced by Gemini AI
      Devised by Karl Sheldon
      Influenced by BasinC, (C) 2003 Paul Dunn, (C) 2011 Arda Erdikmen
Pressing any key or clicking dismisses the splash and enters the interactive IDE.

1.4 Editor Modes & Cursor Status Indicators
--------------------------------------------
BasinPy utilizes authentic Sinclair cursor modes at the prompt:
  K >  Keyword Mode   - Expects a BASIC command or statement keyword.
  L >  Letter Mode    - Accepts general alphanumeric text, variables, or data.
  E >  Extended Mode  - Active when entering extended functions or operators.
  G >  Graphics Mode  - Inputs User Defined Graphics (UDGs 'a' through 'u').
  F >  Forth Mode     - Active when the interactive Abersoft Forth engine is running.
  Z >  Zeus Mode      - Active when the Sinclair Zeus Z80 Assembler is running.
  M >  Monitor Mode   - Active when the Crystal Computing Z80 Machine Code Monitor is running.

1.5 Keyboard Shortcuts & Navigation Controls
---------------------------------------------
  F5         : Run current BASIC program from the first line (RUN)
  F11        : Toggle between Dual-Pane IDE and Fullscreen CRT mode
  Escape/End : Break / Halt running program or stop tape simulation
  Up / Down  : Navigate program listing lines or recall command history
  Enter      : Submit command line or store edited program line
  Click      : Clicking any line in the left listing pane puts it into inline edit mode

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 2. SECTION 1: STANDARD SINCLAIR ZX SPECTRUM 48K BASIC
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Target Extension:    Standard Sinclair ZX Spectrum 48K BASIC
Compilation Support: [48K BASIC COMPATIBLE] / [BORIEL ZXBC COMPATIBLE]
                     (Fully compatible with standard Sinclair 48K ROM BASIC and Boriel's ZXBC
                     compiler. Can be compiled directly to standalone Z80 machine code or run
                     on any real or emulated ZX Spectrum 16K/48K/128K/+2/+3).

This section documents every standard Sinclair ZX Spectrum 48K command, statement,
function, operator, and print control item, complete with syntax and code examples.

2.1 Program Control & Execution Statements
------------------------------------------
Command/Keyword: RUN
Syntax:          RUN [line_number]
Description:     Clears variables and starts program execution from line_number (or the first line if omitted).
Example:         10 RUN 100

Command/Keyword: GOTO / GO TO
Syntax:          GOTO line_number
Description:     Transfers program execution unconditionally to the specified line number.
Example:         50 GOTO 10

Command/Keyword: GOSUB / GO SUB
Syntax:          GOSUB line_number
Description:     Pushes the next statement address onto the call stack and jumps to the subroutine at line_number.
Example:         100 GOSUB 2000

Command/Keyword: RETURN
Syntax:          RETURN
Description:     Pops the return address from the call stack and resumes execution after the matching GOSUB.
Example:         2050 RETURN

Command/Keyword: FOR..TO..STEP..NEXT
Syntax:          FOR var = start TO finish [STEP step] ... NEXT var
Description:     Executes a looping block of code with an incrementing or decrementing numeric loop variable.
Example:         10 FOR i = 1 TO 10 STEP 2: PRINT i: NEXT i

Command/Keyword: IF..THEN
Syntax:          IF condition THEN statement
Description:     Evaluates a conditional expression; executes the statement following THEN if condition is non-zero.
Example:         40 IF x > 100 THEN PRINT "LIMIT EXCEEDED": STOP

Command/Keyword: STOP
Syntax:          STOP
Description:     Terminates program execution and prints the classic '9 STOP statement' report.
Example:         90 STOP

Command/Keyword: CONTINUE / CONT
Syntax:          CONTINUE or CONT
Description:     Resumes program execution at the statement following the point where it was halted by STOP or BREAK.
Example:         CONT

Command/Keyword: PAUSE
Syntax:          PAUSE frames
Description:     Suspends program execution for the specified number of TV screen frames (50 frames = 1 second) or until a key is pressed. PAUSE 0 waits indefinitely.
Example:         50 PAUSE 100

Command/Keyword: NEW
Syntax:          NEW
Description:     Clears the entire program, variables, and buffers, resetting the interpreter to initial power-on state.
Example:         NEW

Command/Keyword: CLEAR
Syntax:          CLEAR [address]
Description:     Clears all variables and strings. If address is specified, sets RAMTOP to reserve memory for machine code.
Example:         10 CLEAR 65000

Command/Keyword: REM
Syntax:          REM [comment text]
Description:     Marks the remainder of the line as a non-executable developer remark or comment.
Example:         10 REM MAIN GAME INITIALIZATION


2.2 Screen Display, Colour & Audio Commands
-------------------------------------------
Command/Keyword: BORDER
Syntax:          BORDER colour
Description:     Sets the colour of the outer border area (0 to 7).
Example:         10 BORDER 1

Command/Keyword: PAPER
Syntax:          PAPER colour
Description:     Sets the background colour for subsequent printing and graphics (0 to 7).
Example:         20 PAPER 7

Command/Keyword: INK
Syntax:          INK colour
Description:     Sets the foreground colour for subsequent characters and drawing operations (0 to 7).
Example:         30 INK 0

Command/Keyword: BRIGHT
Syntax:          BRIGHT mode
Description:     Controls high-intensity colour mode (0 = normal luminance, 1 = bright luminance).
Example:         40 BRIGHT 1

Command/Keyword: FLASH
Syntax:          FLASH mode
Description:     Controls automatic character attribute flashing (0 = off, 1 = on).
Example:         50 FLASH 1

Command/Keyword: INVERSE
Syntax:          INVERSE mode
Description:     Swaps foreground INK and background PAPER colors when printing (0 = normal, 1 = inverted).
Example:         60 INVERSE 1

Command/Keyword: OVER
Syntax:          OVER mode
Description:     Controls transparent and XOR plotting/printing (0 = overwrite, 1 = XOR overlay).
Example:         70 OVER 1

Command/Keyword: CLS
Syntax:          CLS
Description:     Clears the Spectrum CRT screen bitmap and resets character cell attributes to current PAPER and INK.
Example:         80 CLS

Command/Keyword: PLOT
Syntax:          PLOT x, y
Description:     Draws a single pixel at horizontal coordinate x (0-255) and vertical coordinate y (0-191).
Example:         100 PLOT 128, 96

Command/Keyword: DRAW
Syntax:          DRAW dx, dy [, arc]
Description:     Draws a straight line (or circular arc) from the current plot coordinate by relative offsets dx, dy.
Example:         110 DRAW 50, 30

Command/Keyword: CIRCLE
Syntax:          CIRCLE x, y, radius
Description:     Draws a circle centered at coordinate (x, y) with the specified radius in pixels.
Example:         120 CIRCLE 128, 96, 40

Command/Keyword: BEEP
Syntax:          BEEP duration, pitch
Description:     Plays a single-voice tone through the built-in speaker for duration (seconds) at semitone pitch offset from middle C.
Example:         130 BEEP 0.5, 7

Command/Keyword: COPY
Syntax:          COPY
Description:     Transfers the current 256x192 screen bitmap to the Sinclair ZX Printer.
Example:         140 COPY


2.3 Variable, Array & Data Storage Statements
---------------------------------------------
Command/Keyword: LET
Syntax:          LET var = expression
Description:     Assigns the value of an expression to a numeric or string variable.
Example:         10 LET score = 500: LET name$ = "PLAYER 1"

Command/Keyword: DIM
Syntax:          DIM array_name(d1 [, d2, ...])
Description:     Dimensions a numeric or string array with specified bounds.
Example:         20 DIM a(10, 10): DIM names$(20, 30)

Command/Keyword: DATA
Syntax:          DATA item1, item2, ...
Description:     Stores constant numeric and string data items within the program for retrieval by READ.
Example:         100 DATA 10, 20, 30, "BONUS", 50

Command/Keyword: READ
Syntax:          READ var1, var2, ...
Description:     Reads sequential values from program DATA statements into the specified variables.
Example:         30 READ x, y, z, bonus$, pts

Command/Keyword: RESTORE
Syntax:          RESTORE [line_number]
Description:     Resets the internal DATA pointer to the beginning of the program or to the specified line number.
Example:         40 RESTORE 100

Command/Keyword: DEF FN
Syntax:          DEF FN name(param1, ...) = expression
Description:     Defines a single-line user-defined mathematical or string function.
Example:         10 DEF FN d(x, y) = SQR(x*x + y*y)


2.4 Mathematical & Trigonometric Functions
------------------------------------------
Function:        ABS
Syntax:          ABS(x)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the absolute (non-negative) magnitude of numeric expression x.
Example:         PRINT ABS(-42)

Function:        ACS
Syntax:          ACS(x)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the arccosine of x in radians (x must be between -1.0 and 1.0).
Example:         PRINT ACS(0.5)

Function:        ASN
Syntax:          ASN(x)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the arcsine of x in radians (x must be between -1.0 and 1.0).
Example:         PRINT ASN(1.0)

Function:        ATN
Syntax:          ATN(x)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the arctangent of x in radians.
Example:         PRINT ATN(1.0)

Function:        COS
Syntax:          COS(x)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the cosine of angle x (where x is expressed in radians).
Example:         PRINT COS(PI/3)

Function:        EXP
Syntax:          EXP(x)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns natural base e raised to the power of x (e^x).
Example:         PRINT EXP(1)

Function:        INT
Syntax:          INT(x)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the greatest integer less than or equal to x (floor).
Example:         PRINT INT(9.87)

Function:        LN
Syntax:          LN(x)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the natural logarithm (base e) of positive number x.
Example:         PRINT LN(10)

Function:        PI
Syntax:          PI
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the mathematical constant Pi (approximately 3.14159265).
Example:         PRINT 2 * PI * r

Function:        RND
Syntax:          RND
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns a pseudo-random floating point number in the range [0.0, 1.0).
Example:         PRINT INT(RND * 6) + 1

Function:        RANDOMIZE
Syntax:          RANDOMIZE [seed]
Returns:         Statement
Description:     Seeds the pseudo-random number generator (uses internal timer if seed is omitted).
Example:         RANDOMIZE

Function:        SGN
Syntax:          SGN(x)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the sign of x: -1 if negative, 0 if zero, 1 if positive.
Example:         PRINT SGN(-15)

Function:        SIN
Syntax:          SIN(x)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the sine of angle x (where x is expressed in radians).
Example:         PRINT SIN(PI/2)

Function:        SQR
Syntax:          SQR(x)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the square root of non-negative number x.
Example:         PRINT SQR(144)

Function:        TAN
Syntax:          TAN(x)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the tangent of angle x (where x is expressed in radians).
Example:         PRINT TAN(PI/4)


2.5 String Manipulation Functions
---------------------------------
Function:        CHR$
Syntax:          CHR$(code)
Returns:         String
Description:     Returns a single-character string corresponding to Sinclair character code (0-255).
Example:         PRINT CHR$(65)

Function:        CODE
Syntax:          CODE(string$)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the numeric character code of the first character in string$.
Example:         PRINT CODE("A")

Function:        INKEY$
Syntax:          INKEY$
Returns:         String
Description:     Reads the keyboard instantaneously; returns the pressed character or empty string.
Example:         10 IF INKEY$ = "q" THEN STOP

Function:        LEN
Syntax:          LEN(string$)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the number of characters in string$.
Example:         PRINT LEN("SINCLAIR")

Function:        STR$
Syntax:          STR$(x)
Returns:         String
Description:     Converts numeric value x into its decimal string representation.
Example:         PRINT "SCORE: " + STR$(s)

Function:        VAL
Syntax:          VAL(string$)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Evaluates string$ as a numeric expression and returns the resulting number.
Example:         PRINT VAL("12.5") * 2

Function:        VAL$
Syntax:          VAL$(string$)
Returns:         String
Description:     Evaluates string$ as a string expression and returns the evaluated string.
Example:         PRINT VAL$("""HELLO "" + ""WORLD""")


2.6 Screen Pixel & Attribute Inspection Functions
-------------------------------------------------
Function:        ATTR
Syntax:          ATTR(row, col)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the Sinclair attribute byte at character cell row (0-23) and col (0-31). Formula: ATTR = FLASH*128 + BRIGHT*64 + PAPER*8 + INK.
Example:         PRINT ATTR(10, 15)

Function:        POINT
Syntax:          POINT(x, y)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Tests screen pixel at coordinate (x, y); returns 1 if pixel is plotted, 0 if blank.
Example:         IF POINT(100, 50) = 1 THEN BEEP 0.1, 10

Function:        SCREEN$
Syntax:          SCREEN$(row, col)
Returns:         String
Description:     Returns the character currently displayed at character position (row, col).
Example:         PRINT SCREEN$(0, 0)


2.7 Low-Level Hardware & Port Access Functions
----------------------------------------------
Function:        PEEK
Syntax:          PEEK(address)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Reads an 8-bit unsigned byte value (0-255) from the specified 16-bit memory address.
Example:         PRINT PEEK(23672)

Command/Keyword: POKE
Syntax:          POKE address, value
Description:     Writes an 8-bit byte value (0-255) to the specified 16-bit memory address.
Example:         POKE 23624, 2

Function:        IN
Syntax:          IN(port)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Reads an 8-bit byte from the specified Z80 I/O port address.
Example:         PRINT IN(31)

Command/Keyword: OUT
Syntax:          OUT port, value
Description:     Outputs an 8-bit byte value to the specified Z80 I/O port address.
Example:         OUT 254, 1

Function:        USR
Syntax:          USR(address)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Calls machine code at address or returns the starting memory address of a UDG character.
Example:         PRINT USR "a"


2.8 Relational, Logical & Arithmetic Operators
----------------------------------------------
  +   Addition (or string concatenation)
  -   Subtraction (or unary negation)
  *   Multiplication
  /   Division
  ^   Exponentiation (e.g. 2 ^ 8 = 256)
  =   Equality comparison
  <   Less than
  >   Greater than
  <=  Less than or equal
  >=  Greater than or equal
  <>  Not equal
  AND Logical AND (returns right operand if both non-zero, else 0)
  OR  Logical OR (returns left operand if non-zero, else right operand)
  NOT Logical NOT (returns 1 if operand is 0, else 0)

2.9 Print Formatting Controls & Channel Selectors
-------------------------------------------------
PRINT statements support the following positioning and formatting items:
  AT row, col   : Positions text printing cursor at row (0-23) and col (0-31).
                  Example: PRINT AT 10, 5; "GAME OVER"
  TAB col       : Advances text printing cursor to the specified column.
                  Example: PRINT "ITEM"; TAB 20; "PRICE"
  ; (Semicolon) : Keeps the cursor at the current position without advancing.
                  Example: PRINT "A"; "B"; "C"
  , (Comma)     : Moves cursor to the next 16-column print zone.
                  Example: PRINT "ZONE 1", "ZONE 2"
  ' (Apostrophe): Forces an immediate carriage return / newline.
                  Example: PRINT "LINE 1"' "LINE 2"

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 3. SECTION 2: SINCLAIR ZX INTERFACE 1 EXTENSIONS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Target Extension:    Sinclair ZX Interface 1 Extensions
Compilation Support: [INTERPRETER ONLY]
                     (Interface 1 hook codes and shadow ROM routines like OPEN #, CLOSE #,
                     FORMAT, MOVE, ERASE, and CAT require the physical IF1 shadow ROM or
                     BasinPy's runtime interpreter. For compiled standalone programs, file I/O
                     must target standard tape or memory arrays).

Sinclair ZX Interface 1 introduced Microdrive cartridge storage, RS-232 serial
communication, Sinclair ZX Network capabilities, and stream redirection.

3.1 Microdrive Architecture & Drive Addressing
----------------------------------------------
Microdrives are referenced via the device specifier "m", followed by a drive
number (1 through 8) and a filename up to 10 characters.
Syntax template: *"m";drive;"filename"

3.2 Stream Redirection & Channel Syntax
---------------------------------------
Sinclair BASIC directs I/O through numbered streams (0 through 15):
  Stream #0, #1 : Screen lower two lines (input / status area)
  Stream #2     : Screen main upper area (lines 0-21)
  Stream #3     : ZX Printer output

Command/Keyword: OPEN #
Syntax:          OPEN #stream, "channel_specifier"
Description:     Associates a stream number with a peripheral channel or Microdrive file.
Example:         10 OPEN #4, "b"

Command/Keyword: CLOSE #
Syntax:          CLOSE #stream
Description:     Closes an active stream and releases its associated channel resources.
Example:         90 CLOSE #4

Command/Keyword: CLS #
Syntax:          CLS #stream
Description:     Clears the viewport or window associated with the specified stream.
Example:         20 CLS #2

Function:        INKEY$ #
Syntax:          INKEY$ #stream
Returns:         String
Description:     Reads a character from the specified peripheral stream (e.g. RS-232 serial buffer).
Example:         10 LET a$ = INKEY$ #4


3.3 Channel Drivers (RS-232, Screen, Keyboard, Printer, ZX Net)
----------------------------------------------------------------
Interface 1 provides six standard channel drivers:
  "b"  : RS-232 Serial binary channel (raw byte communication).
  "t"  : RS-232 Serial text channel (with automatic CR/LF translation).
  "c"  : Screen console channel (direct visual output).
  "k"  : Keyboard input channel.
  "s"  : Sinclair ZX Printer channel.
  "n"  : Sinclair ZX Net network channel (Syntax: "n";station_number).

3.4 Microdrive Management Commands
----------------------------------
Command/Keyword: FORMAT
Syntax:          FORMAT "m";drive;"cartridge_name"
Description:     Formats a Microdrive cartridge, creating a new directory and initializing sectors.
Example:         FORMAT "m";1;"GAMES"

Command/Keyword: CAT
Syntax:          CAT #stream; "m";drive
Description:     Outputs the directory of the specified Microdrive cartridge showing filename, size, and sectors.
Example:         CAT #2; "m";1

Command/Keyword: SAVE *
Syntax:          SAVE *"m";drive;"filename" [LINE start_line | CODE start, len | DATA arr()]
Description:     Saves a BASIC program, machine code binary block, screen dump, or data array to Microdrive.
Example:         SAVE *"m";1;"SPACE" LINE 10

Command/Keyword: LOAD *
Syntax:          LOAD *"m";drive;"filename" [CODE [start] | DATA arr()]
Description:     Loads a program, machine code block, or array from the specified Microdrive cartridge.
Example:         LOAD *"m";1;"SPACE"

Command/Keyword: VERIFY *
Syntax:          VERIFY *"m";drive;"filename" [CODE | DATA arr()]
Description:     Verifies that a file saved on Microdrive matches the current memory contents.
Example:         VERIFY *"m";1;"SPACE"

Command/Keyword: MERGE *
Syntax:          MERGE *"m";drive;"filename"
Description:     Merges program lines from a Microdrive file into the currently resident BASIC program.
Example:         MERGE *"m";1;"SUBROUT"

Command/Keyword: ERASE
Syntax:          ERASE "m";drive;"filename"
Description:     Deletes the specified file from a Microdrive cartridge.
Example:         ERASE "m";1;"OLDDATA"

Command/Keyword: MOVE
Syntax:          MOVE "m";drive1;"src" TO "m";drive2;"dest"
Description:     Copies or transfers files between Microdrive cartridges or streams.
Example:         MOVE "m";1;"PIC" TO "m";2;"PIC"


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 4. SECTION 3: BETA BASIC V3.0+ & V4.0 ADVANCED EXTENSIONS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Target Extension:    Beta BASIC V3.0+ & V4.0 Advanced Extensions
Compilation Support: [INTERPRETER ONLY] / [CUSTOM COMPILER TRANSPILATION]
                     (Beta BASIC structured constructs such as DEF PROC, PROC, LOCAL, DO..LOOP,
                     WHILE, UNTIL, EXIT IF, JOIN, SPLIT, ALTER, SORT, SHUFFLE, ROLL, SCROLL,
                     KEYIN, and DEF KEY run natively in the BasinPy interpreter. When compiled
                     with BasinPy's multi-pass compiler, select structured constructs and bitwise
                     operators map to Boriel ZXBC routines, while complex dynamic features
                     require the resident interpreter runtime).

Beta BASIC transforms Sinclair BASIC into a powerful structured programming
language, introducing procedures with local scoping, rich control structures,
advanced memory words, generalized string slicing, patterned graphics, windowing,
dynamic code execution (KEYIN), keyboard macros (DEF KEY), array manipulation (SORT,
SHUFFLE, INARRAY), and 128K RAM disc array storage (DIM !).

4.1 Structured Procedures & Scope Control
-----------------------------------------
Command/Keyword: DEF PROC
Syntax:          DEF PROC name[(param1, param2, ...)]
Description:     Begins the definition of a named procedure with optional formal parameters.
Example:         1000 DEF PROC draw_box(x, y, w, h)

Command/Keyword: END PROC
Syntax:          END PROC
Description:     Marks the end of a procedure block, restores local variables, and returns execution to the caller.
Example:         1050 END PROC

Command/Keyword: PROC / CALL
Syntax:          PROC name[(arg1, arg2, ...)] or CALL name[(...)]
Description:     Invokes a named procedure, binding arguments to formal parameters.
Example:         50 PROC draw_box(10, 10, 100, 50)

Command/Keyword: LOCAL
Syntax:          LOCAL var1, var2, ...
Description:     Declares local variables whose scope is restricted to the current procedure execution.
Example:         1010 LOCAL i, temp$, flag

Command/Keyword: DEFAULT
Syntax:          DEFAULT var = default_value
Description:     Assigns a default fallback value to an optional parameter if not supplied by the caller.
Example:         1020 DEFAULT col = 7

Command/Keyword: REF
Syntax:          REF param
Description:     Passes a variable by reference, allowing the procedure to modify the caller's variable in-place.
Example:         1000 DEF PROC swap_vals(REF a, REF b): LOCAL t: LET t=a: LET a=b: LET b=t: END PROC

Command/Keyword: POP
Syntax:          POP
Description:     Discards the topmost return address from the subroutine/procedure stack without executing RETURN.
Example:         1050 IF error_flag THEN POP: GOTO 999


4.2 Advanced Looping & Branching Structures
-------------------------------------------
Command/Keyword: WHILE..WEND
Syntax:          WHILE condition ... WEND
Description:     Pre-tested loop; repeats the enclosed block as long as condition evaluates to true (non-zero).
Example:         10 WHILE score < 1000: PROC play_turn: WEND

Command/Keyword: REPEAT..UNTIL
Syntax:          REPEAT ... UNTIL condition
Description:     Post-tested loop; repeats the enclosed block until condition evaluates to true (non-zero).
Example:         20 REPEAT: LET k$ = INKEY$: UNTIL k$ <> ""

Command/Keyword: DO..LOOP..EXIT
Syntax:          DO ... [EXIT | EXIT IF condition] ... LOOP
Description:     Infinite or general loop structure supporting mid-loop early exits.
Example:         30 DO: PROC update_player: EXIT IF lives = 0: PROC redraw: LOOP

Command/Keyword: CASE..WHEN..OTHERWISE..ENDCASE
Syntax:          CASE expr ... WHEN val1, val2 ... [OTHERWISE ...] ENDCASE
Description:     Multi-branch selective decision structure matching expr against specified cases.
Example:         40 CASE action: WHEN 1: PROC move_up: WHEN 2: PROC move_down: OTHERWISE: BEEP 0.1, 0: ENDCASE


4.3 Enhanced Conditionals & Error Trapping
------------------------------------------
Command/Keyword: IF..THEN..ELSE..ENDIF
Syntax:          IF condition THEN statement [ELSE statement] [ENDIF]
Description:     Block structured conditional execution with full ELSE and optional ENDIF delimiters.
Example:         50 IF health > 50 THEN PRINT "STRONG" ELSE PRINT "WEAK": ENDIF

Command/Keyword: ON ERROR
Syntax:          ON ERROR GOTO line_number or ON ERROR statement
Description:     Traps runtime errors and redirects execution instead of halting the interpreter.
Example:         10 ON ERROR GOTO 9000


4.4 Extended Memory, Bitwise & Numeric Operators
-----------------------------------------------
Command/Keyword: DPOKE
Syntax:          DPOKE address, 16_bit_value
Description:     Writes a 16-bit double byte (little-endian: low byte, high byte) to memory.
Example:         DPOKE 23675, 60000

Function:        DPEEK
Syntax:          DPEEK(address)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Reads a 16-bit double byte (little-endian: low + high * 256) from memory.
Example:         PRINT DPEEK(23675)

Function:        MEM$ / MEMORY$
Syntax:          MEM$(address, length)
Returns:         String
Description:     Reads a block of memory directly into a string.
Example:         LET ram$ = MEM$(16384, 6912)

Function:        DEC
Syntax:          DEC(string$)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Converts a hexadecimal string (or binary string) to a decimal integer.
Example:         PRINT DEC("FFFF")

Function:        HEX$
Syntax:          HEX$(number [, digits])
Returns:         String
Description:     Converts a decimal number to a hexadecimal string representation.
Example:         PRINT HEX$(65535, 4)

Function:        BIN$
Syntax:          BIN$(number [, digits])
Returns:         String
Description:     Converts a decimal number to a binary string representation.
Example:         PRINT BIN$(255, 8)

Function:        MOD
Syntax:          a MOD b
Returns:         Number
Description:     Calculates the remainder of integer division of a by b.
Example:         PRINT 17 MOD 5

Function:        XOR
Syntax:          a XOR b
Returns:         Number
Description:     Performs bitwise and logical exclusive OR between operands a and b.
Example:         PRINT 12 XOR 10

Function:        BAND
Syntax:          BAND(x, y)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Performs a 16-bit bitwise AND between integer operands x and y.
Example:         PRINT BAND(12, 10)

Function:        BOR
Syntax:          BOR(x, y)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Performs a 16-bit bitwise OR between integer operands x and y.
Example:         PRINT BOR(12, 10)

Function:        BXOR
Syntax:          BXOR(x, y)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Performs a 16-bit bitwise exclusive OR between integer operands x and y.
Example:         PRINT BXOR(12, 10)

Function:        BNOT
Syntax:          BNOT(x)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Performs a 16-bit bitwise NOT (inversion) on integer operand x.
Example:         PRINT BNOT(0)


4.5 Advanced String Slicing & Extended String Functions
-------------------------------------------------------
Beta BASIC generalizes Sinclair slicing syntax. Slicing can be performed on:
  - String variables: A$(start TO end)
  - Dynamic string expressions: (a$ + b$)(1 TO 5)
  - String literals: "SINCLAIR SPECTRUM"(1 TO 8)
  - In-place slice assignment: LET a$(3 TO 6) = "TEST"

Function:        UPPER$
Syntax:          UPPER$(string$)
Returns:         String
Description:     Converts all lowercase alphabetical characters in string$ to uppercase.
Example:         PRINT UPPER$("sinclair 48k")

Function:        LOWER$
Syntax:          LOWER$(string$)
Returns:         String
Description:     Converts all uppercase alphabetical characters in string$ to lowercase.
Example:         PRINT LOWER$("ZX SPECTRUM")

Function:        STRING$
Syntax:          STRING$(count, char_or_string$)
Returns:         String
Description:     Generates a string consisting of count repetitions of the specified character.
Example:         PRINT STRING$(32, "=")

Function:        INSTR / INSTRING
Syntax:          INSTR(source$, search$ [, start_pos])
Returns:         Number
Description:     Finds the first character position of search$ within source$; returns 0 if not found.
Example:         PRINT INSTR("ABCDEF", "CD")

Function:        ITEM
Syntax:          ITEM(index, string$, delimiter$)
Returns:         String
Description:     Extracts the nth token from a delimiter-separated string.
Example:         PRINT ITEM(2, "RED,GREEN,BLUE", ",")

Function:        USING$
Syntax:          USING$(format_mask$, number)
Returns:         String
Description:     Formats a numeric value according to a template mask (e.g. "###.##").
Example:         PRINT USING$("###.##", 123.456)

Function:        LENGTH
Syntax:          LENGTH(string_var$)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the physical memory length or character count of string_var$.
Example:         PRINT LENGTH(msg$)


4.6 Multi-Dimensional Arrays & 128K RAM Disk Storage
---------------------------------------------------
Beta BASIC v4.0 introduces the 128K RAM Disc array subsystem, allowing numeric and
string arrays to be allocated directly in the Sinclair 128 expanded memory workspace.
This provides over 73KB of dedicated array storage without reducing the standard 48K
BASIC workspace.

Command/Keyword: DIM !
Syntax:          DIM !name(dim1 [, dim2, ...]) or DIM !name$(dim1 [, dim2, ...])
Description:     Dimensions a numeric or string array into the 128K RAM disc array pool (ram_disk_arrays).
                 Supports multi-dimensional numeric matrices and 2D string arrays.
Example:         DIM !scores(1000): DIM !names$(500, 32)

Command/Keyword: ! Array Element Access
Syntax:          LET !arr(i [, j]) = val  or  PRINT !arr(i [, j])
                 LET !arr$(i) = string$  or  PRINT !arr$(i)
Description:     Reads and writes elements to arrays stored in the 128K RAM disc. Operates transparently
                 in expressions and assignments, and is fully compatible with SORT, SHUFFLE, and INARRAY.
Example:         LET !scores(10) = 450: PRINT !scores(10)

Function:        INARRAY
Syntax:          INARRAY(array_name, search_value [, start_index])
Returns:         Number
Description:     Searches 1D or 2D numeric and string arrays (in either standard 48K memory or 128K RAM disc !array)
                 for search_value, starting from start_index (default 1). For 2D string arrays, checks each row
                 for exact match or substring. Returns 1-based index/row where found, or 0 if absent.
Example:         IF INARRAY(!scores, 100) > 0 THEN PRINT "FOUND!"


4.7 Array Sorting & Permutation: SORT and SHUFFLE
-------------------------------------------------
Beta BASIC 4.0 provides native high-speed array sorting and randomization algorithms,
supporting both 1D and 2D arrays in standard 48K memory and 128K RAM disc storage.

Command/Keyword: SORT
Syntax:          SORT [INVERSE] arr [$] [(slice)] [USING idx]
Description:     Sorts numeric or string arrays in-place.
                 - INVERSE: Sorts in descending order (default is ascending).
                 - slice: On 2D string arrays, restricts sorting key comparison to character slice (e.g. (1 TO 4)).
                 - USING idx: Performs an indirect sort using an index permutation array idx, reordering multiple
                              parallel arrays simultaneously.
Example:         SORT scores: SORT INVERSE names$: SORT names$(1 TO 5): SORT table USING index

Command/Keyword: SHUFFLE
Syntax:          SHUFFLE arr [$]
Description:     Performs an in-place Fisher-Yates random shuffle on elements of a 1D array or rows of a 2D array
                 in both 48K memory and 128K RAM disc storage.
Example:         SHUFFLE deck$: SHUFFLE !cards


4.8 Windows, Graphic Primitives, Patterns & Directional Scrolling
-----------------------------------------------------------------
Command/Keyword: WINDOW
Syntax:          WINDOW left, top, width, height
Description:     Defines a rectangular text window on the screen; subsequent CLS, PRINT, and SCROLL operate inside it.
Example:         WINDOW 4, 4, 24, 16

Command/Keyword: CSIZE
Syntax:          CSIZE font_width, font_height
Description:     Sets character size in pixels:
                 - CSIZE 5, 8: Activates authentic 51-column text mode (256/5 = 51 cols) using custom 5x8 font.
                 - CSIZE 4, 8: Activates compact 64-column text mode (256/4 = 64 cols).
                 - CSIZE 8, 8: Resets to standard Sinclair 32-column text mode.
                 - CSIZE (no args): Defaults to standard 8x8 character cell.
Example:         CSIZE 5, 8: PRINT "51 COLUMNS OF TEXT ACROSS THE CRT SCREEN!"

Command/Keyword: BOLD
Syntax:          BOLD [0 | 1]
Description:     Enables or disables on-screen Bold character rendering. When active, character pixel glyphs
                 are horizontally dilated by 1 pixel (bits | (bits >> 1)), thickening vertical stems.
                 Can also be specified inline within PRINT statements (PRINT BOLD 1; "text")
                 or via embedded control code CHR$ 1.
Example:         BOLD 1: PRINT "THICK STEM BOLD TEXT": BOLD 0

Command/Keyword: ITALIC
Syntax:          ITALIC [0 | 1]
Description:     Enables or disables on-screen Italic cursive rendering. When active, character pixel glyphs
                 are slanted forward by 1 pixel on their top rows, creating an authentic 12° cursive tilt.
                 Can also be specified inline within PRINT statements (PRINT ITALIC 1; "text")
                 or via embedded control code CHR$ 2.
Example:         ITALIC 1: PRINT "ELEGANT ITALIC TEXT": ITALIC 0

Command/Keyword: UNDERLINE
Syntax:          UNDERLINE [0 | 1]
Description:     Enables or disables on-screen Underline rule rendering. When active, character cells display
                 a solid baseline horizontal stroke across bottom row 7, connecting continuously.
                 Can also be specified inline within PRINT statements (PRINT UNDERLINE 1; "text")
                 or via embedded control code CHR$ 3.
Example:         UNDERLINE 1: PRINT "CONTINUOUS BASELINE RULE": UNDERLINE 0

Command/Keyword: PATTERN
Syntax:          PATTERN [n | pattern_mask$]
Description:     Configures the global 8x8 stipple fill pattern used by FILL and BOX FILL.
                 - n: Integer byte 0-255 repeated across 8x8 character cell (e.g. 170 / 0xAA = 50% checkerboard).
                 - pattern_mask$: 8-byte or 16-byte custom pattern string.
                 - PATTERN (no argument): Resets to solid fill (pattern 255).
Example:         PATTERN 170

Command/Keyword: BOX
Syntax:          BOX x, y, width, height [FILL [pattern]]
Description:     Draws a rectangle at (x, y) with specified width and height. If FILL is appended, fills the interior
                 using the active PATTERN or an inline stipple pattern (0-255 or pattern mask string).
Example:         BOX 20, 20, 100, 50 FILL: BOX 10, 10, 80, 40 FILL 170

Command/Keyword: FILL
Syntax:          FILL x, y [, pattern]
Description:     Flood-fills an enclosed graphic region starting from coordinate (x, y) using the active PATTERN
                 or an optional inline stipple pattern (0-255 or pattern mask string).
Example:         FILL 128, 96, 170

Command/Keyword: UNPLOT
Syntax:          UNPLOT x, y
Description:     Erases the single pixel at screen coordinate (x, y).
Example:         UNPLOT 128, 96

Command/Keyword: ALTER
Syntax:          ALTER x, y, attribute_byte
Description:     Directly updates the attribute byte of the character cell covering pixel coordinate (x, y).
Example:         ALTER 100, 100, 71

Command/Keyword: INVERT
Syntax:          INVERT x, y, width, height  or  INVERT #stream
Description:     Inverts display output:
                 - INVERT x, y, w, h: Toggles pixel states and swaps INK/PAPER attribute colours within bounding rectangle.
                 - INVERT #stream (or INVERT #win): Inverts the entire region of the specified window/stream.
Example:         INVERT 0, 0, 256, 192: INVERT #2

Command/Keyword: ROLL
Syntax:          ROLL [UP | DOWN | LEFT | RIGHT] [lines_or_pixels]
Description:     Rolls the active window or screen with wrap-around in the specified direction.
                 Defaults to vertical roll if direction is omitted.
Example:         ROLL DOWN 1: ROLL LEFT 2

Command/Keyword: SCROLL
Syntax:          SCROLL [UP | DOWN | LEFT | RIGHT] [lines_or_pixels]
Description:     Scrolls the active window or screen in the specified direction, shifting contents and clearing
                 the vacated edge. Defaults to upward scroll if direction is omitted.
Example:         SCROLL UP 1: SCROLL RIGHT 1


4.9 Dynamic Code & Keyboard Macros: KEYIN and DEF KEY
-----------------------------------------------------
Beta BASIC 4.0 provides advanced meta-programming and macro facilities:

Command/Keyword: KEYIN
Syntax:          KEYIN string_expr
Description:     Dynamically injects a string of BASIC statements or numbered lines into the interpreter input buffer
                 at runtime. Can insert, replace, or delete program lines, or execute immediate direct commands,
                 enabling self-modifying code and dynamic script generation.
Example:         KEYIN "10 PRINT ""DYNAMIC CODE GENERATED""": KEYIN "BORDER 1: CLS"

Command/Keyword: DEF KEY
Syntax:          DEF KEY "key": "command"  or  DEF KEY key_num, "command"
Description:     Defines a programmable single-key macro. When the assigned key is pressed at the command prompt,
                 its macro string is automatically expanded into the edit line. If the macro ends with a newline
                 (CHR$ 13 or apostrophe '), the command is immediately executed.
Example:         DEF KEY "R": "RUN": DEF KEY 1, "BORDER 2: PAPER 7: CLS'"


4.10 Program Editing, Timing & System Utilities
-----------------------------------------------
Command/Keyword: SWAP
Syntax:          SWAP var1, var2
Description:     Exchanges the values of two variables of the same type without requiring a temporary variable.
Example:         SWAP x, y: SWAP name1$, name2$

Command/Keyword: LOCATE
Syntax:          LOCATE "search_string"
Description:     Searches through all program lines and lists every line containing the specified string.
Example:         LOCATE "GOSUB"

Command/Keyword: AUTO
Syntax:          AUTO [start_line [, step]]
Description:     Enables automatic line numbering during code entry.
Example:         AUTO 100, 10

Command/Keyword: DELETE
Syntax:          DELETE start_line [TO end_line]
Description:     Deletes a range of lines from the current BASIC program.
Example:         DELETE 100 TO 200

Command/Keyword: JOIN
Syntax:          JOIN line1, line2
Description:     Merges two adjacent program lines into a single line separated by colons.
Example:         JOIN 10, 20

Command/Keyword: SPLIT
Syntax:          SPLIT line_number, statement_index
Description:     Splits a multi-statement program line into two separate numbered lines.
Example:         SPLIT 50, 2

Command/Keyword: CLOCK
Syntax:          CLOCK [mode]
Description:     Enables, disables, or updates the real-time on-screen clock display.
Example:         CLOCK 1

Function:        TIME
Syntax:          TIME
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the number of seconds elapsed since system startup or timer reset.
Example:         PRINT TIME

Function:        TIME$
Syntax:          TIME$
Returns:         String
Description:     Returns the current system time as a formatted string ("HH:MM:SS").
Example:         PRINT TIME$

Function:        DATE$ / DATED$
Syntax:          DATE$
Returns:         String
Description:     Returns the current system date as a formatted string ("YYYY-MM-DD").
Example:         PRINT DATE$

Function:        MIN
Syntax:          MIN(a, b [, c, ...])
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the minimum value among the supplied numeric arguments.
Example:         PRINT MIN(10, 25, 4, 88)

Function:        MAX
Syntax:          MAX(a, b [, c, ...])
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the maximum value among the supplied numeric arguments.
Example:         PRINT MAX(10, 25, 4, 88)

Function:        RNDM
Syntax:          RNDM(min, max)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns a pseudo-random integer between min and max inclusive.
Example:         PRINT RNDM(1, 100)

Command/Keyword: RENUMBER / RENUM
Syntax:          RENUMBER [start [, step]]
Description:     Renumbers the entire program and automatically updates all GOTO, GOSUB, and RESTORE targets.
Example:         RENUMBER 10, 10

Command/Keyword: TRON / TROFF
Syntax:          TRON / TROFF
Description:     Enables (TRON) or disables (TROFF) statement execution tracing in the IDE.
Example:         TRON


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 5. SECTION 4: EXTENDED HARDWARE UNITS & PERIPHERALS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Target Extension:    Extended Hardware Units & Peripherals
Compilation Support: [48K BASIC COMPATIBLE] / [BORIEL / Z80 ASM COMPATIBLE] / [INTERPRETER ONLY]
                     (Low-level port I/O via IN and OUT, such as Fuller Sound Ports 63/95,
                     Kempston Joystick Port 31, Fuller Joystick Port 127, SpecDrum Port 223,
                     and Currah uSpeech Port 56 are fully compatible with 48K BASIC and Boriel's
                     ZXBC compiler. Specialized high-level commands like MOUSEY and built-in UDG
                     preset banks are supported directly in the runtime interpreter).

BasinPy Extended provides comprehensive hardware emulation for classic ZX Spectrum
add-ons, sound units, speech synthesizers, joysticks, and alternative language engines.

5.1 Fuller Master Unit: AY-3-8912 3-Channel Sound Chip
------------------------------------------------------
The Fuller Box / Fuller Master Unit equips the ZX Spectrum with the General
Instrument AY-3-8912 Programmable Sound Generator (PSG), featuring 3 independent
analog tone channels (A, B, C), a white noise generator, and an envelope generator.

Fuller I/O Port Mapping:
  OUT 63 : Select AY-3-8912 Register Address (0 through 15)
  OUT 95 : Write 8-bit Data to selected register
  IN 95  : Read 8-bit Data from selected register
  IN 63  : Read 8-bit Data from selected register

AY-3-8912 Complete Register Map:
  Reg  Name                   Bits    Function Description
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
  R0   Channel A Fine Tune    8-bit   Low 8 bits of 12-bit pitch period (0-255)
  R1   Channel A Coarse Tune  4-bit   High 4 bits of 12-bit pitch period (0-15)
  R2   Channel B Fine Tune    8-bit   Low 8 bits of 12-bit pitch period (0-255)
  R3   Channel B Coarse Tune  4-bit   High 4 bits of 12-bit pitch period (0-15)
  R4   Channel C Fine Tune    8-bit   Low 8 bits of 12-bit pitch period (0-255)
  R5   Channel C Coarse Tune  4-bit   High 4 bits of 12-bit pitch period (0-15)
  R6   Noise Pitch Period     5-bit   Noise period 0-31 (lower = higher pitch noise)
  R7   Mixer Control          8-bit   Active-low enables (0 = enable, 1 = disable):
                                      Bit 0: Chan A Tone, Bit 1: Chan B Tone, Bit 2: Chan C Tone
                                      Bit 3: Chan A Noise, Bit 4: Chan B Noise, Bit 5: Chan C Noise
                                      (Standard 3-channel tone only: OUT 63,7: OUT 95,56 / BIN 00111000)
  R8   Channel A Amplitude    5-bit   Bits 0-3: Fixed volume (0-15), Bit 4: Use Envelope
  R9   Channel B Amplitude    5-bit   Bits 0-3: Fixed volume (0-15), Bit 4: Use Envelope
  R10  Channel C Amplitude    5-bit   Bits 0-3: Fixed volume (0-15), Bit 4: Use Envelope
  R11  Envelope Period Fine   8-bit   Low 8 bits of envelope cycle period
  R12  Envelope Period Coarse 8-bit   High 8 bits of envelope cycle period
  R13  Envelope Shape / Cycle 4-bit   Cycle shapes 0-15 (Sawtooth, Triangle, Fade, Repeat)
  R14  I/O Port A             8-bit   Bidirectional 8-bit digital parallel port
  R15  I/O Port B             8-bit   AY-3-8910 dual-port only (unused on AY-3-8912)

Complete 5.1.1 Authentic Hardware Master Clock & Divider 32 Architecture
--------------------------------------------------------------
In authentic General Instrument AY-3-8910 / AY-3-8912 sound hardware, the master clock frequency
is divided by 16 into a 12-bit down-counter, whose output toggles a flip-flop (dividing by 2).
This yields an exact hardware tone frequency divider of 32:

                f_tone = f_clock / (32 * N)

where N is the 12-bit channel pitch period (registers R0/R1, R2/R3, R4/R5).
Dividing by 16 instead of 32 results in all pitches sounding an entire octave too high.
BasinPy implements this authentic 32-divider across all AY-3-8912 emulation modes:
  - Sinclair 128K (+2, +3): Master clock f_clock = 1.7734 MHz.
    Base frequency = 1,773,400 / 32 = 55,420.23 Hz.
    Concert Pitch A4 (440.0 Hz) corresponds to period N = 126 (f = 439.84 Hz).
  - Fuller Box: Master clock f_clock = 1.7500 MHz (derived from 3.5 MHz CPU clock / 2).
    Base frequency = 1,750,000 / 32 = 54,687.50 Hz.
    Concert Pitch A4 (440.0 Hz) corresponds to period N = 124 (f = 441.03 Hz).

5.1.2 Real-Time Audio Streaming Loop & Envelope Pacing
------------------------------------------------------
The internal PSG audio thread streams 25 ms audio sample buffers to the SDL audio engine.
In order to prevent rapid register writes from triggering instant wakeups that burn through
dynamic amplitude envelopes and overflow the mixer queue, BasinPy incorporates paced 12 ms
sleep delays whenever the hardware mixer queue is full. This guarantees authentic PSG envelope
timings and completely eliminates audio dropouts or glitches.

5.1.3 Active-Low Mixer Register 7 Default State
-----------------------------------------------
On the AY-3-8912, Register 7 (Mixer Control) uses active-low logic: bit = 0 enables the tone
or noise, while bit = 1 disables/mutes that channel. To prevent unmuted tone bleed when
the sound chip initializes, Register 7 defaults to 0x3F (binary 00111111), cleanly muting
all 3 tone and noise channels until explicitly activated by BASIC statements.

3-Channel Sound Demo Example:
  10 REM === FULLER BOX AY-3-8912 3-CHANNEL SOUND DEMO ===
  20 BORDER 0: PAPER 0: INK 7: CLS
  30 REM Reset all AY registers
  40 FOR r = 0 TO 14: OUT 63, r: OUT 95, 0: NEXT r
  50 REM Register 7: Enable tone on channels A, B, and C (binary 00111000 = 56)
  60 OUT 63, 7: OUT 95, 56
  70 REM Registers 8, 9, 10: Set volumes to 12
  80 FOR v = 8 TO 10: OUT 63, v: OUT 95, 12: NEXT v
  90 REM Play Chord: Chan A = 250, Chan B = 190, Chan C = 160
  100 OUT 63, 0: OUT 95, 250: OUT 63, 1: OUT 95, 0
  110 OUT 63, 2: OUT 95, 190: OUT 63, 3: OUT 95, 0
  120 OUT 63, 4: OUT 95, 160: OUT 63, 5: OUT 95, 0
  130 PAUSE 150
  140 REM Silence channels
  150 FOR v = 8 TO 10: OUT 63, v: OUT 95, 0: NEXT v

5.2 Fuller Master Unit: Orator SP0256-AL2 Speech Chip & Allophone Table
------------------------------------------------------------------------
The Fuller Master Unit includes the General Instrument Orator SP0256-AL2 speech
synthesizer, containing 64 phonetic allophones in internal ROM.

Fuller Orator I/O Ports:
  OUT 127 : Write 6-bit Allophone Index (0 to 63) to start speech generation.
  IN 127  : Read Status. Bit 7 is set (> 127) when chip is BUSY speaking.
            Bit 7 is 0 when chip is IDLE and ready for the next allophone.

Complete 64-Allophone Reference Table:
  Code  Mnemonic  Sound / Sample Word               Duration
  ------------------------------------------------------------
  0     PA1       Pause (shortest silence)          10ms
  1     PA2       Pause (short silence)             30ms
  2     PA3       Pause (medium silence)            50ms
  3     PA4       Pause (standard word space)       100ms
  4     PA5       Pause (sentence boundary)         200ms
  5     OY        boy, toy                          420ms
  6     AY        sky, my                           260ms
  7     EH        end, met                          70ms
  8     KK3       comb, kick (before long vowels)   120ms
  9     PP        powder, pit                       210ms
  10    JH        judge, jeep                       140ms
  11    NN1       need, thin (short)                140ms
  12    IH        sit, fix                          70ms
  13    TT2       to, test (rapid)                  140ms
  14    RR1       rural, read                       170ms
  15    AX        about, galaxy                     70ms
  16    MM        milk, mine                        180ms
  17    TT1       tart, pat (final sound)           100ms
  18    DH1       they, then                        290ms
  19    IY        see, meet                         250ms
  20    EY        slay, made                        280ms
  21    DD1       do, dog (initial)                 70ms
  22    UW1       two, broom                        100ms
  23    AO        all, caught                       100ms
  24    AA        hot, father                       320ms
  25    YY2       yes, hear                         180ms
  26    AE        hat, fast                         280ms
  27    HH1       he, home                          130ms
  28    BB1       business, bit (initial)           80ms
  29    TH        thin, breath                      180ms
  30    UH        book, put                         210ms
  31    UW2       food, loop                        260ms
  32    AW        out, down                         370ms
  33    DD2       do, did (final)                   160ms
  34    GG3       wig, finger (final)               140ms
  35    VV        vest, wave                        190ms
  36    GG1       got, gun (initial)                120ms
  37    SH        ship, wish                        160ms
  38    ZH        azure, treasure                   190ms
  39    RR2       brain, brown                      120ms
  40    FF        food, off                         150ms
  41    KK2       sky, crack                        190ms
  42    KK1       can't, clown                      160ms
  43    ZZ        zoo, please                       210ms
  44    NG        ring, long                        220ms
  45    LL        lake, call                        110ms
  46    WW        wool, wet                         180ms
  47    XR        repair, rare                      360ms
  48    WH        whistle, white                    200ms
  49    YY1       yes, yard                         130ms
  50    CH        church, match                     190ms
  51    ER1       fir, letter                       160ms
  52    ER2       bird, turn                        300ms
  53    OW        beau, go                          240ms
  54    DH2       they, bathe                       240ms
  55    SS        vest, sit                         90ms
  56    NN2       no, name                          190ms
  57    HH2       hoe, huge                         180ms
  58    OR        door, form                        330ms
  59    AR        alarm, farm                       290ms
  60    YR        clear, hear                       350ms
  61    GG2       guest, give                       140ms
  62    EL        saddle, little                    190ms
  63    BB2       business, cab                     60ms

Complete Fuller Speech Code Example (Says 'HELLO'):
  10 REM === FULLER ORATOR SPEECH SYNTHESIS ===
  20 RESTORE 60
  30 READ a: IF a = -1 THEN STOP
  40 REM Wait until speech chip is ready (Bit 7 of Port 127)
  50 IF IN 127 > 127 THEN GOTO 50
  60 OUT 127, a: GOTO 30
  70 DATA 27, 7, 45, 53, 4, -1

5.3 Fuller Master Unit: Fuller Joystick (Port 127)
--------------------------------------------------
The Fuller Box includes an Atari standard 9-pin joystick port addressed at Port 127.
When Fuller Joystick mode is selected, reading Port 127 returns active-low switch
bits (0 = pressed, 1 = unpressed):
  Bit 0 (1)   : Up
  Bit 1 (2)   : Down
  Bit 2 (4)   : Left
  Bit 3 (8)   : Right
  Bit 5 (32)  : Secondary Fire Button
  Bit 7 (128) : Primary Fire Button
To activate Fuller joystick reading, enter: JOYSTICK FULR

5.4 Currah uSpeech / Microspeech Interface
-------------------------------------------
The Currah uSpeech peripheral interfaces to memory location 4096 and allows
phonetic speech to be output directly via bracketed phonemes in PRINT statements:
  PRINT "(h)(e)(l)(l)(o)(w)"
Keyvoice mode is also supported, speaking keys as they are typed on the keyboard.

5.5 Unified Joystick Subsystem
------------------------------
BasinPy Extended provides a unified joystick command supporting all major ZX Spectrum
joystick standards and multi-pad USB controllers.

Command/Keyword: JOYSTICK
Syntax:          JOYSTICK <mode>
Description:     Switches the active joystick emulation profile. Modes: KEMP, CURS, SINC1, SINC2, INF2, FULR.
Example:         JOYSTICK KEMP

Available Joystick Modes:
  KEMP  : Kempston Joystick interface (I/O Port 31). Active-high bits:
          Bit 0: Right (1), Bit 1: Left (2), Bit 2: Down (4), Bit 3: Up (8), Bit 4: Fire (16).
  CURS  : Cursor Joystick / AGF / Protek (Emulates Sinclair keys 5=Left, 6=Down, 7=Up, 8=Right, 0=Fire).
  SINC1 : Sinclair Interface 2 Port 1 (Emulates keys 6=Left, 7=Right, 8=Down, 9=Up, 0=Fire).
  SINC2 : Sinclair Interface 2 Port 2 (Emulates keys 1=Left, 2=Right, 3=Down, 4=Up, 5=Fire).
  INF2  : Dual Sinclair Interface 2 mode! Automatically maps USB Joypad 1 to Port 1
          and USB Joypad 2 to Port 2 simultaneously for two-player gaming.
  FULR  : Fuller Joystick interface (I/O Port 127).

Function:        JOYSTICK$
Syntax:          JOYSTICK$(pad_id)
Returns:         String
Description:     Queries the current directional state of joystick pad_id; returns strings such as "UP", "FIRE", etc.
Example:         PRINT JOYSTICK$(0)


5.6 User Defined Graphics (UDG): 16 Built-in Preset Banks
---------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy provides 16 pre-programmed User Defined Graphics banks covering classic
arcade, gaming, sci-fi, platform, and GUI motifs. UDGs are accessed using
graphics characters 'a' through 'u' (character codes CHR$(144) to CHR$(164)).

Command/Keyword: UDG
Syntax:          UDG <bank_number 1-16> or UDG ?
Description:     Instantly reprograms the 21 UDG character cells (a-u) with one of 16 themed design banks. 'UDG ?' displays the bank catalog.
Example:         UDG 1

Catalog of 16 Preset UDG Banks:
  Bank 1 : Space Invaders & Classic Space Games (Invaders, UFO, Shields, Missiles)
  Bank 2 : Pac-Man & Classic Maze Games (Pac-Man open/closed, Ghosts, Dots, Fruit)
  Bank 3 : Donkey Kong & Platform Games (Kong, Barrels, Ladders, Girders, Princess)
  Bank 4 : Super Mario Brothers (Mario running/jumping, Question Blocks, Mushrooms, Pipes)
  Bank 5 : Sonic the Hedgehog (Sonic running, Golden Rings, Spikes, Springboards)
  Bank 6 : Cards & Dice Games (Hearts, Diamonds, Clubs, Spades, Dice faces 1 to 6)
  Bank 7 : Common Emoji (Smiley, Grin, Wink, Heart, Thumbs Up, Star, Flame)
  Bank 8 : Sci-Fi & Futuristic (Robots, Laser Cannons, Forcefields, Teleporters)
  Bank 9 : Jet Set Willy & Manic Miner (Miner Willy, Keys, Guardian Hazards, Toilets)
  Bank 10: Chuckie Egg & Classic Platformers (Hen, Chicks, Grain seeds, Elevators)
  Bank 11: Lode Runner, Robotron & Defender (Runners, Humanoids, Landers, Mutants)
  Bank 12: Scramble & Arcade Shooters (Rockets, Fuel Tanks, Mystery Targets, Caverns)
  Bank 13: Snake & Grid Games (Snake Head, Body segments, Tail, Apples, Walls)
  Bank 14: Battle & War Games (Tanks, Turrets, Radars, Explosions, Jeeps)
  Bank 15: Psion Horace Games (Hungry Horace, Skiing Horace, Spiders, Skis)
  Bank 16: Word & Excel Style Office Icons (File, Save, Folder, Chart, Cut, Paste, Calc)

UDG Usage Example:
  10 UDG 1: REM Load Space Invaders Bank
  20 CLS: FOR i = 144 TO 150: PRINT CHR$(i); " ";: NEXT i

5.7 Abersoft Forth / fig-FORTH 1.1 Complete 181-Word Dictionary Reference
------------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy Extended embeds a full Abersoft fig-FORTH 1.1 engine. Forth code can be
executed directly inside BASIC programs or run in an interactive Forth console.

Invocation Modes:
  1. Inline Mode:      FORTH <code ...> END FORTH
  2. Interactive Mode: Type FORTH at the K > prompt. System displays 'OK' prompt.
                       Type BYE or END FORTH to return to BasinPy BASIC.

Complete 181-Word Dictionary Catalog (with Stack Effects):
  Word           Stack Effect        Description
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
  !              ( n addr -- )       Stores 16-bit number n at memory address addr
  *              ( n1 n2 -- n3 )     Multiplies n1 by n2, returns 16-bit product
  */             ( n1 n2 n3 -- n4 )  Multiplies n1*n2 with 32-bit intermediate, divides by n3
  */MOD          ( n1 n2 n3 -- rem quot ) Multiply-divide with remainder and quotient
  +              ( n1 n2 -- n3 )     Adds n1 and n2
  +!             ( n addr -- )       Adds n to the 16-bit value at memory address addr
  +LOOP          ( n -- )            Adds n to loop counter and checks against limit
  ,              ( n -- )            Reserves 2 bytes in dictionary and stores n
  -              ( n1 n2 -- n3 )     Subtracts n2 from n1 (n1 - n2)
  -ROT           ( n1 n2 n3 -- n3 n1 n2 ) Rotates the top three stack elements backwards
  .              ( n -- )            Prints top of stack as a signed integer followed by space
  .R             ( n width -- )      Prints integer right-justified in specified width
  /              ( n1 n2 -- n3 )     Divides n1 by n2 (integer division)
  /MOD           ( n1 n2 -- rem quot ) Divides n1 by n2, leaving remainder and quotient
  0<             ( n -- flag )       Returns true (1) if n is negative, else false (0)
  0=             ( n -- flag )       Returns true (1) if n is zero, else false (0)
  0>             ( n -- flag )       Returns true (1) if n is positive, else false (0)
  1+             ( n -- n+1 )        Increments top of stack by 1
  1-             ( n -- n-1 )        Decrements top of stack by 1
  2!             ( d addr -- )       Stores 32-bit double number at memory address
  2*             ( n -- n*2 )        Multiplies n by 2 (arithmetic shift left)
  2+             ( n -- n+2 )        Adds 2 to top of stack
  2-             ( n -- n-2 )        Subtracts 2 from top of stack
  2/             ( n -- n/2 )        Divides n by 2 (arithmetic shift right)
  2>R            ( d -- ) (R: -- d ) Moves 32-bit double number to return stack
  2@             ( addr -- d )       Fetches 32-bit double number from memory address
  2DROP          ( d -- )            Drops 32-bit double number or two items from stack
  2DUP           ( d -- d d )        Duplicates top two items on data stack
  2OVER          ( d1 d2 -- d1 d2 d1 ) Copies second pair of items to top of stack
  2R>            ( -- d ) (R: d -- ) Moves 32-bit double number from return stack
  2R@            ( -- d ) (R: d -- d ) Copies 32-bit double number from return stack
  2SWAP          ( d1 d2 -- d2 d1 )  Swaps top two pairs of items on stack
  :              ( -- )              Begins compilation of a new Forth colon definition
  ;              ( -- )              Ends compilation of the current colon definition
  <              ( n1 n2 -- flag )   Returns true if n1 is strictly less than n2
  <>             ( n1 n2 -- flag )   Returns true if n1 is not equal to n2
  =              ( n1 n2 -- flag )   Returns true if n1 equals n2
  >              ( n1 n2 -- flag )   Returns true if n1 is strictly greater than n2
  >R             ( n -- ) (R: -- n ) Moves top item from data stack to return stack
  ?              ( addr -- )         Fetches and prints the 16-bit value at addr
  ?DUP           ( n -- n n | 0 )    Duplicates n only if it is non-zero
  ?KEY           ( -- flag )         Checks if a key has been pressed without waiting
  @              ( addr -- n )       Fetches 16-bit value from memory address addr
  ABS            ( n -- u )          Returns absolute value of n
  AGAIN          ( -- )              Unconditional loop back to BEGIN
  ALLOT          ( n -- )            Allocates n bytes in dictionary memory
  AND            ( n1 n2 -- n3 )     Bitwise logical AND of n1 and n2
  ARC            ( x y rad a1 a2 -- ) Draws circular arc on Spectrum CRT
  AT             ( row col -- )      Positions text cursor at specified row and column
  ATTR           ( row col -- attr ) Returns attribute byte at row and col
  BASE           ( -- addr )         User variable holding current numeric I/O base
  BEEP           ( dur pitch -- )    Plays Spectrum tone using duration and pitch
  BEGIN          ( -- )              Marks start of indefinite loop structure
  BINARY         ( -- )              Sets numeric radix base to 2
  BLANKS         ( addr n -- )       Fills n bytes of memory at addr with ASCII spaces
  BLEEP          ( pitch -- )        Plays short arcade bleep sound
  BLOCK          ( u -- addr )       Loads 1024-byte screen/block u into memory buffer
  BORDER         ( col -- )          Sets Sinclair screen border colour (0-7)

  C!             ( byte addr -- )    Stores 8-bit byte at memory address addr
