5.8 Mouse Subsystem: Kempston & AMX Mouse Emulation, MOUSEY Command
-------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy provides full hardware emulation for both Kempston Mouse and Advanced
Memory Systems (AMX) Mouse interfaces. The mouse state is continuously
synchronized with the physical host mouse and mapped to real-time I/O ports and
memory addresses.

Mouse Mode Selection & Switch Command:
Command/Keyword: MOUSEY
Syntax:          MOUSEY [/KEMP | /AMX]
Description:     Switches or toggles mouse emulation mode between Kempston Mouse and AMX Mouse.
                 - MOUSEY /KEMP (or MOUSE /KEMP): Selects Kempston Mouse protocol.
                 - MOUSEY /AMX (or MOUSE /AMX): Selects AMX Mouse protocol.
                 - MOUSEY (or MOUSE without switch): Toggles between KEMP and AMX modes.
                 - MOUSEY ? (or /HELP): Displays current mouse mode and switch syntax.
Example:         MOUSEY /KEMP


Kempston Mouse Protocol & Hardware I/O Ports:
  Port #FBDF (64479) : Read horizontal X coordinate (0 to 255)
  Port #FFDF (65503) : Read vertical Y coordinate (0 to 255; also supports slave port #3FDF / 16351)
  Port #FADF (64223) : Read button status (Active-low, default 0xFF / 255 unpressed):
                       Bit 0 (1) : Right button  (0 = pressed)
                       Bit 1 (2) : Left button   (0 = pressed)
                       Bit 2 (4) : Middle button (0 = pressed)
                       Values: 255 (none), 253 (Left), 254 (Right), 252 (Left+Right)
  Port 223   (0xDF)  : In KEMP mode, reading Port 223 returns Kempston button status.

AMX Mouse Protocol & Hardware I/O Ports:
  Port 223 (0xDF) : Read buttons (Active-low):
                    Bit 7 (128) : Left button   (0 = pressed)
                    Bit 6 (64)  : Middle button (0 = pressed)
                    Bit 5 (32)  : Right button  (0 = pressed)
  Port 31  (0x1F) : X-axis movement delta pulses (active in AMX mode)
  Port 63  (0x3F) : Y-axis movement delta pulses (active in AMX mode)

AMX Direct Memory PEEK Addresses:
  PEEK 51915 : Current mouse X coordinate on Spectrum screen (0 to 255)
  PEEK 51916 : Current mouse Y coordinate on Spectrum screen (0 to 191)
  PEEK 51917 : Left button status (1 = pressed, 0 = released)
  PEEK 51918 : Right button status (1 = pressed, 0 = released)
  PEEK 51919 : Middle button status (1 = pressed, 0 = released)
  PEEK 51920 : Combined button mask: bit 0 Left, bit 1 Right, bit 2 Middle

Common Mouse Functions (Available in Both AMX and Kempston Modes):
Function:        MOUSEX
Syntax:          MOUSEX
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the current horizontal mouse coordinate (0-255).
Example:         LET mx = MOUSEX

Function:        MOUSEY
Syntax:          MOUSEY
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the current vertical mouse coordinate (0-191).
Example:         LET my = MOUSEY

Function:        MOUSEB
Syntax:          MOUSEB
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the current button status bitmask (1=Left, 2=Right, 4=Middle).
Example:         IF MOUSEB AND 1 THEN PRINT "CLICK!"


5.9 Cheetah SpecDrum Subsystem: 8-Bit DAC Hardware & Sample Kits
----------------------------------------------------------------
5.9.1 SpecDrum Audio Isolation from AY Sound Engine
---------------------------------------------------
Cheetah SpecDrum 8-bit digital-to-analog converter (DAC) output (port 223 / 0xDF) is
strictly routed through the dedicated SpecDrum digital audio subsystem and runs completely
independent of the AY-3-8912 sound generator. When launching the drum machine or sequencing
drum tracks, the AY-3-8912 chip is explicitly reset and silenced, ensuring zero audio bleed
between the SpecDrum rhythm engine and the AY PSG synthesizer.

BasinPy Extended provides comprehensive hardware emulation of the 1985 Cheetah
SpecDrum digital drum machine peripheral. The original hardware communicated via an
8-bit Digital-to-Analogue Converter (DAC) on I/O port 223 (0xDF).

BasinPy includes all 4 authentic original Cheetah sample packs (32 sounds in total):
  1. STANDARD  : The core kit (Kick, Snare, Mid Tom, Low Tom, Cowbell, Hi-Hat Closed, Hi-Hat Open, Claps)
  2. ELECTRO   : Electronic/Simmons set (E Kick, E Snare, E Peow pitch sweep, E Hi Tom, E Lo Tom, E Hi-Hat, E Cymbal, E Clap)
  3. LATIN     : Latin percussion (Kick D, Snare Hard, Hi Timbale, Lo Timbale, Hand Cowbell, Stick, Cabasa, Tamborine)
  4. AFRO      : Ethnic percussion (Trunk, Buash, Hi Conga, Lo Conga, Clave, Coconut, Guiro, Whistle)

Hardware DAC Output:
  Port 223 (0xDF) : Real-time 8-bit unsigned PCM DAC input. Streaming byte values to
                    OUT 223 outputs audio through the SpecDrum playback engine.

SpecDrum Commands & Switches:
Command/Keyword: DRUM / SPECDRUM
Syntax:          DRUM <id_or_name> [, volume]
Description:     Triggers a drum sample from the active kit with polyphonic playback.
                 - id_or_name: 1 to 8 or name (e.g. "KICK", "BASS", "SNARE", "COWBELL", "CLAP", "PEOW").
                 - volume: optional volume level from 0.0 to 1.0 (default 1.0).
Example:         DRUM "KICK"

Command/Keyword: SPECDRUM /KIT
Syntax:          SPECDRUM /KIT "kit_name"
Description:     Switches the active drum kit (e.g. "STANDARD", "ELECTRO", "LATIN", "AFRO", or custom installed kits).
Example:         SPECDRUM /KIT "ELECTRO"

Command/Keyword: SPECDRUM /INSTALL
Syntax:          SPECDRUM /INSTALL "path_or_zip" [, "kit_name"]
Description:     Installs a new sample pack from a folder or .ZIP archive containing .WAV files and optional drumkit.txt mapping.
Example:         SPECDRUM /INSTALL "C:/SAMPLES/ROCK_KIT.ZIP", "ROCK"

Command/Keyword: SPECDRUM /LIST
Syntax:          SPECDRUM /LIST
Description:     Displays all installed sample kits, marks the active kit, and enumerates all 8 drum slots.
Example:         SPECDRUM /LIST

Command/Keyword: SPECDRUM /RESET
Syntax:          SPECDRUM /RESET
Description:     Resets the active kit to the factory original Standard SpecDrum kit.
Example:         SPECDRUM /RESET


SpecDrum Functions in Expressions:
Function:        DRUM
Syntax:          DRUM(id_or_name [, volume])
Returns:         Number
Description:     Plays the specified drum sample and returns 1.0.
Example:         IF DRUM("SNARE") THEN PRINT "BEAT!"

Function:        SPECDRUM
Syntax:          SPECDRUM(id_or_name [, volume])
Returns:         Number
Description:     Plays the specified drum sample and returns 1.0.
Example:         LET x = SPECDRUM(1)


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 6. SECTION 5: SINCLAIR ZX SPECTRUM 128K SUBSYSTEM & EXTRA COMMANDS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Target Extension:    Sinclair ZX Spectrum 128K Subsystem & Extra Commands
Compilation Support: [48K BASIC COMPATIBLE via Port I/O] / [BORIEL ZXBC COMPATIBLE via Port 0x7FFD] / [INTERPRETER ONLY]
                     (Low-level 128K port paging via OUT 32765 and AY sound via OUT 65533/49149
                     are fully compatible with Boriel ZXBC and Z80 assembly. High-level commands
                     such as PLAY, SOUND /SIN, BANK, PAGE, and SPECTRUM 128 execute directly in
                     the BasinPy interpreter runtime or require 128K ROM environment).

BasinPy Extended provides comprehensive hardware and language emulation of the classic
1985 Sinclair ZX Spectrum 128 (The 'Toastrack' / +2 / +3), including the 3-channel
AY-3-8912 sound chip, multi-voice PLAY sequencer, 128K memory paging architecture,
the 128K Silicon Disc (RAM disk) system, and mode switching between 48K and 128K.

6.1 128K System Architecture & Memory Paging (Port 32765 / 0x7FFD)
------------------------------------------------------------------
The ZX Spectrum 128K memory system contains 128KB of RAM divided into 8 physical banks
of 16KB each (Banks 0 to 7), mapped into the Z80 64KB address space:
  - 0x0000 - 0x3FFF (00000-16383) : 16KB ROM (ROM 0 = 128K Editor, ROM 1 = 48K BASIC)
  - 0x4000 - 0x7FFF (16384-32767) : Bank 5 (Fixed: Normal Screen 16384-23295 & System Vars)
  - 0x8000 - 0xBFFF (32768-49151) : Bank 2 (Fixed: User BASIC Workspace)
  - 0xC000 - 0xFFFF (49152-65535) : Switchable 16KB RAM Bank (Bank 0 to 7, default Bank 0)

Hardware Memory Paging Register (Port 32765 / 0x7FFD):
  Controlled by OUT 32765, value:
    Bits 0..2 (0x07) : Paged RAM bank (0 to 7) mapped into 49152-65535 ($C000-$FFFF)
    Bit 3     (0x08) : Screen select: 0 = Normal screen in Bank 5, 1 = Shadow screen in Bank 7
    Bit 4     (0x10) : ROM select: 0 = 128K ROM 0, 1 = 48K BASIC ROM 1
    Bit 5     (0x20) : Paging lock: 1 = locks further paging until system reset
  BasinPy maintains live 16KB buffers for all 8 banks, preserving memory across bank swaps.
  Reading Port 32765 (IN 32765) returns the last written paging control byte.

6.2 AY-3-8912 Sound Emulation & SOUND Command (/FLR and /SIN Switches)
----------------------------------------------------------------------
The General Instrument AY-3-8912 Programmable Sound Generator (PSG) provides 3 independent
tone channels (A, B, C), a pseudo-random noise generator, volume/envelope controls,
and 16 internal sound registers.

BasinPy Extended supports both Sinclair 128K and Fuller Box AY sound architectures:
  - Sinclair 128K AY Ports (Standard):
      Port 65533 (0xFFFD) : Register Select (OUT) and Register Data Read (IN)
      Port 49149 (0xBFFD) : Register Data Write (OUT)
  - Fuller Box AY Ports (Fuller Master Unit):
      Port 63    (0x3F)   : Register Select (OUT) and Register Data Read (IN)
      Port 95    (0x5F)   : Register Data Write (OUT)

SOUND Command & Hardware Switch Syntax:
Command/Keyword: SOUND
Syntax:          SOUND [/SIN | /FLR | /MIDI] or SOUND reg, val
Description:     Controls AY sound chip emulation mode, MIDI output status, and direct register programming.
                 - SOUND /SIN: Switches AY emulation to Sinclair 128K ports (65533 / 49149).
                 - SOUND /FLR: Switches AY emulation to Fuller Box ports (63 / 95).
                 - SOUND /MIDI [ON|OFF|RESET]: Controls or queries Windows default MIDI output subsystem.
                 - SOUND (or SOUND ? / STATUS): Displays active AY emulation mode, MIDI device, and port maps.
                 - SOUND reg, val: Writes value (0-255) directly to AY register reg (0-15).
Example:         SOUND /SIN: SOUND /MIDI ON

Command/Keyword: SOUND Direct Register Write
Syntax:          SOUND reg, val
Description:     Directly programs AY-3-8912 sound registers. Example: sets Channel A tone period and volume.
Example:         SOUND 0, 252: SOUND 8, 15


6.3 Polyphonic Music Sequencer & MIDI Subsystem: PLAY Command & Sub-String Syntax
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The PLAY command provides an authentic Sinclair ZX Spectrum 128 polyphonic music
sequencer driving the internal 3-channel AY-3-8912 sound chip alongside the Windows
default MIDI output device (e.g. Microsoft MIDI Mapper, Microsoft GS Wavetable Synth,
or connected USB-MIDI synthesizers and virtual instruments).

Polyphonic Architecture & Voice Routing:
  - Supports up to 8 simultaneous polyphonic channel strings (s1$ through s8$).
  - Channels 1 to 3 (s1$, s2$, s3$): Routed to internal AY-3-8912 PSG (Channels A, B, C) AND MIDI.
  - Channels 4 to 8 (s4$ through s8$): Routed to Windows Default MIDI Output as expanded polyphonic voices.
  - Default MIDI channels: String 1 -> MIDI Channel 1, String 2 -> MIDI Channel 2, ..., String 8 -> MIDI Channel 8.

Command/Keyword: PLAY
Syntax:          PLAY s1$ [, s2$ ... [, s8$]]
Description:     Sequences and plays polyphonic music across up to 8 voices simultaneously.
                 Strings define note pitches, accidentals, octaves, tempo, durations, volume/velocity,
                 hardware envelopes, noise mixing, nested loops, MIDI channel selection, and MIDI codes.
Example:         PLAY "T140 O4 3c#5 d &5 e", "T140 O3 5c g c g", "", "Y4 O5 c e g"

Command/Keyword: PLAY Switches
Syntax:          PLAY [/STATUS | /MIDI | /MIDI ON | /MIDI OFF | /MIDI RESET | ?]
Description:     Queries or toggles the Windows default MIDI output subsystem state and displays active device details.
Example:         PLAY /STATUS: PLAY /MIDI ON


Complete PLAY Sub-String Control Codes:
  Notes:           a, b, c, d, e, f, g (notes in current octave)
                   A, B, C, D, E, F, G (notes in octave above current octave)
  Accidentals:     # (sharp, +1 semitone), $ (flat, -1 semitone)
                   Can immediately follow or precede a note letter (e.g. c# or #c, d$ or $d)
  Rests:           & (silence for the current note duration; e.g. &5)
  Durations (1-9): Sets duration for subsequent notes or attaches to a single note:
                     1 = Semiquaver (1/16th note, 0.25 beat)
                     2 = Dotted semiquaver (3/32nd note, 0.375 beat)
                     3 = Quaver (1/8th note, 0.5 beat)
                     4 = Dotted quaver (3/16th note, 0.75 beat)
                     5 = Crotchet (1/4 note, 1.0 beat - DEFAULT)
                     6 = Dotted crotchet (3/8th note, 1.5 beat)
                     7 = Minim (1/2 note, 2.0 beat)
                     8 = Dotted minim (3/4 note, 3.0 beat)
                     9 = Semibreve (Whole note, 4.0 beat)
  Octave (O0..O8): Sets active octave from 0 to 8 (Default O5; Middle C is O5 c -> MIDI note 60, Concert A440 is O5 a -> MIDI note 69)
  Volume (V0..V15):Sets channel volume (0-15); also sets MIDI key velocity calculated as volume * 8 (0 to 120; default V15 -> velocity 120)
  Tempo (T60..T240):Sets music tempo in BPM (beats per minute; default T120, global)
  MIDI Controls:
                   Yn : Selects MIDI channel n (1 to 16) for subsequent notes in that voice string (e.g. Y1, Y10; Y0 disables MIDI)
                   Zn : Transmits raw MIDI programming code / byte n (0-255), such as Program/Patch Changes (e.g. Z192 Z5 or Z5)
  Hardware Envelope:
                   U  : Enables hardware AY envelope volume mode on current channel
                   Wn : Sets envelope waveform shape (0 to 15; AY Register 13)
                   Xn : Sets envelope repeat period (0 to 65535; AY Registers 11 and 12)
  Channels & Noise Mixing:
                   M1 : Tone only (default)
                   M2 : Noise only
                   M3 : Tone + Noise combined
                   Nn : Sets noise frequency pitch (0 to 31; AY Register 6)
  Ties:            _  : Ties previous note, sustaining it without retriggering attack (preserves MIDI Note-On across duration)
  Loops & Repeats: ( ... )  : Repeats enclosed section twice total
                   ( ... )n : Repeats enclosed section n times (supports nesting)

6.4 Mode Switching & Emulation Engine: ZX48, ZX128, and SPECTRUM Commands
-------------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy features an authentic multi-generation Sinclair ZX Spectrum emulation engine,
supporting instantaneous switching between classic 48K hardware and full Sinclair ZX
Spectrum +3 (128K) hardware. Dedicated first-class commands ZX48 and ZX128, along with
the traditional SPECTRUM command, configure the exact hardware specifications, cycle-accurate
frame timing, memory layouts, banking registers, peripheral sets, and system variables.

Command/Keyword: ZX48
Syntax:          ZX48
Description:     Instantly switches BasinPy into authentic Sinclair ZX Spectrum 48K mode.
                 Configures standard 48K hardware architecture:
                 1. CPU Clock & Frame Timing:
                    - CPU Clock: 3.50000 MHz.
                    - Frame Period: 69,888 T-states per 50Hz video interrupt.
                    - Scanlines: 312 lines total, 224 T-states per scanline.
                    - Visible Display: Scanlines 64 to 255 (first 128 T-states per line).
                 2. Memory Layout & Slots:
                    - Slot 0 (0x0000..0x3FFF): 16KB Sinclair ZX Spectrum 48K ROM.
                      Strict ROM write protection enabled (writes to <0x4000 are discarded).
                    - Slot 1 (0x4000..0x7FFF): 16KB Contended RAM (RAM Bank 5, primary VRAM).
                    - Slot 2 (0x8000..0xBFFF): 16KB Uncontended RAM (RAM Bank 2).
                    - Slot 3 (0xC000..0xFFFF): 16KB Uncontended RAM (RAM Bank 0).
                    - Memory paging is permanently locked (bit 5 of 0x7FFD set).
                 3. System Variables (48K Initialization):
                    - RAMTOP (23730-23731 / 0x5CB2): 65367 (0xFF57).
                    - P_RAMT (23732-23733 / 0x5CB4): 65535 (0xFFFF).
                    - UDG    (23675-23676 / 0x5C7B): 65368 (0xFF58).
                    - CHARS  (23606-23607 / 0x5C36): 15360 (0x3C00, points to 0x3D00 - 256).
                    - ERR_NR (23610 / 0x5C3A):       255 (0xFF).
                    - BORDCR (23624 / 0x5C48):       56 (0x38, Paper 7, Ink 0, Border 7).
                    - ATTR_P (23693 / 0x5C8D):       56 (0x38).
                    - ATTR_T (23695 / 0x5C8F):       56 (0x38).
                    - RASP   (23697 / 0x5C91):       0.
                    - PIP    (23698 / 0x5C92):       0.
                 4. Peripherals & Bus State:
                    - AY-3-8912 sound chip is disconnected (ports 0xFFFD and 0xBFFD disabled).
                    - Reads to unattached ports return the active ULA Floating Bus byte.
                    - Single display screen active (RAM Bank 5 only; shadow screen disabled).
                    - Video Memory & Display Refresh: Screen stays clean white (Paper 7, Ink 0, Border 7).
                      RAM Bank 5 VRAM attribute buffer (offset 6144..6911) and BORDCR are initialized
                      to 0x38 (Paper 7, Ink 0).
                    - Displays confirmation banner in the output screen window: '* ZX SPECTRUM 48K *'.
Example:         ZX48


Command/Keyword: ZX128
Syntax:          ZX128
Description:     Instantly switches BasinPy into authentic Sinclair ZX Spectrum +3 (128K) mode.
                 Configures complete +3 hardware architecture, banking, and peripherals:
                 1. CPU Clock & Frame Timing:
                    - CPU Clock: 3.54690 MHz.
                    - Frame Period: 70,930 T-states per 50Hz video interrupt.
                    - Scanlines: 311 lines total, 228 T-states per scanline.
                    - Gate array contention applied to RAM Banks 4, 5, 6, and 7.
                 2. Memory Architecture & Authentic Sinclair +3 ROMs:
                    - 64KB Address Space backed by 128KB RAM (8x16KB banks) and 64KB ROM (4x16KB banks).
                    - The ROM code area (0x0000..0x3FFF) is immediately populated with authentic
                      Sinclair ZX Spectrum +3 ROM 0 (Power-up, 128K Editor, System Menu, Self-test).
                    - All 4 ROM banks are backed by the authentic Sinclair +3 64KB ROM set:
                      * ROM 0: +3 128K Editor and System Menu (starts DI; LD BC, 0x6C03; JP 0x010F).
                      * ROM 1: +3 Syntax Checker and Math routines.
                      * ROM 2: +3DOS Floppy Disk Operating System (Amstrad 1987).
                      * ROM 3: 48K BASIC Compatibility ROM.
                    - Port 0x7FFD (32765, Decoded when port16 & 0xC002 == 0x4000):
                      * Bits 0-2: RAM bank paged to upper slot (0xC000..0xFFFF). Banks 0..7.
                      * Bit 3:    Screen select (0 = Normal VRAM in Bank 5, 1 = Shadow VRAM in Bank 7).
                      * Bit 4:    ROM select low bit (combined with Port 0x1FFD bit 2).
                      * Bit 5:    Paging lock bit (1 = lock paging until next hardware reset).
                    - Port 0x1FFD (8189, Decoded when port16 & 0xF002 == 0x1000):
                      * Bit 0:    Paging mode: 0 = Normal Paging, 1 = Special All-RAM Mode.
                      * Bit 1:    Special All-RAM Mode config bit 0.
                      * Bit 2:    Normal Mode ROM select high bit / Special Mode config bit 1.
                      * Bit 3:    Floppy disk drive motor control (1 = Motor On, 0 = Motor Off).
                      * Bit 4:    Centronics parallel printer strobe line.
                 3. Paging Configurations:
                    - Normal Mode (Port 0x1FFD bit 0 = 0):
                      * Slot 0 (0x0000..0x3FFF): ROM 0..3 selected by ((0x1FFD & 4) >> 1) | ((0x7FFD & 16) >> 4).
                      * Slot 1 (0x4000..0x7FFF): RAM Bank 5 (Screen 0 VRAM).
                      * Slot 2 (0x8000..0xBFFF): RAM Bank 2.
                      * Slot 3 (0xC000..0xFFFF): RAM Bank (0x7FFD bits 0..2).
                    - Special All-RAM Mode (Port 0x1FFD bit 0 = 1):
                      All 4 slots map to RAM banks; Slot 0 (0x0000..0x3FFF) is fully writable:
                      * Config 0 (Bits 2..1 = 00): RAM 0, RAM 1, RAM 2, RAM 3
                      * Config 1 (Bits 2..1 = 01): RAM 4, RAM 5, RAM 6, RAM 7
                      * Config 2 (Bits 2..1 = 10): RAM 4, RAM 5, RAM 6, RAM 3
                      * Config 3 (Bits 2..1 = 11): RAM 4, RAM 7, RAM 6, RAM 3
                 4. System Variables (128K / +3 Initialization):
                    - BANK_M   (23388 / 0x5B5C): Copy of last byte output to Port 0x7FFD (default 0x00).
                    - BANK_678 (23389 / 0x5B5D): Internal token and ROM bank state (default 0x00).
                    - TSTACK   (23390-23391 / 0x5B5E): Temporary stack pointer for paging (0x5B5E).
                    - BANK_M3  (23399 / 0x5B67): Copy of last byte output to Port 0x1FFD (default 0x00).
                    - BORDCR   (23624 / 0x5C48): 56 (0x38, Paper 7, Ink 0, Border 7).
                    - ATTR_P   (23693 / 0x5C8D): 56 (0x38).
                    - ATTR_T   (23695 / 0x5C8F): 56 (0x38).
                 5. Peripherals & Subsystems:
                    - AY-3-8912 Sound Processor enabled on Ports 0xFFFD (address) and 0xBFFD (data).
                    - NEC uPD765A Floppy Disk Controller active:
                      * Port 0x2FFD (12285): Main Status Register (MSR, Bit 7 = RQM).
                      * Port 0x3FFD (16381): Data FIFO Register.
                    - Shadow Screen page-flipping: Toggling bit 3 of 0x7FFD instantly redirects
                      the raster beam to Bank 7 (0xC000 / shadow) with real-time screen redraw.
                    - MIDI interface and dual Sinclair/Kempston joysticks active.
                    - Video Memory & Display Refresh: Screen stays clean white (Paper 7, Ink 0, Border 7).
                      Both RAM Bank 5 and Bank 7 VRAM attribute buffers (offset 6144..6911) are initialized
                      to 0x38 (Paper 7, Ink 0).
                    - Displays confirmation banner in the output screen window: '* ZX SPECTRUM +3 (128K) *'.
Example:         ZX128


Command/Keyword: SPECTRUM
Syntax:          SPECTRUM [48 | 128]
Description:     Unified mode switching command:
                 - SPECTRUM 48 (or SPECTRUM): Routes directly to ZX48 mode switcher.
                 - SPECTRUM 128: Routes directly to ZX128 (+3) mode switcher.
Example:         SPECTRUM 128


6.4.1 ULA Contention Delay & Floating Bus Simulation
---------------------------------------------------
BasinPy emulates the authentic timing artifacts and bus physics of the Sinclair ULA:
1. Contention Delays:
   When the ULA is reading video memory during visible display lines (scanlines 64 to 255,
   T-states 0 to 127 of the scanline), any CPU memory access to contended RAM or I/O ports
   is stalled with wait states.
   - Contention Delay Pattern: [6, 5, 4, 3, 2, 1, 0, 0] based on (t_state % 8).
   - In 48K Mode: Applied to RAM Bank 5 (0x4000..0x7FFF) and Port 0xFE.
   - In +3 Mode: Applied to RAM Banks 4, 5, 6, and 7 across contended slots.
2. Floating Bus Emulation:
   When reading from an unattached or disabled port (e.g. unattached 0xFF in 48K mode):
   - During visible display lines (64..255) and display cycles (0..127): The read returns the
     exact VRAM bitmap or attribute byte currently on the internal data bus being sampled by the
     ULA raster beam at that microsecond.
   - During horizontal/vertical blanking or border intervals: Returns 0xFF (255).


6.5 ROM Subsystem & Dynamic Bank Switching: ROM / ROMS Command & Roms Directory
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy features an authentic ROM management subsystem supporting external ROM image files,
dynamic runtime bank hot-swapping, and built-in peripheral hardware initialization.

1. ROM Search & Auto-Loading Hierarchy:
   At startup and whenever switching machine modes (ZX48, ZX128, CP/M), BasinPy automatically
   searches for authentic Sinclair and peripheral ROM images across multiple directories:
   - `./Roms/`
   - `./roms/`
   - `<basinpy_dir>/Roms/`
   - `<basinpy_dir>/roms/`
   If an external ROM file is not found, BasinPy seamlessly falls back to its internal,
   high-fidelity base64-decompressed ROM image.
   
   Standard Bundled & Recognized ROM Files:
   - 48.rom       : 16,384 bytes (16K standard Sinclair ZX Spectrum 48K ROM).
   - 128-0.rom    : 16,384 bytes (128K Toastrack/Spanish 128K ROM Bank 0 - Editor/Menu).
   - 128-1.rom    : 16,384 bytes (128K ROM Bank 1 - 48K BASIC compatibility).
   - plus3-0.rom  : 16,384 bytes (Sinclair +3 ROM Bank 0 - Editor and System Menu).
   - plus3-1.rom  : 16,384 bytes (Sinclair +3 ROM Bank 1 - Syntax Checker & Floating Point).
   - plus3-2.rom  : 16,384 bytes (Sinclair +3 ROM Bank 2 - +3DOS Floppy Disk OS).
   - plus3-3.rom  : 16,384 bytes (Sinclair +3 ROM Bank 3 - 48K BASIC Compatibility).
   - plus3.rom    : 65,536 bytes (Complete concatenated 64KB +3 ROM covering all 4 banks).
   - if1.rom      : 8,192 bytes (Sinclair ZX Interface 1 Shadow ROM v2).
   - disciple.rom : 8,192 bytes (Miles Gordon Technology MGT DISCiPLE Interface ROM).
   - plusd.rom    : 8,192 bytes (MGT +D Floppy Interface ROM).
   - trdos.rom    : 16,384 bytes (Technology Research Ltd. Beta 128 TR-DOS v5.04T ROM).

2. Default Hardware Peripherals:
   Upon cold boot and when switching into 48K mode via ZX48, both the Sinclair Interface 1
   and the MGT DISCiPLE interface are initialized, active, and ready by default with their
   respective shadow ROMs and hook entry vectors configured.

3. ROM / ROMS Command:
   Command/Keyword: ROM / ROMS
   Syntax:          ROM
                    ROMS
                    ROM "filename.rom" [, bank]
   Description:     Inspects available ROMs or hot-swaps active ROM banks at runtime.
                    - ROM or ROMS without parameters: Scans the Roms/ directory, prints a list
                      of all available ROM images with file sizes, and displays the currently
                      active ROM bank index (0..3).
                    - ROM "filename" [, bank]: Dynamically loads the specified ROM file from the
                      Roms/ directory and mounts it into the specified bank (0 to 3, defaults to
                      active bank or bank 0). In 48K mode, Bank 0 is re-paged with the new ROM code.
                      In +3 / 128K mode, the targeted bank is swapped instantly.
   Examples:        ROM
                    ROMS
                    ROM "48.rom"
                    ROM "plus3-2.rom", 2


6.6 Memory Banking Commands: BANK and PAGE
------------------------------------------
Command/Keyword: BANK
Syntax:          BANK <0..7>
Description:     Pages the specified 16KB RAM bank (0 to 7) into upper memory at 49152-65535 ($C000-$FFFF).
                 Preserves previous bank contents in RAM buffer.
Example:         BANK 3

Command/Keyword: PAGE
Syntax:          PAGE <0..7>
Description:     Equivalent synonym for BANK command to switch active 16KB memory page.
Example:         PAGE 1


6.7 Silicon Disc: 128K RAM Disk Operations (! Syntax)
-----------------------------------------------------
The Sinclair 128 Silicon Disc allows lightning-fast storage and retrieval of programs,
screens, binary blocks, and arrays in virtual 128K RAM without touching physical media.
In 128K syntax, the exclamation mark '!' designates the RAM disk device:

Command/Keyword: CAT !
Syntax:          CAT !
Description:     Lists all files currently resident on the virtual 128K RAM disk, displaying filename,
                 data type (PROG, SCREEN$, CODE, DATA), byte size, and free RAM available.
Example:         CAT !

Command/Keyword: SAVE !
Syntax:          SAVE !"filename" [LINE ln | CODE start, len | SCREEN$ | DATA [!]arr()]
Description:     Stores program, machine code, screen dump, or numeric/string array (including 128K DATA !arr())
                 directly into the 128K RAM disk.
Example:         SAVE !"GAME" LINE 10: SAVE !"SCORES" DATA !scores()

Command/Keyword: LOAD !
Syntax:          LOAD !"filename" [CODE [dest] | SCREEN$ | DATA [!]arr()]
Description:     Loads program, code block, screen, or numeric/string array (including 128K DATA !arr())
                 directly from the 128K RAM disk into memory.
Example:         LOAD !"SCRN" SCREEN$: LOAD !"SCORES" DATA !scores()

Command/Keyword: MERGE !
Syntax:          MERGE !"filename"
Description:     Merges a BASIC program stored on the 128K RAM disk into current resident program lines.
Example:         MERGE !"MODULE"

Command/Keyword: ERASE !
Syntax:          ERASE !"filename"
Description:     Deletes the specified file from the 128K RAM disk and reclaims memory.
Example:         ERASE !"TEMP"


6.8 128K Expression Functions (SOUND, AY, PLAY, SPECTRUM, BANK)
---------------------------------------------------------------
Function:        SOUND
Syntax:          SOUND(reg)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Reads the current value (0-255) of AY register reg.
Example:         PRINT SOUND(8)

Function:        AY
Syntax:          AY(reg)
Returns:         Number
Description:     Alias for SOUND(reg); reads current value of AY register reg.
Example:         LET vol = AY(8)

Function:        PLAY
Syntax:          PLAY / PLAY()
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns 1.0 if music is currently being sequenced by PLAY, or 0.0 if idle.
Example:         IF PLAY THEN PAUSE 10

Function:        SPECTRUM
Syntax:          SPECTRUM / SPECTRUM()
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns 128.0 if currently in 128K mode, or 48.0 if in 48K mode.
Example:         IF SPECTRUM = 128 THEN BANK 1

Function:        BANK
Syntax:          BANK / BANK()
Returns:         Number
Description:     Returns the currently paged RAM bank number (0 to 7).
Example:         PRINT "Active Bank: "; BANK


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 7. SECTION 6: SYSTEM UTILITY & MULTI-DISK SUBSYSTEM (+3, TR-DOS, OPUS, MGT, MICRODRIVES)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Target Extension:    Multi-Standard Floppy Disk & Microdrive Systems (+3, TR-DOS, Opus, MGT)
Compilation Support: [INTERPRETER ONLY] / [48K BASIC COMPATIBLE for Tape I/O]
                     (Disk management, multi-format controller switching, editor manipulation,
                     tracing, and catalog utilities such as SET DRIVE, SET MDR, ATTACH, DIR,
                     CAT, FORMAT, ERASE, MOVE, COPY, RENUMBER, FIND, REPLACE, TRON, CPM, and
                     ZXDB are IDE and runtime commands. Standard Sinclair tape commands like SAVE,
                     LOAD, VERIFY, and MERGE are 48K BASIC compatible).

BasinPy Extended includes comprehensive emulation of multiple vintage ZX Spectrum disk
controller architectures, Sinclair Microdrive cartridges, interactive file mounting,
directories, program renumbering, and execution tracing.

7.1 Multi-Disk Controller Architecture: SET DRIVE Command (MGT, PLUS3, OPUS, TRD, BETA)
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy incorporates a multi-standard floppy disk controller architecture supporting all
major vintage ZX Spectrum disk systems. The active disk system and controller emulation
are dynamically selected using the `SET DRIVE` command:

Command/Keyword: SET DRIVE
Syntax:          SET DRIVE
                 SET DRIVE <type>
Description:     Configures the active floppy disk drive system, I/O ports, system variables,
                 and file format handling:
                 - Supported Types:
                   * MGT    : Miles Gordon Technology (DISCiPLE and +D floppy interfaces).
                              Uses .mgt and .fdi disk container formats.
                   * PLUS3  : Sinclair ZX Spectrum +3 disk system (+3DOS).
                              Uses .dsk standard and extended CP/M disk containers.
                   * OPUS   : Opus Discovery 1 / 2 floppy disk subsystem.
                              Uses .opd and .dsk disk container formats.
                   * TRD    : Technology Research / Beta 128 Disk Operating System (TR-DOS).
                              Uses .trd and .scl disk container formats.
                   * BETA   : Alias for TRD.
                 - Status Query: Executing `SET DRIVE` without arguments displays the currently
                   active disk system, controller hardware status, mounted disk images, and
                   active drive letter assignments (A:, B:, etc.).
Example:         SET DRIVE TRD
                 SET DRIVE OPUS
                 SET DRIVE PLUS3
                 SET DRIVE MGT
                 SET DRIVE

7.2 Sinclair Microdrive Subsystem: SET MDR Command, Cartridge Auto-Creation & Concurrency
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Sinclair ZX Interface 1 Microdrives are fully supported both standalone and in harmony with
the active floppy disk subsystem:

Command/Keyword: SET MDR
Syntax:          SET MDR
                 SET MDR <drive_number> ...
                 SET MDR "filename" [, <drive_number>]
Description:     Mounts, inspects, or generates authentic Sinclair Microdrive cartridge images:
                 - Status Query: Executing `SET MDR` without arguments displays the status of
                   all 8 Microdrive units (1 through 8) and mounted cartridge filenames.
                 - Blank Cartridge Auto-Creation: Executing `SET MDR 1 2` (or specifying any list
                   of drive numbers 1 to 8) automatically creates brand-new, empty cartridge
                   files (`1.mdr`, `2.mdr`, etc.) formatted with the authentic Sinclair ZX
                   Interface 1 geometry: exactly 137,923 bytes (254 sectors * 543 bytes/sector).
                 - File Mounting: Executing `SET MDR "cartridge.mdr", 1` mounts an existing .mdr
                   cartridge file into the specified Microdrive unit.
                 - Concurrency: Microdrives run concurrently alongside floppy disk systems
                   (e.g., TR-DOS, Opus, or +3). Commands targeting Microdrives (such as CAT "m",
                   LOAD *"m";1;"file", or OPEN #4,"m";1;"data") operate seamlessly without
                   syntax collisions or interrupting active floppy drives.
Example:         SET MDR 1 2
                 SET MDR "GAMES.MDR", 1
                 SET MDR

7.3 Media Mounting: ATTACH, ADD DISK / ADDDISK / ADD DRIVE (Interactive File Picker & Auto-Detection)
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy features an intelligent, unified media mounting subsystem supporting Microdrive cartridges,
floppy disk images across diverse microcomputer formats, TR-DOS images, and cassette tapes.

Command/Keyword: ATTACH
Syntax:          ATTACH
                 ATTACH "filename" [, unit_number [, type]]
Description:     Attaches a storage image to the specified or auto-detected virtual drive.
                 - Interactive File Selector Dialog: When executed without arguments (or with an empty string),
                   ATTACH opens a native graphical file chooser dialog. The dialog filters for all supported
                   storage containers:
                   * Microdrive Cartridges: .mdr
                   * Floppy Disks: .dsk, .img, .imd, .cpm, .raw, .flp, .mgt, .fdi
                   * TR-DOS Disks: .trd, .scl
                   * Cassette Tapes: .tap, .tzx
                 - Container Auto-Inspection & Drive Allocation: When a file is selected (via dialog or
                   command line without explicit unit/type), BasinPy inspects the file extension and container
                   format, then automatically routes it to the first free or appropriate virtual drive:
                   * .mdr: Sinclair Microdrive (scans Drives 1 through 8 for the next free slot).
                   * .dsk, .img, .imd, .cpm, .raw, .flp: Sinclair +3 / CP/M Floppy Disk (mounts to Drive A: / 1
                     or Drive B: / 2; automatically synchronizes with CP/M drives A: and B:).
                   * .mgt, .fdi: MGT DISCiPLE / +D Floppy Disk (Drive 1 or Drive 2).
                   * .trd, .scl: Beta 128 TR-DOS Floppy Disk (Drive A: or Drive B:).
                   * .tap, .tzx: Virtual Cassette Deck (TAP system).
                 - User Feedback: Prints an informative badge confirming the detected container format, file
                   name, and the exact destination drive (e.g. Attached '+3 Disk' (game.dsk) to +3 Drive A:).
                 - Manual Parameters: Explicitly specify "filename", unit_number (1..8 for MDR, 1/2 or A/B for disk),
                   and type (MDR, DSK, FDI, MGT, TRD).
Example:         ATTACH
                 ATTACH "MYGAME.DSK"
                 ATTACH "UTILITIES.MDR", 1, MDR

Command/Keyword: ADD DRIVE
Syntax:          ADD DRIVE
                 ADD DRIVE "filename" [, unit_number [, type]]
Description:     Unified synonym for ATTACH. When executed without parameters, invokes the interactive file
                 selector dialog and automatically routes the container to the appropriate drive.
Example:         ADD DRIVE
                 ADD DRIVE "BACKUP.DSK", 2, DSK

Command/Keyword: ADD DISK / ADDDISK
Syntax:          ADD DISK ["filename" [, unit_number [, type]]]
Description:     Mounts or creates a floppy disk drive container (.DSK, .IMG, .IMD, .CPM, .RAW, .FLP, or legacy .FDI)
                 in Drive A: (1) or Drive B: (2). Full bi-directional read and write persistence with authentic
                 +3DOS 128-byte file headers. If invoked without arguments, brings up the interactive file selector.
Example:         ADD DISK
                 ADD DISK "UTILITIES.DSK", A

Executing ATTACH, ADD DRIVE, or ADD DISK without arguments interactively prompts for a file; if canceled,
it displays the complete status of all 8 Microdrive units and both Disk Drive units (A: and B:).

7.4 TR-DOS & Beta 128 Emulation: RANDOMIZE USR 15619, CALL 0x3D13 ROM Hook & WD1793 Ports
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The TR-DOS (Beta 128) subsystem is a complete emulation of the Western Digital WD1793 floppy
disk controller and Beta 128 interface ROM:
  - Sinclair BASIC Command Syntax:
    TR-DOS commands are issued from Sinclair BASIC using the standard Beta 128 syntax:
      RANDOMIZE USR 15619: REM: <command>
    Supported commands include:
      * RANDOMIZE USR 15619: REM: CAT              (Displays TR-DOS catalog)
      * RANDOMIZE USR 15619: REM: * "A:"           (Selects Drive A:)
      * RANDOMIZE USR 15619: REM: * "B:"           (Selects Drive B:)
      * RANDOMIZE USR 15619: REM: LOAD "filename"  (Loads TR-DOS program or code)
      * RANDOMIZE USR 15619: REM: RUN "filename"   (Runs TR-DOS program)
  - Z80 Machine Code Hook (CALL 0x3D13):
    TR-DOS provides a universal machine code hook vector at address 0x3D13. Machine code
    programs call 0x3D13 with the C register loaded with the desired function code:
      * C = 0x00 : Restore (Seek Track 0)
      * C = 0x01 : Select Drive (A=0, B=1, C=2, D=3)
      * C = 0x02 : Seek Track (D = Track number)
      * C = 0x03 : Format Track
      * C = 0x04 : Write Sector (D = Track, E = Sector, HL = Buffer Address)
      * C = 0x05 : Read Sector to RAM (D = Track, E = Sector, HL = Buffer Address)
      * C = 0x06 : Read Next Sector
      * C = 0x08 : Read System Sector (Track 0, Sector 9 catalog sector)
      * C = 0x18 : Write System Sector
  - Hardware I/O Port Map (WD1793):
    * Port #1F (31)  : WD1793 Command / Status Register
    * Port #3F (63)  : WD1793 Track Register
    * Port #5F (95)  : WD1793 Sector Register
    * Port #7F (127) : WD1793 Data Register
    * Port #FF (255) : System Control Register (Drive Select: bits 0-1, Side Select: bit 4)
  - System Variables:
    * 23796 : Currently selected drive (0=A, 1=B, 2=C, 3=D)
    * 23797 : Maximum available drives (default: 4)

7.5 Opus Discovery Disk Subsystem: WD1770 Ports, OPD Geometry & System Variables
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The Opus Discovery 1 & 2 floppy disk system is emulated with its Western Digital WD1770 controller:
  - Hardware I/O Port Map (WD1770):
    * Port #38 (56) : WD1770 Command / Status Register
    * Port #39 (57) : WD1770 Track Register
    * Port #3A (58) : WD1770 Sector Register
    * Port #3B (59) : WD1770 Data Register
    * Port #EE (238): Drive & Side Control Register
  - File Format:
    Supports .opd (Opus Discovery raw sector dump: 40 tracks, 18 sectors/track, 256 bytes/sector,
    giving 184,320 bytes for single-sided or 368,640 bytes for double-sided) and standard .dsk.
  - System Variables:
    Maintains Opus system variables tracking active drive (Drive 1 / 2) and Directory Track (Track 0).

7.6 Forth Multi-Disk Dictionary Words
-------------------------------------
BasinPy's integrated Abersoft Forth / fig-FORTH dictionary provides direct words for disk control:
  - SET-DRIVE : ( addr len -- ) Sets active disk system by name (e.g. S" TRD" SET-DRIVE).
  - SET-MDR   : ( drive -- ) Attaches or activates Microdrive unit.
  - ATTACH    : ( addr len -- ) Attaches storage container from Forth.
  - ADD-DRIVE : ( addr len unit type -- ) Mounts storage container with explicit unit and type.

7.7 Directory & Catalog Utilities: DIR, CAT, CAT EXP, ZXDB
-----------------------------------------------------------
Command/Keyword: DIR
Syntax:          DIR [drive_number | "filter"]
Description:     Lists files present on the currently selected disk drive or host directory.
Example:         DIR 1

Command/Keyword: CAT
Syntax:          CAT ["drive:" | drive_specifier]
Description:     Displays the catalog of files, lengths, and sectors for the active storage device (e.g. CAT "a:", CAT "b:", CAT "m:", CAT "t:", CAT 1, CAT !).
Example:         CAT "a:"

Command/Keyword: CAT EXP
Syntax:          CAT EXP ["drive:"]
Description:     Displays the Sinclair ZX Spectrum +3 expanded directory listing, including filename, file type (BASIC, CODE, DATA, SCREEN$), size in bytes, attributes (R/W), and remaining free disk space.
Example:         CAT EXP "a:"

Command/Keyword: ZXDB
Syntax:          ZXDB [search_query]
Description:     Opens the authentic ZX Spectrum (32x24 Sinclair attribute display) online
                 software browser and search engine querying ZXDB (the Sinclair ZX Spectrum
                 online database via zxinfo.dk / spectrumcomputing.co.uk).
                 - Lists matching titles with publisher, year, format badges (TAP, TZX, Z80, SNA, DSK),
                   and available release files.
                 - If called with an argument (e.g. ZXDB "MANIC" or ZXDB "KNIGHT LORE"), immediately
                   searches for matching software.
                 - Real-time keyboard navigation: UP / Q (move up), DOWN / A (move down),
                   PAGE UP / PAGE DOWN (scroll pages), HOME / END (jump to top/bottom).
                 - S or / : Enter search/filter query directly within the browser interface.
                 - ENTER  : Opens file selection for the selected title or initiates download.
                  - Two-Step Download & Multi-Archive Search Architecture:
                    1. Primary ZXDB Download: BasinPy uses the release file information from ZXDB 1st
                       to download directly from the primary ZXDB repository.
                    2. Automatic ZX-Art Backup Search: If the primary ZXDB search produces 0 hits,
                       fails, or times out, BasinPy seamlessly executes a real-time backup query
                       against the ZX-Art database (zxart.ee API), displaying matching releases
                       with direct download links.
                    3. Extended Archive Search & Search Options: If the primary ZXDB file download
                       fails, is corrupt/dummy, or is distribution-denied on ZXDB, BasinPy prompts the
                       user with options to extend search across all major retro archives:
                       * [1] SOURCE : Toggle archive repository:
                                      ALL ARCHIVES -> INTERNET ARCHIVE -> WOS MIRRORS -> ZX-ART
                       * [2] FORMAT : Toggle format filter (ALL / TAP / TZX / Z80 / SNA / DSK).
                       * [3] QUERY  : Edit and refine external search query string.
                       * [ENTER/Y]  : Search external archives immediately.
                       * [ESC/N]    : Cancel and return to ZXDB release list.
                    4. Archive Candidate File Selection: Discovered archive files from Internet Archive,
                       World of Spectrum (WoS) mirrors, and ZX-Art are presented in an interactive
                       Sinclair Spectrum list with format tags ([TAP], [TZX], [Z80], etc.), file sizes,
                       and archive badges ([ZX-Art], [WoS Mirror], [Archive.org]). The user navigates
                       with cursor keys, selects the desired candidate, and presses [ENTER] to download,
                       unpack, and boot the program.
                  - ESC    : Returns to previous screen or exits the ZXDB browser.
                  - Includes an offline fallback catalog of classic Spectrum software if no
                    network connection is available.
Example:         ZXDB "MANIC MINER"
                 ZXDB "KNIGHT LORE"
                 ZXDB "EXOLON"
                 ZXDB


7.8 Program Transfer & Verification: SAVE, LOAD, VERIFY, MERGE
--------------------------------------------------------------
Command/Keyword: SAVE
Syntax:          SAVE "[drive:]filename" [LINE start_line | CODE start, len | SCREEN$ | DATA arr()]
Description:     Saves the resident BASIC program, machine code memory range, screen bitmap, or data array to the attached tape, disk (Drive A: / B:), or RAMdisk (Drive M:).
Example:         SAVE "a:GAME.BAS" LINE 10

Command/Keyword: LOAD
Syntax:          LOAD "[drive:]filename" [CODE [start] | SCREEN$ | DATA arr()]
Description:     Loads a program, machine code block, full 6912-byte screen image, or data array into memory from tape, disk (A: / B:), or RAMdisk (M:).
Example:         LOAD "a:TITLE" SCREEN$

Command/Keyword: VERIFY
Syntax:          VERIFY "[drive:]filename" [CODE | SCREEN$ | DATA arr()]
Description:     Verifies that a saved file on tape, disk, or RAMdisk matches memory without modifying RAM.
Example:         VERIFY "a:GAME.BAS"

Command/Keyword: MERGE
Syntax:          MERGE "[drive:]filename"
Description:     Loads a BASIC program from storage (tape, disk A:/B:, or RAMdisk M:), adding or overwriting lines into the current program without clearing existing lines.
Example:         MERGE "a:EXTRA.BAS"


7.9 Sinclair ZX Spectrum +3 Disk Management: FORMAT, ERASE, MOVE, COPY
----------------------------------------------------------------------
Command/Keyword: FORMAT
Syntax:          FORMAT "[drive:][label]"
Description:     Formats floppy drive A: or B: (with optional disk label) or clears the 128K RAMdisk M: (e.g. FORMAT "a:SYSTEM", FORMAT "b:", FORMAT "m:"). Also supports Microdrive formatting (FORMAT "m";1;"cart").
Example:         FORMAT "a:DISC1"

Command/Keyword: ERASE
Syntax:          ERASE "[drive:]filename"
Description:     Deletes a file from floppy drive A: or B:, RAMdisk M:, or Microdrive. Supports wildcard deletion (e.g. ERASE "a:*.*", ERASE "m:*.*") to purge entire directories.
Example:         ERASE "a:OLDGAME.BAS"

Command/Keyword: MOVE
Syntax:          MOVE "src_dev:file" TO "dst_dev:file"
Description:     Renames a file on the same drive (e.g. MOVE "a:old" TO "a:new", MOVE "m:old" TO "m:new") or moves a file across storage devices, deleting the source upon successful copy.
Example:         MOVE "a:PROG.BAS" TO "a:BACKUP.BAS"

Command/Keyword: COPY
Syntax:          COPY ["src_dev:file" TO "dst_dev:file" | TO SCREEN$ | TO LPRINT]
Description:     Sinclair ZX Spectrum +3 multi-mode copy command:
                 1. File duplication: COPY "a:src" TO "b:dst", COPY "a:src" TO "m:dst"
                 2. Direct screen render: COPY "a:pic.scr" TO SCREEN$
                 3. Text dump to printer: COPY "a:doc.txt" TO LPRINT
                 4. Bare COPY (no args): Dumps the current screen text directly to the printer stream.
Example:         COPY "a:PICTURE" TO SCREEN$


7.5 Program Development & Utilities: EDIT, RENUMBER, FIND, REPLACE, TRON, TROFF, RUN, RUN/FS, MOUSEY, QUIT
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Command/Keyword: EDIT
Syntax:          EDIT line_number
Description:     Pulls the specified program line down into the command prompt line for interactive editing.
Example:         EDIT 100

Command/Keyword: RENUMBER / RENUM
Syntax:          RENUMBER [start_line [, step_interval]]
Description:     Renumbers all program lines and adjusts internal GOTO, GOSUB, and RESTORE destinations.
Example:         RENUM 100, 10

Command/Keyword: FIND
Syntax:          FIND "string"  or  FIND string
Description:     Locates occurrences of a target string across the entire BASIC program listing.
                 Automatically scrolls the IDE listing area so that the program is listed with the line
                 containing the first find visible in the listing area.
                 Prompts in the bottom input area with mode letter '?' and:
                 STOP (S) or FIND NEXT (F)
                 - Pressing 'F' (or 'f') jumps to and displays the next occurrence in the program listing.
                 - Pressing 'S' (or 's' / ESC) stops searching and restores normal direct command prompt mode.
                 - Reaching the end of all matches automatically concludes the search.
Example:         FIND "SCORE"
                 FIND PRINT

Command/Keyword: REPLACE
Syntax:          REPLACE "old", "new"  or  REPLACE "old" TO "new"
Description:     Searches the entire program listing and replaces every occurrence of the "old" substring
                 with the "new" substring across all program lines.
                 Upon completing replacement, internal tokenization, DATA lines, and compiled caches are cleared,
                 and the program is automatically relisted from the 1st line (resetting listing scroll position to 0).
Example:         REPLACE "CLS", "CLEAR"
                 REPLACE "old_var", "new_var"

Command/Keyword: TRON
Syntax:          TRON
Description:     Enables execution tracing; as each statement runs, the IDE highlights the line in the listing.
Example:         TRON

Command/Keyword: TROFF
Syntax:          TROFF
Description:     Disables execution tracing.
Example:         TROFF

Command/Keyword: RUN/FS
Syntax:          RUN/FS [line_number]
Description:     Starts program execution and automatically maximizes the CRT display to Fullscreen Mode.
Example:         RUN/FS

Command/Keyword: AUTORUN
Syntax:          AUTORUN [n | ON | OFF]
Description:     Controls whether loaded BASIC programs with an embedded autostart line automatically execute upon loading.
                 - AUTORUN 1 (or AUTORUN ON): Enables automatic execution of loaded programs (default behavior).
                 - AUTORUN 0 (or AUTORUN OFF): Disables automatic execution. Loaded programs remain in memory for inspection, listing, or editing.
                 - AUTORUN (without arguments): Queries and displays the current state (AUTORUN is ON (1) or AUTORUN is OFF (0)).
                 Accepts expressions (e.g. AUTORUN 1-1). Explicit RUN "..." commands continue to execute regardless of the AUTORUN setting.
Example:         AUTORUN 0
                 LOAD "game.tap"
                 LIST

Command/Keyword: MOUSEY
Syntax:          MOUSEY [/KEMP | /AMX]
Description:     Switches or toggles active mouse emulation mode between Kempston Mouse (/KEMP) and AMX Mouse (/AMX).
Example:         MOUSEY /KEMP

Command/Keyword: QUIT
Syntax:          QUIT
Description:     Safely closes the BasinPy interpreter and exits to the host operating system.
Example:         QUIT

Command/Keyword: HELP (or ?)
Syntax:          HELP [topic | keyword]   or   ? [topic | keyword]
Description:     Displays real-time interactive help directly within the BasinPy 32-column display.
                 - HELP (or ?): Displays the master quick-reference guide including built-in editor keywords
                   and extended command categories (Next, Audio, Graphics, DOS, Network, Procedures).
                 - HELP EDITORS: Lists all 15+ built-in visual and interactive development studios
                   (MUSIC tracker, DRUMS rhythm box, SFX preset studio, UDGED matrix tool, EDSPRITES Next sprite editor,
                   EDTILES tilemap designer, PAINT hi-colour studio, ICONED WIMP designer, Z80 Zeus assembler,
                   MONITOR hex disassembler, DECOMPILE/ZLOAD snapshot analyzer, CPM 2.2/Plus shell, FORTH, LOGO, PROLOG).
                 - HELP COMMANDS: Displays custom extended command keywords grouped by subsystem.
                 - HELP <keyword>: Shows syntax and concise documentation for any specific command or tool
                   (e.g., HELP MUSIC, HELP TURBO, HELP SFX, HELP SPRITE, HELP TILEMAP, HELP SOUND, HELP PROC).
                 All output lines are strictly <= 32 characters to prevent wrapping artifacts on the CRT canvas.
Example:         HELP
                 HELP EDITORS
                 HELP COMMANDS
                 HELP MUSIC
                 HELP TURBO

Command/Keyword: COMPLIANCE / SYNTAX
Syntax:          COMPLIANCE [line_number] | SYNTAX [line_number]
Description:     Inspects resident program lines and evaluates keyword compliance across the 6 system tiers:
                 1. 48K BASIC: Pure Sinclair ZX Spectrum 48K standard keywords.
                 2. 128K Extra: Hardware commands for 128K/+2/+3 (PLAY, SOUND, SPECTRUM, BANK, PAGE, CAT !).
                 3. Beta BASIC 3.0: Structured programming (PROC, BOX, FILL, WINDOW, DPOKE, TIME, UDG).
                 4. Beta BASIC 4.0: Algorithms, bitwise & macros (SORT, SHUFFLE, PATTERN, DEF KEY, DIM !, LET !).
                 5. Peripherals: Dedicated hardware (JOYSTICK, MOUSE, DRUM, SPECDRUM, SAY, FORTH).
                 6. System: BasinPy system management (CPM, SPEC, ATTACH, ADD DISK, TRON, TROFF).
                 Outputs total counts, active keywords, and minimum machine target requirements.
Example:         COMPLIANCE


7.11 Sinclair ZX Spectrum +3 & CP/M 2.2 / CP/M Plus (v3.0) Native Operating System: CPM, SPEC
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy Extended incorporates a complete, authentic CP/M Plus (v3.0, CP/M 2.2 compatible)
operating system environment designed for native Sinclair Z80 CPU execution. BasinPy allows
running standard CP/M .COM binary executables, executing full BDOS and BIOS system calls,
rendering through a multi-standard terminal escape sequence engine (VT52, ADM-3A, ANSI), and
mounting multi-format CP/M disk images across microcomputer platforms.

Virtual Drives & Default Disk Configuration:
  - Drive A:  Primary Floppy Drive. Pre-loaded with official Sinclair ZX Spectrum +3 CP/M system utilities.
  - Drive B:  Secondary Floppy Drive. Used for mounting external CP/M disk images (.DSK, .IMG, .IMD, etc.).
  - Drive M:  64K Dynamic RAMdisk for ultra-fast temporary file operations.

Command/Keyword: CPM
Syntax:          CPM ["command_line"]
Description:     Initiates the Sinclair ZX Spectrum +3 CP/M Plus environment.
                 - Automatic +3 Mode Configuration: Automatically configures authentic Sinclair ZX Spectrum +3
                   hardware mode (set_zxp3_mode), loading +3 ROMs and preparing the uPD765A floppy controller.
                 - Drive Synchronization: Automatically synchronizes all currently mounted +3 disk drives (Drive 1
                   and Drive 2) with CP/M virtual drives A: and B:, detecting floppy disk geometry, sector skew,
                   and directory structures.
                 - Startup Banner: Displays the Locomotive Software CP/M Plus startup banner, reporting the current
                   mounted status and format badge for Drive A: and Drive B:, then presents the interactive A> prompt.
                 - Direct Execution: If a command line is provided, executes that command directly without waiting.
Example:         CPM

Command/Keyword: ADD DISK
Syntax:          ADD DISK "filename", Drive (A or B)
Description:     Mounts an existing CP/M disk image (.DSK, .IMG, .IMD, .CPM, .RAW, .FLP) to Drive A: or B: for bi-directional read and write operations. BasinPy's unified CPMDiskImage engine auto-detects track/sector geometry, sector skew, and directory layouts across 11 standard formats. If the specified file does not exist, BasinPy automatically formats a fresh 180K CP/M disk image in the specified format with an empty directory. Any file saved, modified, or deleted on that drive is immediately synchronized and written back to the disk image file on the host filesystem.
Example:         ADD DISK "KAYPRO.IMG", B

Command/Keyword: SPEC
Syntax:          SPEC
Description:     Exits the CP/M environment and returns directly to Sinclair ZX Spectrum +3 Beta BASIC mode. Restores the standard 32x24 character screen layout, resets colors to PAPER 7: INK 0: BRIGHT 0, clears the graphics canvas, and prints '0 OK, 0:1'.

Resident Commands:

  - README.TXT     : Sinclair ZX Spectrum +3 CP/M Plus system overview and quick-start guide.


7.11.1 System Architecture, Memory Map & Zero Page Vectors (0x0000..0x00FF, 64K RAM)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Unlike standard Sinclair 48K BASIC which reserves 0x0000..0x3FFF for ROM, BasinPy's CP/M
mode configures the SinclairZ80CPU with full 64KB RAM access, lifting ROM write protections.
This establishes a standard CP/M Transient Program Area (TPA) spanning from 0x0100 up to
the BDOS/BIOS boundary at 0xFE00 (providing a massive 63.5KB of contiguous TPA memory).

The standard CP/M Zero Page (0x0000..0x00FF) is configured authentic to Digital Research specifications:
  - 0x0000..0x0002 : Warm Boot Vector (JP 0xFE03 -> BIOS WBOOT vector).
  - 0x0003         : I/O Byte (IOBYTE, hardware device redirection configuration).
  - 0x0004         : Current Drive & User Number (Bits 0..3: Drive 0=A..15=P; Bits 4..7: User 0..15).
  - 0x0005..0x0007 : BDOS System Call Entry Vector (JP 0xFE06 -> BDOS dispatcher).
  - 0x0008..0x0037 : Interrupt Vectors & Restart vectors (RST 1..7).
  - 0x0038..0x003A : Maskable Interrupt Vector (RST 38H).
  - 0x005C..0x006B : Default File Control Block 1 (FCB1, populated from 1st command-line argument).
  - 0x006C..0x007F : Default File Control Block 2 (FCB2, populated from 2nd command-line argument).
  - 0x0080..0x00FF : Default DMA Buffer (128 bytes) and Command-Line Parameter Tail:
                     * 0x0080     : Length of command tail string (excluding terminating 0x00).
                     * 0x0081..0x00FF : Command tail text characters (ASCII), terminated by 0x00.
  - 0x0100..0xFDFF : Transient Program Area (TPA). Standard origin (ORG 100H) for CP/M .COM binaries.
  - 0xFE00..0xFE33 : CP/M BIOS Jump Table (17 vector entries with RET / hardware traps).
  - 0xFE34..0xFFFF : BDOS & BIOS runtime workspace, disk buffers, and stack.


7.11.2 Native Sinclair Z80 CPU Execution of CP/M .COM Binaries (run_com_program)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy executes unmodified CP/M 8080/Z80 binary executables (.COM files) directly on
the native SinclairZ80CPU emulation core. When a binary is launched:
  1. Low-Memory Initialization: Zero page vectors (0x0000, 0x0005), default FCB1 (0x005C),
     FCB2 (0x006C), and the command tail DMA buffer (0x0080) are formatted from user parameters.
  2. Binary Injection: Binary bytes are loaded directly into RAM at address 0x0100.
  3. Register Setup:
     - PC = 0x0100 (Program Counter starts at TPA origin).
     - SP = 0xFE00 (Stack Pointer initialized at the top of TPA).
     - AF = 0x0040 (Zero flag set, A=0).
     - BC = 0x0000, DE = 0x0000, HL = 0x0000.
     - Return address 0x0000 is pushed to the stack (so a bare RET terminates back to Warm Boot).
  4. Execution Loop:
     - SinclairZ80CPU steps instructions with cycle-accurate timing.
     - Low-memory addresses (0x0000, 0x0005) and BIOS jump table entries (0xFE00..0xFE33)
       are trapped seamlessly by check_rom_hook() and routed to the BDOS/BIOS handlers.
     - Real-time keyboard input events (Return, Backspace, Ctrl+C break, alphanumeric characters)
       are queued and dispatched to BDOS console input calls.
     - CRT screen redraws are dynamically synchronized at regular instruction intervals.


7.11.3 Complete BDOS System Calls (0..37, 45, 105, 108) & BIOS Vector Table (0xFE00..0xFE33)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy provides full implementation of the official Digital Research CP/M 2.2 and CP/M Plus
Basic Disk Operating System (BDOS) services invoked via CALL 0005H (Function code in C register,
arguments in DE register, single-byte result in A/L, word result in HL/BA):

System Control & Device I/O Functions:
  - Func 0   (0x00) : P_TERMCPM - System Reset / Warm Boot. Terminates execution cleanly.
  - Func 1   (0x01) : C_READ    - Console Input. Waits for character from keyboard, echoes to screen.
  - Func 2   (0x02) : C_WRITE   - Console Output. Outputs character in E register to screen.
  - Func 3   (0x03) : A_READ    - Auxiliary / Reader Input. Returns character in A (0x1A on EOF).
  - Func 4   (0x04) : A_WRITE   - Auxiliary / Punch Output. Sends character in E to aux port.
  - Func 5   (0x05) : L_WRITE   - List / Printer Output. Sends character in E to listing channel.
  - Func 6   (0x06) : C_RAWIO   - Direct Console I/O. If E=0xFF, returns input character or 0; else prints E.
  - Func 7   (0x07) : GET_IOB   - Get I/O Byte. Returns current IOBYTE from 0x0003 in A.
  - Func 8   (0x08) : SET_IOB   - Set I/O Byte. Stores value in E into 0x0003.
  - Func 9   (0x09) : C_WRITESTR- Print String. Outputs characters at DE address until '$' delimiter.
  - Func 10  (0x0A) : C_READSTR - Buffered Console Input. Reads edited line into buffer at DE:
                                  DE[0]=max buffer size, DE[1]=actual characters returned, DE[2..]=text.
  - Func 11  (0x0B) : C_STAT    - Get Console Status. Returns 0xFF if character ready, 0x00 if none.
  - Func 12  (0x0C) : S_BDOSVER - Return Version Number. Returns 0x0022 (CP/M 2.2) or 0x0031 (CP/M Plus).

Disk & Drive Management Functions:
  - Func 13  (0x0D) : DRV_ALLRESET - Reset Disk Subsystem. Resets default drive to A:, DMA to 0x0080.
  - Func 14  (0x0E) : DRV_SET      - Select Disk. Selects drive in E (0=A..15=P). Returns 0 or 0xFF.
  - Func 24  (0x18) : DRV_LOGINVEC - Return Login Vector. Bitmask of online drives in HL (0x0003 for A/B).
  - Func 25  (0x19) : DRV_GET      - Return Current Disk. Returns active drive index (0=A..15=P) in A.
  - Func 26  (0x1A) : F_DMAOFF     - Set DMA Address. Sets transfer buffer address to DE.
  - Func 27  (0x1B) : DRV_ALLOCVEC - Get Allocation Vector. Returns allocation bitmask pointer in HL.
  - Func 28  (0x1C) : DRV_SETRO    - Write Protect Current Disk. Flags active drive as read-only.
  - Func 29  (0x1D) : DRV_ROVEC    - Get Read-Only Vector. Returns read-only drive bitmask in HL.
  - Func 31  (0x1F) : DRV_DPB      - Get Disk Parameter Block. Returns pointer to DPB in HL.
  - Func 32  (0x20) : F_USERNUM    - Get/Set User Code. If E=0xFF, returns current user; else sets user (0..15).
  - Func 37  (0x25) : DRV_RESET    - Reset Drive. Resets drive bitmask in DE.

File Control Block (FCB) Operations:
  - Func 15  (0x0F) : F_OPEN     - Open File. Opens file specified by FCB at DE. Returns 0 or 0xFF.
  - Func 16  (0x10) : F_CLOSE    - Close File. Flushes directory updates and saves disk image.
  - Func 17  (0x11) : F_SFIRST   - Search First. Finds 1st matching directory entry (supports '?' wildcards).
                                   Copies 32-byte directory record to current DMA buffer.
  - Func 18  (0x12) : F_SNEXT    - Search Next. Finds subsequent directory match, copies to DMA.
  - Func 19  (0x13) : F_DELETE   - Delete File. Deletes files matching FCB name and wildcards.
  - Func 20  (0x14) : F_READ     - Read Sequential. Reads 128-byte record into DMA, increments FCB.CR/EX.
  - Func 21  (0x15) : F_WRITE    - Write Sequential. Writes 128-byte DMA record into file, auto-expanding.
  - Func 22  (0x16) : F_MAKE     - Make / Create File. Creates new empty file on disk and initializes FCB.
  - Func 23  (0x17) : F_RENAME   - Rename File. Renames file from FCB1 (DE+1..11) to FCB2 (DE+17..27).
  - Func 30  (0x1E) : F_ATTRIB   - Set File Attributes. Sets R/O and System flags in directory entry.
  - Func 33  (0x21) : F_READRAND - Read Random. Reads record indexed by 24-bit random record field (R0..R2).
  - Func 34  (0x22) : F_WRITRAND - Write Random. Writes DMA buffer to record indexed by R0..R2.
  - Func 35  (0x23) : F_SIZE     - Compute File Size. Calculates total 128-byte records, writes to R0..R2.
  - Func 36  (0x24) : F_RANDREC  - Set Random Record. Converts current sequential FCB pointer to R0..R2.
  - Func 40  (0x28) : F_WRITEZF  - Write Random With Zero Fill. Writes random record, zeroing unwritten gap.

CP/M Plus (v3.0) Extended Services:
  - Func 45  (0x2D) : Set Hardware Error Action. Configures BDOS error trapping (return code vs prompt).
  - Func 105 (0x69) : Get Date and Time. Returns system clock and date records.
  - Func 108 (0x6C) : Get/Set Return Code. Programs process exit status code.

CP/M BIOS Jump Table Vectors (0xFE00..0xFE33):
  The BIOS entry points are mapped to standard 3-byte jump slots at 0xFE00..0xFE33:
    * 0xFE00 : BOOT   - Cold start initialization
    * 0xFE03 : WBOOT  - Warm boot / reload CCP and reset
    * 0xFE06 : CONST  - Console status (returns 0xFF in A if key ready, 0x00 if empty)
    * 0xFE09 : CONIN  - Console input (returns ASCII character in A)
    * 0xFE0C : CONOUT - Console output (prints character in C register)
    * 0xFE0F : LIST   - List device output (character in C)
    * 0xFE12 : PUNCH  - Punch device output (character in C)
    * 0xFE15 : READER - Reader device input (returns character in A)
    * 0xFE18 : HOME   - Move drive head to Track 0
    * 0xFE1B : SELDSK - Select disk drive (disk in C, returns DPH in HL)
    * 0xFE1E : SETTRK - Set track number (track in BC)
    * 0xFE21 : SETSEC - Set sector number (sector in BC)
    * 0xFE24 : SETDMA - Set DMA transfer address (buffer in BC)
    * 0xFE27 : READ   - Read physical sector (returns 0 in A on success)
    * 0xFE2A : WRITE  - Write physical sector (returns 0 in A on success)
    * 0xFE2D : LISTST - List device status (returns 0xFF in A if ready)
    * 0xFE30 : SECTRN - Sector translation (skew mapping from logical in BC to physical in HL)


7.11.4 Multi-Standard CRT Terminal Emulation Engine (CPMScreen: VT52, ADM-3A, ANSI/VT100)
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy's CPMScreen terminal engine provides comprehensive terminal emulation for CP/M software,
supporting three distinct escape sequence standards used across historic microcomputers:

1. DEC VT52 Terminal Emulation:
   - ESC Y <row+32> <col+32> : Direct cursor addressing (row and col offset by ASCII 32).
   - ESC A                   : Cursor Up by 1 row.
   - ESC B                   : Cursor Down by 1 row.
   - ESC C                   : Cursor Right by 1 column.
   - ESC D                   : Cursor Left by 1 column.
   - ESC H                   : Cursor Home (row 0, col 0).
   - ESC J                   : Clear from cursor to end of screen.
   - ESC K                   : Clear from cursor to end of current line.

2. Lear Siegler ADM-3A / Kaypro / Osborne Terminal Emulation:
   - ESC = <row+32> <col+32> : Direct cursor addressing (ADM-3A / Kaypro standard).
   - Ctrl+^ (0x1E)           : Cursor Home (row 0, col 0).
   - Ctrl+Z (0x1A)           : Clear Entire Screen (CLS) and home cursor.
   - Backspace (0x08, 0x7F)  : Destructive backspace with character erasure.
   - Tab (0x09)              : Advance cursor to next 8-column tab stop.

3. ANSI / DEC VT100 Terminal Emulation (80x24 Dedicated Green Phosphor CRT Window):
   BasinPy features an authentic DEC VT100 terminal window activated via 'DISPLAY 80', 'SET24X80', or 'CPM /FS'.
   - Dedicated OS/Pygame Window: 80 columns x 24 rows (672x416 px), black paper (#000000), crisp green phosphor text:
     * Standard Green : RGB(34, 220, 34)
     * Bright / Bold  : RGB(68, 255, 68)
     * Dim Text       : RGB(18, 128, 18)
     * Block Cursor   : RGB(50, 255, 50) with blinking timer.
   - ZX Interface 1 RS-232 Serial Mapping:
     * Directly bound to ZX Interface 1 RS-232 serial channel 'b'.
     * BIOS Call 6 (PUNCH) and BDOS Function 4 (Punch Output) route character streams directly to the active VT100 display.
     * High-speed binary (SerialBinChannel) and text (SerialTextChannel) streaming channels mirror all output directly to VT100.
   - Supported Escape Sequences:
     * ESC [ <r> ; <c> H       : Direct cursor position (1-based row and column).
     * ESC [ <r> ; <c> f       : Horizontal and vertical position (HVP).
     * ESC [ <n> A / B / C / D : Cursor movement Up, Down, Forward, Backward by n cells.
     * ESC [ 2 J / ESC [ J     : Clear entire screen / clear from cursor to bottom.
     * ESC [ 2 K / ESC [ K     : Clear line / clear from cursor to end of line.
     * ESC [ <top> ; <bot> r   : Sets scroll margins (top and bottom row boundaries).
     * ESC [ 7 m / ESC [ 0 m   : Enable Inverse Video / Reset Normal Video.
     * ESC [ 1 m / ESC [ 2 m   : Bold / Dim green phosphor intensity.
     * ESC 7 / ESC 8           : Save / Restore cursor coordinates.
     * ESC Y <r> <c>           : VT52 cursor addressing compatibility mode.
     * ESC [ c / ESC [ 6 n     : Primary Device Attributes and Cursor Position reports.


7.11.5 Multi-Format CP/M Disk Geometry Engine (CPMDiskImage: 11 Standard Microcomputer Formats)
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy includes an automated disk geometry detection and sector read/write engine (CPMDiskImage)
supporting 11 industry-standard CP/M disk formats across major historic microcomputers:

+-------------------------------------+--------+-------+---------+----------+----------+------------+------------+--------------------+
| Machine / Format Name               | Tracks | Sides | Sectors | Sec Size | Res. Trk | Block Size | Dir Entr.  | Total Capacity     |
+-------------------------------------+--------+-------+---------+----------+----------+------------+------------+--------------------+
| Sinclair +3 / PCW 180K (CF2)        | 40     | 1     | 9       | 512      | 1        | 1024       | 64         | 184,320 bytes      |
| Sinclair +3 / PCW 720K (CF2DD)      | 80     | 2     | 9       | 512      | 1        | 2048       | 256        | 737,280 bytes      |
| Amstrad CPC / PCW 360K (DSDD)       | 40     | 2     | 9       | 512      | 1        | 2048       | 128        | 368,640 bytes      |
| IBM 3740 Standard 8" SSSD           | 77     | 1     | 26      | 128      | 2        | 1024       | 64         | 256,256 bytes      |
| Kaypro II (200K)                    | 40     | 1     | 10      | 512      | 1        | 1024       | 64         | 204,800 bytes      |
| Kaypro 4 (400K)                     | 80     | 1     | 10      | 512      | 1        | 2048       | 128        | 409,600 bytes      |
| Osborne 1 SSSD (100K)               | 40     | 1     | 10      | 256      | 3        | 1024       | 64         | 102,400 bytes      |
| Osborne 1 SSDD (200K)               | 40     | 1     | 5       | 1024     | 3        | 2048       | 64         | 204,800 bytes      |
| Apple II SoftCard CP/M (140K)       | 35     | 1     | 16      | 256      | 3        | 1024       | 64         | 143,360 bytes      |
| Commodore 128 1571 (340K)           | 70     | 1     | 5       | 1024     | 2        | 1024       | 64         | 358,400 bytes      |
| Commodore 128 1581 (800K)           | 80     | 2     | 10      | 512      | 1        | 2048       | 128        | 819,200 bytes      |
| PC 720K CP/M                        | 80     | 2     | 9       | 512      | 1        | 2048       | 128        | 737,280 bytes      |
| PC 1.44M CP/M                       | 80     | 2     | 18      | 512      | 1        | 2048       | 256        | 1,474,560 bytes    |
+-------------------------------------+--------+-------+---------+----------+----------+------------+------------+--------------------+

IBM 3740 Standard 8" SSSD 6:1 Sector Interleaving:
  The IBM 3740 standard 8" single-density floppy uses authentic 6:1 physical sector skew.
  BasinPy handles this with a 0-based sector translation map:
  [0, 6, 12, 18, 24, 4, 10, 16, 22, 2, 8, 14, 20, 1, 7, 13, 19, 25, 5, 11, 17, 23, 3, 9, 15, 21]
  This guarantees cycle-authentic read and write access for vintage 8-inch disk archives.

Automatic Geometry Heuristics & Scoring:
  When a disk image (.IMG, .IMD, .DSK, .CPM, .RAW, .FLP) is mounted, BasinPy tests candidate
  formats against the file size. For ambiguous sizes or multi-density images, it evaluates
  directory block offsets across candidate reserved track values (0..4), scoring entries for
  valid CP/M user numbers (0..15), ASCII filename attributes, and 0xE5 unallocated markers.
  The candidate with the highest structural confidence is automatically selected.


7.11.6 Direct Program & Disk Loading: LOAD "filename.COM", LOAD "disk.DSK", and Auto-Booting
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy supports effortless, zero-friction loading of CP/M binaries and disk images directly
from the BASIC command prompt and the host filesystem:

1. Direct .COM Program Execution:
   Typing:
     LOAD "MYPROG.COM"
   or passing a `.com` file on startup (`python main.py MYPROG.COM`) instantly:
     - Detects the CP/M binary.
     - Transitions the environment from Spectrum BASIC to the CP/M Plus runtime.
     - Installs the program onto Drive A:.
     - Prepares low-memory zero-page vectors and default FCBs.
     - Executes the binary natively on the Sinclair Z80 CPU at 0x0100.

2. Direct Disk Image Auto-Mounting & Auto-Booting:
   Typing:
     LOAD "DISKNAME.DSK"   or   LOAD "DISKNAME.IMG"   or   LOAD "DISKNAME.IMD"
   automatically:
     - Analyzes the disk container and auto-detects its CP/M geometry.
     - Mounts the disk into Drive A:.
     - Scans the directory for CP/M boot files or `.COM` executables.
     - If CP/M executables are detected, boots directly into the CP/M environment with
       Drive A: active and files immediately accessible via DIR, STAT, and direct execution.

7.12 Locomotive Mallard-80 BASIC Language Reference (CP/M Mode)
-------------------------------------------------------------
When running in Sinclair ZX Spectrum +3 CP/M mode, typing MALLARD or BAS activates
the full authentic Locomotive Software Mallard-80 BASIC interpreter. Mallard-80 is
a classic Microsoft BASIC-80 compatible interpreter designed specifically for CP/M 2.2
and CP/M Plus. It presents an 'Ok' prompt, uses 10-based line numbering, and operates
in complete isolation from standard Sinclair BASIC (preventing keyword crossover).

7.7.1 Dialect Isolation & Command Execution Rules
  - Environment Prompt:  'Ok' on successful command execution; error messages follow CP/M convention.
  - Line Numbering:      1 to 65529. Entering a line number stores or replaces that line in memory.
  - Multi-Statement:     Colon (:) separates statements on the same line (e.g. 10 CLS: PRINT X).
  - Variable Names:      Up to 40 characters. Type suffixes: % (Integer), ! (Single), # (Double), $ (String).
  - Spectrum Rejection:  Spectrum-specific commands (BEEP, BORDER, INK, PAPER, FLASH, BRIGHT, PLOT, DRAW,
                         CIRCLE) are strictly rejected with 'Syntax error (Spectrum keyword not valid in CP/M)'.

7.7.2 Program Line Management Commands
  - AUTO [start][,inc]   Automatically generates line numbers on each Return (default: 10, 10).
  - DELETE [start][-end] Deletes specified line range (e.g. DELETE 50, DELETE 100-200, DELETE -150).
  - EDIT [linenum]       Loads line into input buffer for editing.
  - RENUM [new][,[old][,inc]] Renumbers program lines and updates all GOTO, GOSUB, THEN, ELSE targets.
  - LIST [range]         Lists program lines (e.g. LIST, LIST 100, LIST 10-50, LIST -100).
  - LLIST [range]        Lists program lines to simulated printer device.
  - NEW                  Clears all program lines, variables, and arrays from memory.
  - RUN [linenum]        Clears variables and starts program execution from line number (or first line).
  - CONT                 Continues program execution after a STOP statement or break.
  - SYSTEM               Exits Mallard-80 BASIC and returns directly to the CP/M command prompt (A>).
  - TRON / TROFF         Enables/disables execution line tracing (displays [line] during execution).

7.7.3 Structured Control Flow Statements
  - FOR var = init TO limit [STEP inc] ... NEXT [var]
                         Standard loops with positive/negative fractional step support and multi-variable NEXT.
  - WHILE expr ... WEND  Repeats enclosed statements while expression evaluates to non-zero (true).
  - IF expr THEN stmt [ELSE stmt] / IF expr GOTO line [ELSE stmt]
                         Full conditional branching with nested IF and ELSE clause support.
  - GOTO line            Unconditional jump to specified line number.
  - GOSUB line ... RETURN Subroutine invocation and return via call stack.
