  - ON n GOTO l1, l2...  Computed branch jumping to the nth line number.
  - ON n GOSUB l1, l2... Computed subroutine call to the nth line number.
  - ON ERROR GOTO line   Installs runtime error trap handler.
  - RESUME [line|NEXT]   Resumes execution after error trap handling.
  - STOP                 Halts execution and reports 'Break in line X'. Allows resumption via CONT.
  - END                  Terminates program execution cleanly without break message.

7.7.4 Variables, Arrays & Data Typing Statements
  - DEFINT letter-range  Defines default variable type as Integer (e.g. DEFINT I-N).
  - DEFSNG letter-range  Defines default variable type as Single precision (e.g. DEFSNG A-H).
  - DEFDBL letter-range  Defines default variable type as Double precision (e.g. DEFDBL D, X-Z).
  - DEFSTR letter-range  Defines default variable type as String (e.g. DEFSTR S).
  - DEF FN name(args) = expr User-defined single-line mathematical or string function.
  - DIM var(dim1[,dim2...]) Allocates multi-dimensional numeric or string arrays.
  - ERASE var[,var...]   Deallocates arrays to reclaim memory.
  - CLEAR                Resets all numeric and string variables and arrays to zero/empty.
  - SWAP var1, var2      Exchanges the values of two variables of identical type.
  - RANDOMIZE [seed]     Re-seeds the pseudo-random number generator.
  - DATA item1, item2... Embeds constant data items within program code.
  - READ var1, var2...   Reads sequential data items from DATA statements into variables.
  - RESTORE [line]       Resets DATA pointer to beginning (or specified line).

7.7.5 Console & Printer Formatting Statements
  - PRINT [items]        Outputs expressions to the 51x24 CP/M display. Semicolon (;) suppresses newline,
                         comma (,) tabs to 14-character column intervals.
  - PRINT USING fmt; items Formats numeric and string expressions according to formatting mask:
                         # (digit position), . (decimal point), , (comma thousands separators),
                         + / - (leading/trailing signs), $$ (floating dollar), ** (asterisk fill),
                         **$ (combined asterisk fill and dollar), ^^^^ (exponential scientific notation),
                         ! (first character of string), \  \ (fixed-width string), & (variable-length string).
  - LPRINT [USING] ...   Directs formatted text output to simulated CP/M printer stream.
  - WRITE [items]        Outputs expressions separated by commas with strings enclosed in quotation marks.
  - INPUT ["prompt";] var[,var...] Prompts user for console input.
  - LINE INPUT ["prompt";] var$ Reads an entire line of text including commas into a string variable.
  - TAB(n) / SPC(n)      Moves print cursor to column n, or prints n blank spaces.
  - POS(0)               Returns current column position of the cursor (0 to 50).

7.7.6 Sequential File Operations
  - OPEN mode, #stream, filespec (e.g. OPEN "O", #1, "LOG.TXT", OPEN "I", #1, "DATA.DAT")
                         Modes: 'O' (Output), 'I' (Input), 'A' (Append), 'R' (Random).
  - CLOSE [#stream[,#stream...]] Flushes buffers and closes specified open file streams.
  - PRINT #stream, items Writes formatted text to sequential file stream.
  - WRITE #stream, items Writes comma-delimited quoted values to sequential file stream.
  - INPUT #stream, vars  Reads comma-delimited fields from sequential file stream.
  - LINE INPUT #stream, v$ Reads an entire line of text from sequential file stream.
  - EOF(stream)          Returns -1 (True) if end of file reached, 0 otherwise.
  - LOF(stream)          Returns total length of open file in bytes.
  - LOC(stream)          Returns current byte offset or record position within open file.

7.7.7 Random-Access Disk Files & Binary Packing
  - OPEN "R", #stream, filespec [,rec_len] Opens random-access file (default record length 128 bytes).
  - FIELD #stream, len AS var$ [, len AS var$ ...] Allocates variable buffers within record buffer.
  - LSET var$ = expr     Left-justifies string into allocated FIELD buffer.
  - RSET var$ = expr     Right-justifies string into allocated FIELD buffer.
  - PUT #stream [,rec]   Writes record buffer to specified record number on disk.
  - GET #stream [,rec]   Reads record number from disk into record buffer.
  - MKI$(integer)        Converts 16-bit signed integer to 2-byte binary string.
  - CVI(string)          Converts 2-byte binary string back to 16-bit signed integer.
  - MKS$(single)         Converts 32-bit single-precision float to 4-byte binary string.
  - CVS(string)          Converts 4-byte binary string back to 32-bit single-precision float.
  - MKD$(double)         Converts 64-bit double-precision float to 8-byte binary string.
  - CVD(string)          Converts 8-byte binary string back to 64-bit double-precision float.

7.7.8 File & Disk Utility Statements
  - SAVE filespec        Saves active program to specified CP/M drive and filename.
  - LOAD filespec [,R]   Loads program from disk (and optionally runs immediately).
  - MERGE filespec       Merges disk program lines into active program in memory.
  - CHAIN [MERGE] filespec [,line] Passes control to another program, preserving variables.
  - COMMON var[,var...]  Declares variables to be retained across CHAIN operations.
  - KILL filespec        Deletes specified file from disk.
  - NAME old AS new      Renames file on disk.
  - FILES [filespec]     Displays directory listing of files on specified or active drive.
  - RESET                Closes all open disk files and resets drive directory buffers.

7.7.9 Memory & Hardware Access
  - PEEK(addr)           Reads byte (0-255) from virtual CP/M 64K memory.
  - POKE addr, byte      Writes byte (0-255) into virtual CP/M 64K memory.
  - INP(port)            Reads hardware byte from I/O port.
  - OUT port, byte       Writes hardware byte to I/O port.
  - WAIT port, mask[,xor] Suspends execution until port bits match mask condition.
  - VARPTR(var)          Returns memory address of variable storage.
  - DEF USR[n] = addr    Sets entry address for user machine-code subroutine n.
  - USR[n](arg)          Invokes user machine-code routine with argument.

7.7.10 Mathematical & Trigonometric Functions
  - ABS(x)               Absolute value |x|.
  - SGN(x)               Sign function: 1 (positive), 0 (zero), -1 (negative).
  - INT(x)               Greatest integer less than or equal to x (floor).
  - FIX(x)               Truncates decimal part towards zero (trunc).
  - SQR(x)               Square root of positive number.
  - SIN(x), COS(x), TAN(x) Standard trigonometric functions in radians.
  - ATN(x)               Arctangent function returning radians.
  - EXP(x)               Natural exponential function e^x.
  - LOG(x)               Natural logarithm ln(x).
  - RND[(x)]             Pseudo-random float between 0.0 and 1.0.
  - CINT(x)              Converts expression to integer.
  - CSNG(x)              Converts expression to single-precision float.
  - CDBL(x)              Converts expression to double-precision float.

7.7.11 String Functions & Statements
  - LEN(s$)              Returns length of string in characters.
  - LEFT$(s$, n)         Returns leftmost n characters of string.
  - RIGHT$(s$, n)        Returns rightmost n characters of string.
  - MID$(s$, p[, n])     Returns substring starting at position p of length n.
  - MID$(v$, p[, n]) = expr Substring replacement statement modifying characters within string variable.
  - INSTR([start,] s$, sub$) Returns 1-based index of substring within string (0 if not found).
  - STRING$(n, ch)       Creates string of n repeated characters (specified by char or ASCII code).
  - SPACE$(n)            Creates string of n space characters.
  - CHR$(code)           Returns ASCII character string corresponding to byte code.
  - ASC(s$)              Returns ASCII code of first character of string.
  - STR$(n)              Converts numeric expression to string representation (with leading space for >= 0).
  - VAL(s$)              Parses numeric value from string representation.
  - HEX$(n)              Returns hexadecimal string representation of integer.
  - OCT$(n)              Returns octal string representation of integer.

7.7.12 Arithmetic, Relational & Logical Operators
  - Arithmetic:          + (addition), - (subtraction), * (multiplication), / (floating division),
                         \\ (integer division), ^ (exponentiation), MOD (modulus remainder).
  - Relational:          = (equal), <> (not equal), < (less), > (greater), <= (less/equal), >= (greater/equal).
  - Bitwise / Logical:   NOT (bitwise/logical negation), AND (conjunction), OR (disjunction),
                         XOR (exclusive OR), EQV (logical equivalence), IMP (logical implication).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 8. SECTION 7: SUPPORTED FILE FORMATS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Target Extension:    Host System & Container Formats
Compilation Support: [UNIVERSAL HARDWARE / EMULATOR COMPATIBLE]
                     (Covers standardized file containers (.TAP, .SCR, .MDR, .DSK, .ZXF, .ZXB)
                     supported across BasinPy, real Sinclair hardware, and standard emulators).

BasinPy Extended provides native reading, writing, and parsing for all standard
Sinclair and modern retro-computing file formats:

8.1 .ZXB - Basin BASIC ASCII Source
-----------------------------------
Format: Standard UTF-8 / ASCII text file containing numbered Sinclair BASIC statements.
Usage:  Basin's native source format, allowing easy editing in external text editors.

8.2 .TAP - Sinclair ZX Tape Container
-------------------------------------
Format: Binary tape block sequence consisting of 2-byte block length headers followed
        by Sinclair ROM tape blocks (flag byte, header/data payload, parity checksum).
Usage:  Full emulation of standard Spectrum tape loading, including pilot tone and
        flashing border simulation.

8.3 .SCR / _SCR.ZXBCODE - 6912-byte Screen Dump
-----------------------------------------------
Format: Exactly 6912 raw bytes representing a complete ZX Spectrum screen:
        - 6144 bytes: Bitmap pixel data organized in authentic non-linear Spectrum screen layout
                      (3 thirds of 2048 bytes; scanlines interleaved by 256 bytes).
        - 768 bytes:  Color attribute matrix (32 columns x 24 rows).
Usage:  Directly loadable via LOAD "" SCREEN$ or SAVE "" SCREEN$.

8.4 .ZXBCODE - Compiled Binary Machine Code
-------------------------------------------
Format: Raw Z80 binary executable blocks.
Usage:  Loaded into memory via LOAD "" CODE address and executed via USR.

8.5 .DZXDATA - Beta BASIC Data Array Storage
--------------------------------------------
Format: Serialized numeric and string array data blocks.
Usage:  Fast saving and loading of multi-dimensional arrays without program code.

8.6 .MDR - Sinclair Microdrive Cartridge Image
----------------------------------------------
Format: Complete 130KB-140KB raw magnetic tape loop image containing up to 254 sectors.
Usage:  Mounted via ATTACH or ADD DISK for authentic Interface 1 Microdrive operations.

8.7 .FDI - Floppy Disk Image
----------------------------
Format: Standard Beta 128 / TR-DOS floppy disk image container with track/sector geometry.
Usage:  Directly accessible via FDI Forth words and disk utility statements.

8.8 .FTH - Forth Source Screen / Block
--------------------------------------
Format: 1024-byte virtual screen blocks (16 lines of 64 characters) conforming to fig-FORTH standards.
Usage:  Loaded and edited using BLOCK, BUFFER, LOAD, LIST, and TRIAD.

8.9 .SBIN / .SERT / .NET - Serial & ZX Net Streams
--------------------------------------------------
Format: Binary packet and ASCII data streams emulating Interface 1 RS-232 and Sinclair Network.
Usage:  Input and output via stream redirection: OPEN #4, "b" / OPEN #5, "n";1.

8.10 .DSK - CP/M & Spectrum +3 Floppy Disk Image (Standard, Extended, CPCEMU)
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Format: Extended CPC DSK ('EXTENDED CPC DSK File') and Standard CPC DSK ('MV - CPCEMU')
        disk images, as well as raw 180K/720K CP/M floppy sector dumps.
Usage:  Fully supported for both READ and WRITE operations in both Sinclair ZX Spectrum +3
        mode and CP/M mode.
        - Sinclair +3 Mode: Attach with ATTACH "filename.dsk", 1, DSK or ADD DISK "filename.dsk", A.
          Files saved via SAVE "a:filename" [LINE n / SCREEN$ / CODE addr, len] automatically
          receive an authentic 128-byte +3DOS header (b'PLUS3DOS\x1A', issue 1, version 0, payload
          length, autostart line or machine code start address, and checksum).
          Files are loaded, merged, verified, copied, renamed, and erased seamlessly across .dsk files.
        - CP/M Mode: Mount with ADD DISK "filename.dsk", Drive (A or B), or direct LOAD "filename.dsk".
          BasinPy's CPMDiskImage engine auto-detects track/sector parameters, block sizes, and directory
          blocks. All CP/M utilities (DIR, TYPE, ERA, REN, PIP), native .COM binaries, and Locomotive
          Mallard BASIC operate directly on .dsk files with instant persistence to the host disk image.
        - Interoperability: Disks written in Spectrum +3 mode can be read in CP/M mode, and vice versa.

8.11 .IMD - Dave Dunfield ImageDisk CP/M Disk Image (All Densities & Track Formats)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Format: Authentic Dave Dunfield ImageDisk format (.imd) featuring ASCII comment banner
        terminated by 0x1A, track records with transfer mode/cylinder/head/sector count,
        physical sector numbering map, and compressed (0x02) or uncompressed (0x01) sector data.
Usage:  Mountable in CP/M mode via ADD DISK "filename.imd", Drive (A or B), MOUNT, or direct
        LOAD "filename.imd". BasinPy provides full read and write persistence for ImageDisk files,
        auto-detecting geometry from track headers and compressing empty 0xE5 sectors into 2-byte
        run-length records when saving to disk.

8.12 .IMG / .CPM / .RAW / .FLP - Raw Sector Multi-Format CP/M Floppy Disk Images
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Format: Direct uncompressed sector image dumps (.img, .cpm, .raw, .flp) representing sequential
        track and sector payloads without container headers.
Usage:  Mountable in CP/M mode via ADD DISK "filename.ext", Drive (A or B), MOUNT, or direct
        LOAD "filename.ext". BasinPy's CPMDiskImage engine incorporates automated geometry heuristics
        detecting Sinclair +3 (180K/720K), Amstrad CPC/PCW (360K), IBM 3740 standard 8" SSSD (with 6:1
        sector skew), Kaypro II/4, Osborne 1 SSSD/SSDD, Apple II SoftCard, Commodore 128 (1571/1581),
        and PC 720K/1.44M disk images. Full read and write persistence: changes to files, allocation
        blocks, and directory entries are directly flushed and written back to the host image file.

8.13 .ZXF - Sinclair Forth Native Compiled Container
----------------------------------------------------
Format: Native binary container consisting of a 16-byte metadata header followed by compiled Z80 machine code:
        - Bytes 0..3:   Magic identifier 'ZXF\x01' (Sinclair Forth Native Binary, version 1)
        - Bytes 4..5:   Load/origin memory address (16-bit unsigned little-endian, default 49152 / 0xC000)
        - Bytes 6..7:   Entry point execution address (16-bit unsigned little-endian)
        - Bytes 8..9:   Code payload byte length (16-bit unsigned little-endian)
        - Bytes 10..15: Reserved (6 null bytes)
        - Offset 16+:   Compiled native Z80 machine code payload directly executable via RANDOMIZE USR.
Usage:  Created and saved using SAVE "filename", FORTH or SAVE "filename.zxf".
        Loaded into Spectrum RAM using LOAD "filename", FORTH or LOAD "filename.zxf".
        Cataloged and inspected automatically by DIR and CAT with origin address and code size.

8.14 .COM - Native CP/M 8080/Z80 Binary Executable Program
----------------------------------------------------------
Format: Standard Digital Research CP/M executable binary consisting of pure 8080 or Z80 machine
        code assembled for origin address 0x0100 (100H).
Usage:  Directly executable within BasinPy's native CP/M environment:
        - Launched from the CP/M command line prompt (e.g. `A> MYPROG` or `A> MYPROG FILE.TXT`).
        - Launched directly from Sinclair BASIC via `LOAD "MYPROG.COM"` or from the host OS command
          line via `python main.py MYPROG.COM`.
        - Executes with full 64KB RAM access, 63.5KB TPA, authentic Zero Page setup (0x0000 Warm Boot,
          0x0005 BDOS, 0x005C/0x006C FCBs, 0x0080 DMA/command tail), and full BDOS system call handling.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 9. SECTION 8: IDE, DUAL-PANE EDITOR & CRT EMULATION
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Target Extension:    BasinPy Dual-Pane IDE & CRT Engine
Compilation Support: [INTERPRETER ONLY] / [IDE ENVIRONMENT]
                     (The dual-pane editor, interactive click-to-edit, compliance analyzer,
                     and tape simulation are native features of the BasinPy IDE workstation).

BasinPy Extended is engineered to offer a seamless bridge between a 1980s 8-bit
computer experience and modern IDE productivity.

9.1 Dual-Pane vs CRT Layout
---------------------------
The workspace is divided into two synchronized real-time views:
  - LEFT PANEL:  Syntax-highlighted BASIC program listing with line numbers,
                 procedure folding, active trace indicator, and scrollable listing.
  - RIGHT PANEL: 320x256 simulated ZX Spectrum CRT display featuring authentic
                 ULA graphic border, 256x192 paper surface, and command prompt.

9.2 Interactive Click-to-Edit & In-Listing Editing
--------------------------------------------------
Clicking any line in the left listing instantly brings that line down into the
lower prompt line for immediate in-place editing, just like classic Basin on Windows.
Pressing ENTER updates the resident program in memory and refreshes the listing.

9.3 CRT Scaling, Aspect Ratio & Fullscreen Mode
-----------------------------------------------
BasinPy maintains an authentic 1.25 (5:4) aspect ratio with crisp integer or nearest-neighbor
scaling. Pressing F11 or executing RUN/FS engages true fullscreen CRT mode, perfect for
retro-gaming and distraction-free programming sessions.

9.4 Syntax Highlighter & Multi-Tier Command Compliance Engine
-------------------------------------------------------------
The listing area features an authentic, real-time syntax highlighter that classifies
and visually highlights code tokens across eight distinct compliance categories:
  1. 48K BASIC         (Royal Blue: RGB 0, 50, 200)      - Standard Sinclair ZX Spectrum 48K keywords
  2. 128K Extra        (Deep Magenta: RGB 170, 0, 150)   - 128K/+2/+3 hardware commands (PLAY, SOUND, BANK, PAGE)
  3. Beta BASIC 3.0    (Forest Green: RGB 0, 130, 40)    - Structured procedural extensions (PROC, BOX, FILL, WINDOW)
  4. Beta BASIC 4.0    (Burnt Orange: RGB 200, 80, 0)    - Algorithms & RAM arrays (SORT, SHUFFLE, PATTERN, DEF KEY, DIM !)
  5. Boriel's ZX Basic (Royal Purple: RGB 120, 50, 200)  - Modern compiled extensions (SUB, FUNCTION, CONST, CAST, SHL, SHR, etc.)
  6. Timex TS2068      (Deep Cyan: RGB 0, 150, 175)      - Timex 2068 hardware, Silver Sonic AY sound, and LKDOS disk commands
  7. Peripherals       (Teal / Cyan: RGB 0, 135, 140)    - Dedicated hardware (JOYSTICK, MOUSE, DRUM, SPECDRUM, SAY, FORTH)
  8. System Commands   (Crimson / Ruby: RGB 175, 20, 45) - BasinPy controls (CPM, SPEC, ATTACH, ADD DISK, COMPILE, TRON, TROFF)
Non-keyword elements are styled for maximum readability on the listing paper:
  - Line Numbers:      Slate Gray (RGB 100, 110, 130)
  - String Literals:   Dark Wine (RGB 130, 20, 20)
  - REM Comments:      Olive Green (RGB 70, 115, 60)
  - Identifiers:       Crisp Black (RGB 20, 20, 25)

Listing Header Compliance Legend Bar:
A visual legend bar is rendered across the top of the program listing:
    [48K]  [128K]  [BB3]  [BB4]  [BORIEL]  [TS2068]  [PERIPH]  [SYSTEM]
Each badge is rendered in its corresponding compliance color for instant visual reference.

Live Highlighting During In-Listing Editing:
When editing a line directly in the listing area, syntax highlighting updates dynamically
in real time on every keystroke, with precise cursor alignment.

9.5 Program Compliance Analysis: COMPLIANCE & SYNTAX Commands
-------------------------------------------------------------
The COMPLIANCE (or SYNTAX) command scans resident program lines and evaluates keyword
usage across all eight compliance tiers. It prints an immediate breakdown to the Spectrum
canvas, detailing keyword counts and the minimum target hardware/software environment
required to run the program (e.g. Pure Sinclair 48K, 128K Spectrum, Beta BASIC 3.0,
Beta BASIC 4.0, Boriel's ZX Basic (ZXBC), Timex Sinclair 2068 (TS2068 / TC2068),
Spectrum + Peripherals, or BasinPy Custom Environment).
Syntax: COMPLIANCE [line_number]   or   SYNTAX [line_number]

9.6 Authentic Tape Loading Border & Audio Feedback
--------------------------------------------------
When loading .TAP tape files:
  - The outer border simulates classic Sinclair tape loading stripes:
      * Red / Cyan stripes during pilot tone header search
      * Yellow / Blue alternating stripes during data block transmission
  - Real-time authentic audio square waves reproduce the nostalgic screech and whine

9.7 Program Compilation & Tape Container Generation: COMPILE Command
-------------------------------------------------------------------
The COMPILE command compiles or packages a BASIC source program into a standard Sinclair
ZX Spectrum tape container (.TAP):
Syntax:
  - COMPILE                  : Compiles the currently resident program in memory to program.tap
  - COMPILE "program.zxb"    : Loads and compiles specified .zxb file to program.tap
  - COMPILE "src" TO "out.tap": Compiles source program to target .tap tape file

Compilation Pipeline:
  1. Pure Sinclair BASIC Programs:
     If the program uses pure Sinclair BASIC without Beta BASIC extensions, COMPILE invokes
     Boriel's ZX BASIC Compiler (zxbc) to compile directly into Z80 machine code with an
     automatic Sinclair BASIC loader block (CLEAR, LOAD "" CODE, RANDOMIZE USR).
  2. Extended 128K Beta BASIC Programs (e.g. TASWORD2.ZXB):
     For programs using Beta BASIC 3.0/4.0 extensions (such as DEF PROC, CSIZE, UDG 16, BOLD,
     ITALIC, UNDERLINE, DEF KEY, DIM !), COMPILE serializes the program into a conforming
     Sinclair ZX Spectrum tape container (.TAP) with 2-byte block length headers, standard ROM
     flag bytes (0x00 header, 0xFF data), parity checksums, and authentic loading simulation.
  3. Output Synchronization:
     Compiled .TAP tape files are simultaneously written to the current working directory
     and the Sinclair storage repository (~/Documents/ZXBASIC/).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
10. SECTION 9: BORIEL'S ZX BASIC (ZXBC) COMPILER & LANGUAGE EXTENSIONS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Target Extension:    Boriel's ZX Basic (ZXBC) Compiler & Language Extensions
Compilation Support: [BORIEL ZXBC COMPATIBLE] / [NATIVE Z80 MACHINE CODE]
                     (Full native compilation to high-speed Z80 machine code binaries and
                     self-contained autostarting .TAP tape containers via the resident zxbc
                     compiler toolchain. All structured subroutines, types, inline assembly,
                     and bitwise expressions are fully compiled).

BasinPy includes comprehensive support for Boriel's ZX Basic (ZXBC), the modern,
optimizing compiler for the Sinclair ZX Spectrum. BasinPy supports Boriel syntax
highlighting, direct interpreter execution of Boriel constructs and functions,
and seamless native compilation of Sinclair and Boriel BASIC programs directly
into standalone machine code tape containers (.TAP) equipped with autostart loaders.

10.1 Structured Subroutines & Functions: SUB, FUNCTION, END SUB, END FUNCTION
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------
Boriel's ZX Basic introduces structured procedure and function definitions with
explicit parameters, return types, and local variable scoping:

  - SUB name([param [AS type], ...]) [FASTCALL | STDCALL]
    Defines a subroutine procedure.
    Can be invoked with or without call syntax:
      MySub(arg1, arg2)
      MySub arg1, arg2
      CALL MySub(arg1, arg2)
    Exited via END SUB or an early RETURN.

  - FUNCTION name([param [AS type], ...]) [AS type] [FASTCALL | STDCALL]
    Defines a function returning a typed value.
    Returns a value to the caller via:
      RETURN expression
    Terminated by END FUNCTION.
    Can be used directly in any arithmetic, logical, or string expression:
      PRINT "Hypotenuse: "; CalcHypot(3, 4)

10.2 Parameter Passing & Calling Conventions: BYVAL, BYREF, FASTCALL, STDCALL
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------
Boriel subroutines and functions support fine-grained parameter semantics:
  - BYVAL: Passes argument by value (default for numeric and scalar types).
  - BYREF: Passes argument by reference, allowing the subroutine to mutate
           the caller's variable in place.
  - FASTCALL: Boriel compiler optimization passing the first parameter in
              Z80 CPU registers (A, HL, or DE) for ultra-fast invocation.
  - STDCALL: Standard caller/callee stack frame convention.
BasinPy's interpreter transparently accepts and honors these annotations.

10.3 Static Variable Typing & Memory Storage: DIM var AS type, CONST
--------------------------------------------------------------------
Boriel Basic supports explicit static data typing for high performance and low
memory footprints:
  - Supported Data Types:
      * BYTE / UBYTE    : 8-bit signed (-128..127) / unsigned (0..255) integer (1 byte)
      * INTEGER / UINTEGER : 16-bit signed (-32768..32767) / unsigned (0..65535) (2 bytes)
      * LONG / ULONG    : 32-bit signed / unsigned integer (4 bytes)
      * FIXED           : 32-bit fixed point (16.16) (4 bytes)
      * FLOAT           : Standard 40-bit ZX Spectrum floating point format (5 bytes)
      * STRING          : Variable-length dynamic string
  - Static Variable Declaration:
      DIM count AS UBYTE = 0
      DIM total AS UINTEGER = 1000
      DIM name AS STRING = "Spectrum"
      DIM scores(10) AS INTEGER
  - Compile-Time Constants:
      CONST MAX_PLAYERS = 4
      CONST SCREEN_WIDTH = 256
      CONST GREETING$ = "Welcome to BasinPy"

10.4 Type Casting & Memory Introspection: CAST, SIZEOF, LBOUND, UBOUND
----------------------------------------------------------------------
  - CAST(type, value):
    Explicitly converts and casts an expression to a target Boriel type:
      x = CAST(UBYTE, 300)      --> Evaluates to 44 (300 % 256)
      y = CAST(INTEGER, 3.1415) --> Evaluates to 3
  - SIZEOF(type | var):
    Returns the memory footprint in bytes of a data type or variable:
      SIZEOF(BYTE)      --> 1
      SIZEOF(INTEGER)   --> 2
      SIZEOF(LONG)      --> 4
      SIZEOF(FLOAT)     --> 5
  - LBOUND(array [, dim]) / UBOUND(array [, dim]):
    Returns the lower bound (0 or 1) and upper bound of an array dimension:
      DIM arr(20) AS BYTE
      PRINT LBOUND(arr), UBOUND(arr)  --> 0, 20

10.5 Bitwise Operators & Binary Expressions: SHL, SHR, XOR
----------------------------------------------------------
Boriel Basic provides hardware-level bit manipulation operators:
  - a SHL n : Shift bits left by n positions (equivalent to a * 2^n)
  - a SHR n : Shift bits right by n positions (equivalent to a // 2^n)
  - a XOR b : Bitwise exclusive OR between two integer expressions
Examples:
  mask = 1 SHL 3        --> 8
  val = 255 XOR 15      --> 240
  high_nibble = x SHR 4

10.6 Extended Structured Control Flow: ELSEIF, DO WHILE / UNTIL, LOOP WHILE / UNTIL
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Boriel Basic expands control flow with structured multi-branching and loop conditions:
  - ELSEIF condition THEN:
      IF x = 1 THEN
        PRINT "One"
      ELSEIF x = 2 THEN
        PRINT "Two"
      ELSE
        PRINT "Other"
      END IF
  - DO WHILE / UNTIL ... LOOP:
      DO WHILE count < 10
        count = count + 1
      LOOP
  - DO ... LOOP WHILE / UNTIL:
      DO
        k$ = INKEY$
      LOOP UNTIL k$ <> ""

10.7 Inline Assembly & Declarations: ASM ... END ASM, DECLARE
-------------------------------------------------------------
For maximum speed, Boriel Basic allows mixing Z80 machine code assembly with BASIC:
  - ASM ... END ASM:
      ASM
        ld a, 2
        out (254), a
      END ASM
    In BasinPy's interpreter, inline assembly blocks are cleanly recognized and
    bypassed during interpreted runs, while being fully preserved for compilation.
  - DECLARE SUB / FUNCTION:
    Forward declaration of procedures and functions for modular source files:
      DECLARE SUB DrawSprite(x AS UBYTE, y AS UBYTE)

10.8 Direct Machine Code Compilation to Tape (.TAP) with Autoloader: zxbc Pipeline
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy seamlessly links to Boriel's ZX Basic Compiler (zxbc) for compiling source
programs into production-ready Sinclair ZX Spectrum tape containers:
  - Automated Toolchain Detection:
    Automatically locates `zxbc.exe` on Windows (via PATH or Python Scripts folder).
  - Compilation Syntax:
    COMPILE                     : Compiles resident program to program.tap
    COMPILE "game.zxb"          : Compiles source file to game.tap
    COMPILE "game.zxb" TO "out.tap": Compiles source file to custom target tap
  - Machine Code Container Architecture:
    The compiler automatically outputs a complete two-block Sinclair tape file:
      1. Sinclair BASIC Autostart Loader Block (Header + Program Data):
         Contains a self-starting Sinclair BASIC loader (e.g. 10 CLEAR 24575:
         LOAD "" CODE: RANDOMIZE USR 24576) with autostart line set.
      2. Z80 Machine Code Binary Block (Header + Code Data):
         Contains the compiled Z80 machine code executable with target load address.
  - Sinclair TAP Detokenizer:
    BasinPy features a built-in Sinclair token detokenizer. Loading any compiled
    tape file back into BasinPy via LOAD "game.tap" immediately reconstitutes
    and displays the Sinclair BASIC autoloader in the listing panel!



--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 11. SECTION 10: SINCLAIR Z80 CPU HARDWARE EMULATION & MACHINE CODE ENGINE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Target Extension:    Sinclair Z80 CPU Hardware Emulation & Machine Code Engine
Compilation Support: [NATIVE Z80 MACHINE CODE]
                     (Executes cycle-accurate native Z80 machine code binaries directly in
                     Spectrum 64KB RAM via USR calls, with full ROM call interception,
                     VRAM bitmapping, port I/O, and interrupt simulation).

BasinPy Extended incorporates an authentic, high-performance Sinclair Z80 CPU
emulation core (SinclairZ80CPU) capable of executing native Z80 machine code
programs, commercial ZX Spectrum tape images, and compiled Boriel ZXB binary
executables with cycle-accurate timing and seamless hardware integration.

11.1 SinclairZ80CPU Hardware Architecture & Register Set
--------------------------------------------------------
The CPU emulator implements the complete Zilog Z80 microprocessor specification:
  - 8-Bit Primary Registers:
      * A (Accumulator): Arithmetic and logical accumulator (8 bits).
      * F (Flags): S (Sign), Z (Zero), Y (Bit 5), H (Half-Carry), X (Bit 3),
        P/V (Parity/Overflow), N (Add/Subtract), C (Carry).
      * B, C, D, E, H, L: General-purpose 8-bit registers.
  - Alternate (Shadow) Register Set:
      * A', F', B', C', D', E', H', L': Swapped via EX AF, AF' and EXX instructions.
  - 16-Bit Register Pairs:
      * BC, DE, HL, AF: Composed of 8-bit pairs with direct 16-bit math and addressing.
  - Dedicated 16-Bit Index Registers:
      * IX, IY: 16-bit index pointers supporting displacement addressing (IX+d, IY+d).
  - System Pointers & Registers:
      * SP: 16-bit Stack Pointer (initialized to 0xFFFE).
      * PC: 16-bit Program Counter tracking instruction execution.
      * I: Interrupt vector page register.
      * R: Memory refresh counter.
  - Interrupt Control:
      * IFF1, IFF2: Interrupt enable flip-flops (toggled by EI and DI instructions).
      * IM 0, IM 1, IM 2: Interrupt modes (defaults to Spectrum standard IM 1).
  - Complete Instruction Set Coverage:
      * Primary Opcode Map (0x00..0xFF): NOP, LD, INC, DEC, ADD, ADC, SUB, SBC,
        AND, XOR, OR, CP, JR, JP, CALL, RET, RST, PUSH, POP, IN, OUT, HALT, etc.
      * CB-Prefix Bitwise Instructions: RLC, RRC, RL, RR, SLA, SRA, SLL, SRL,
        BIT b, r, RES b, r, and SET b, r across all registers and (HL).
      * ED-Prefix Extended Instructions: 16-bit ADC/SBC, IM 0/1/2, LD I/R, NEG,
        RETI, RETN, block transfers (LDIR, LDDR, CPIR, CPDR), and block I/O.
      * DD/FD-Prefix Indexed Instructions: Complete IX and IY displacement operations,
        including indexed ALU, indexed bit manipulations (DDCB / FDCB), and pushes.

11.2 Machine Code Execution: USR Function & RANDOMIZE USR Command
----------------------------------------------------------------
Machine code subroutines are invoked from Sinclair BASIC using the USR function:
  - Command Syntax:
      RANDOMIZE USR address
      LET return_val = USR address
  - Execution Mechanics:
      1. BasinPy pushes the current return address onto the Z80 machine stack (SP).
      2. The CPU's Program Counter (PC) is loaded with the target entry address.
      3. SinclairZ80CPU executes machine code instructions at full speed.
      4. When the routine completes with a Z80 RET instruction (opcode 0xC9),
         execution returns seamlessly to the BASIC interpreter.
      5. The 16-bit value in the BC register pair is returned as the numeric
         result of the USR function.
  - Examples:
      10 CLEAR 30000 : REM Protect memory above 30000
      20 LOAD "code.bin" CODE 32768
      30 RANDOMIZE USR 32768 : REM Execute machine code game

11.3 Spectrum 48K ROM Call Interception & Hardware Call Bridges
---------------------------------------------------------------
Commercial Sinclair ZX Spectrum software and compiled programs frequently call
into standard ROM routines in the 0x0000..0x3FFF address space. BasinPy provides
automatic, hardware-faithful interception for all core Spectrum 48K ROM entry points:
  - RST 0 (0x0000): START / Reset - Reinitializes system variables and resets CPU.
  - RST 8 (0x0008): ERROR-1 - Dispatches runtime and syntax errors with error code.
  - RST 16 (0x0010): PRINT-A-1 - Prints the character in register A to current stream.
  - RST 24 (0x0018): GET-CHAR - Reads character from CH_ADD, automatically skipping spaces.
  - RST 32 (0x0020): NEXT-CHAR - Advances CH_ADD pointer by 1 and scans next character.
  - RST 40 (0x0028 & 0x335B): FP-CALCULATOR - Comprehensive bridge to the Sinclair
    Floating-Point Calculator, interpreting calculation bytecodes with full stack sync.
  - RST 48 (0x0030): BC-ADD - Computes 16-bit addition DE = HL + BC.
  - RST 56 (0x0038): MASK-INT - 50Hz frame interrupt counter updating FRAMES (23672).
  - 0x028E / 0x02BF: KEY-SCAN - Scans hardware Port 0xFE keyboard matrix and updates LAST-K.
  - 0x03B5: BEEPER - Synthesizes square wave tones with pitch in HL and duration in DE.
  - 0x0D6B: CLS - Clears screen memory (0x4000..0x57FF) and resets attributes to ATTR-P.
  - 0x22AA: PLOT - Plots high-resolution pixel at coordinate (C, B) with attribute check.
  - 0x24B7: DRAW - Draws relative line or arc with coordinate offsets in (E, D).

11.4 Port 0xFE I/O Keyboard Matrix & Audio/Border Emulation
-----------------------------------------------------------
Sinclair hardware I/O port 0xFE is fully modeled for both input and output:
  - Port 0xFE Keyboard Matrix Read (IN A, (0xFE)):
      Address lines A8..A15 select the 8 physical half-rows of the Spectrum keyboard:
        * 0xFEFE: SHIFT, Z, X, C, V       0xEFFE: 0, 9, 8, 7, 6
        * 0xFDFE: A, S, D, F, G           0xDFFE: P, O, I, U, Y
        * 0xFBFE: Q, W, E, R, T           0xBFFE: ENTER, L, K, J, H
        * 0xF7FE: 1, 2, 3, 4, 5           0x7FFE: SPACE, SYM, M, N, B
      Keys pressed return active-low 0 bits on data bus lines D0..D4.
  - Port 0xFE Output (OUT (0xFE), A):
      * Bits 0..2: Border color (0 to 7), instantly updating CRT border display.
      * Bit 3: MIC socket audio output.
      * Bit 4: EAR speaker line driving 1-bit beeper sound synthesis.

11.5 Memory-Mapped Video RAM & Screen Architecture (0x4000..0x5AFF)
-------------------------------------------------------------------
SinclairZ80CPU writes directly to the Sinclair ZX Spectrum physical display memory:
  - Bitmap Graphics RAM (0x4000..0x57FF, 6,144 bytes):
      Organized in classic Sinclair interleaved scanlines:
        * 3 vertical thirds (2,048 bytes each: Top, Middle, Bottom)
        * 8 character rows per third
        * 8 scanlines per character row
        * 32 columns per scanline (256 horizontal pixels)
      Writes to VRAM instantly reflect on the real-time CRT canvas.
  - Attribute RAM (0x5800..0x5AFF, 768 bytes):
      One attribute byte per 8x8 character cell:
        * Bits 0..2: INK color (0 to 7)
        * Bits 3..5: PAPER color (0 to 7)
        * Bit 6: BRIGHT flag (high-intensity luminance)
        * Bit 7: FLASH flag (alternating ink/paper)

11.6 Cycle-Accurate Execution & 50Hz Frame Synchronization
----------------------------------------------------------
In real ZX Spectrum hardware, a 50Hz maskable interrupt occurs every 20ms (every
69,888 T-states of the 3.5 MHz Z80 CPU). SinclairZ80CPU batches execution into
authentic frame intervals, increments the FRAMES system variable (23672..23674)
at 50Hz, updates keyboard inputs, and synchronizes video output with real time.

11.7 CP/M Full 64KB RAM Execution Mode, BDOS & BIOS Interception Traps
----------------------------------------------------------------------
To support authentic CP/M operating system execution, SinclairZ80CPU incorporates a
dual-mode memory architecture:
  - Standard ZX Spectrum Mode:
      * Addresses 0x0000..0x3FFF are protected against writes (ROM emulation).
      * Standard 48K ROM entry points (0x12A9, 0x1303, 0x0556, etc.) are intercepted
        for instant high-speed arithmetic, tape, and printing hooks.
  - Native CP/M Mode (cpu.cpm_mode = True / hook_cpm_trap active):
      * ROM protection across 0x0000..0x3FFF is completely disabled, granting
        unrestricted read and write access across the entire 64KB address space (0x0000..0xFFFF).
      * BDOS Vector Traps: Execution of CALL 0005H or PC at 0x0005 intercepts BDOS system calls.
      * Warm Boot Trap: Jump or call to PC 0x0000 initiates warm boot reset.
      * BIOS Jump Table Traps: Vectors at 0xFE00..0xFE33 intercept BIOS driver calls directly
        from user machine code or runtimes.
      * TPA Space: Provides 63.5KB (0x0100..0xFE00) of uninterrupted machine code space.



--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 12. SECTION 11: SINCLAIR ZEUS Z80 ASSEMBLER (Z80 / END Z80)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Target Extension:    Sinclair Zeus Z80 Assembler (Z80 / END Z80)
Compilation Support: [NATIVE Z80 MACHINE CODE] / [STANDALONE .TAP COMPILATION]
                     (Directly compiles Z80 mnemonics into native machine code binaries at specified
                     ORG addresses. Fully supports INCLUDE SPRITES and INCLUDE TILEMAP to bundle
                     hardware engines and graphic definitions into standalone autostarting .TAP containers).

BasinPy Extended includes an authentic, integrated reproduction of the famous
Crystal Computing / Sinclair ZEUS Z80 Assembler. ZEUS provides an interactive
assembly environment for writing, assembling, testing, and saving native Z80
machine code programs without leaving BasinPy.

12.1 Interactive ZEUS Assembler Environment & Commands
------------------------------------------------------
The assembler can be entered interactively from the command line or executed
inline within BASIC source files:
  - Command Syntax:
      Z80                      : Enters interactive ZEUS Assembler mode (prompt 'Z>').
      Z80 <assembly_code>      : Assembles single-line instruction directly.
      Z80 ... END Z80          : Inline block assembly within a BASIC listing.
  - Interactive Command Set:
      * A                      : Assemble resident source buffer into machine code bytes.
      * R [address]            : Run assembled code immediately via SinclairZ80CPU.
      * L [start] [end]        : List source code buffer with line numbers.
      * N                      : Clear source buffer (New assembly project).
      * D <line>               : Delete specified line from assembly source.
      * SAVE "<name>"          : Exports source to <name>.asm AND compiles a complete,
                                 conforming Sinclair .TAP tape file with a self-starting
                                 Sinclair BASIC autoloader!
      * M [address]            : Opens the Crystal Computing Z80 Machine Code Monitor at [address]
                                 (defaults to current PC or assembly origin address).
      * END Z80 / BYE          : Exits ZEUS and returns directly to BasinPy BASIC.

12.2 Assembler Directives: ADDRESS, ORG, DEFB/DB, DEFW/DW, DEFS/DS, EQU & Labels
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ZEUS supports standard Z80 assembler directives and symbolic labels:
  - ADDRESS <addr>             : Sets destination origin address in memory (e.g. ADDRESS 45000,
                                 ADDRESS #B000, ADDRESS $B000, ADDRESS &HB000, or 0xB000).
  - ORG <addr>                 : Equivalent to ADDRESS; sets target origin address in memory.
  - DEFB / DB b1, b2, ...      : Defines byte data constants or ASCII text strings.
  - DEFW / DW w1, w2, ...      : Defines 16-bit word constants in little-endian format.
  - DEFS / DS count [, fill]   : Reserves storage space of count bytes.
  - EQU value                  : Defines symbolic numeric constant (e.g. BORDER EQU 254).
  - Labels:
      Labels can be placed at the start of a line (e.g. `LOOP: LD B, 10` or `LOOP`).
      Labels can be used as targets in JP, JR, CALL, and 16-bit load instructions.

12.3 Inline Z80 Compilation on 1st Run & Execution Fast-Path Caching
--------------------------------------------------------------------
Inline Z80 blocks (`Z80 ... END Z80`) embedded directly inside Sinclair BASIC listings
feature an intelligent compilation caching engine:
  - 1st Run Compilation:
      When execution reaches an inline Z80 block for the first time, the resident Zeus
      assembler compiles the assembly statements into target memory at the address specified
      by `ADDRESS <n>` (defaulting to 32768 if omitted).
      The compiler marks the block as compiled (`compiled = True`) and stores the bytecode
      hash and destination address in the compilation cache.
      The machine code then executes directly via the high-speed SinclairZ80CPU engine as if
      `USR address` was called!
  - Subsequent Run Fast-Path:
      On subsequent executions (such as inside `FOR...NEXT` or `DO...LOOP` iterations, or
      repeated calls to the subroutine), BasinPy detects that the block has already been
      compiled and completely bypasses the assembler, immediately launching execution
      directly from the pre-assembled machine code in memory!
  - Automatic Cache Invalidation:
      The compilation cache is automatically invalidated when `NEW`, `CLEAR`, or program
      `LOAD` commands are issued, or whenever the line text is modified, ensuring code changes
      are immediately recompiled on their next run.

12.4 Z80 Decompiler (DECOMPILE address, "filename") & .z8c Opcode Listing
------------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy includes a full Z80 disassembler/decompiler that can reconstruct assembly source
listings directly from live Spectrum RAM:
  - Command Syntax:
      DECOMPILE address, "filename"
      DECOMPILE address, length, "filename"
  - File Format (.z8c):
      The decompiler automatically appends the `.z8c` extension if omitted.
      The resulting output file is a standard, plain-text Z80 opcode listing beginning with
      `ADDRESS <address>`, followed by cleanly formatted mnemonic statements:
          ADDRESS 45000
          LD A, 42
          LD BC, 1234
          ADD HL, BC
          RET
  - Instruction Coverage:
      Supports full standard Z80 instruction set decoding, including 8-bit/16-bit loads,
      ALU arithmetic/logic, conditional & relative jumps (JR, JP, DJNZ), subroutine calls
      and returns (CALL, RET, RET cc), RST restarts, bit operations (CB prefix: BIT, SET, RES,
      rotates and shifts), extended block operations (ED prefix: LDIR, CPIR, INIR, OTIR, etc.),
      and indexed register addressing (DD/FD prefixes: IX, IY, with displacements).
  - Termination:
      The decompiler automatically stops decoding upon reaching an unconditional return
      (`RET`, `RETI`, `RETN`) or unconditional jump (`JP nn`), or upon reaching the specified
      length limit (default 512 bytes).

12.5 Z80 Program Block Loader (ZLOAD "filename", line_number)
-------------------------------------------------------------
The `ZLOAD` command loads a decompiled `.z8c` plain-text opcode listing directly back into
the active Sinclair BASIC program:
  - Command Syntax:
      ZLOAD "filename", line_number
  - Automatic Block Framing:
      The `.z8c` file extension is automatically appended if omitted.
      `ZLOAD` reads the opcode listing and automatically frames the machine code with
      `Z80` at the top and `END Z80` at the bottom.
  - Program Insertion:
      Inserts the statements cleanly into the active BASIC program dictionary starting at
      `line_number` and incrementing by 10 (e.g. lines 100, 110, 120, ...).
      Invalidates the compilation cache so the newly inserted block compiles cleanly on 1st run.
  - Example Workflow:
      10 REM Capture a machine code routine from memory address 45000:
      DECOMPILE 45000, "mycode"
      20 REM Load the code into the BASIC program starting at line 500:
      ZLOAD "mycode", 500
      30 REM Run line 500 onwards to compile on 1st run and execute!
      RUN 500

12.6 Automated Tape Container (.TAP) Export with BASIC Autoloader
----------------------------------------------------------------
Executing `SAVE "myprog"` inside ZEUS automatically generates two production-ready files:
  1. `myprog.asm` - The raw ASCII assembly source text.
  2. `myprog.tap` - A dual-block Sinclair ZX Spectrum tape container containing:
       - Header + Data Block 1: Sinclair BASIC autoloader (`10 CLEAR (ORG-1): LOAD "" CODE: RANDOMIZE USR ORG`).
       - Header + Data Block 2: Compiled Z80 machine code binary block.
Loading `myprog.tap` into any real Spectrum emulator or back into BasinPy immediately
loads the code into RAM and autostarts execution!


12.7 Machine Code Sprite & Tilemap Integration: INCLUDE SPRITES & INCLUDE TILEMAP
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy provides native, zero-overhead machine code integration between the Sinclair
ZEUS Z80 Assembler and the hardware-style Sprite Engine and Tilemap Subsystem. By
adding the directives `INCLUDE SPRITES` and/or `INCLUDE TILEMAP` to an assembly source
buffer (or inline `Z80 ... END Z80` block), developers can call sprite and tile routines
directly from pure Z80 machine code without declaring manual equates or memory offsets.

Furthermore, when saving via `SAVE "filename"` in ZEUS or compiling via `COMPILE`, BasinPy
automatically bundles the relocatable machine code engines (`sp_engine` at 50000, `tl_engine`
at 64000), graphic definition data (`sp_data` at 53072), and tile matrix banks (`tilemap` at 40000)
along with a unified Sinclair BASIC autoloader into a standalone, production-ready `.TAP`
tape container that runs independently on standard 48K Sinclair ZX Spectrum hardware!

1. Directives Overview:
   - `INCLUDE SPRITES` (or `INCLUDE SPRITE`):
       Injects all standard equates for the relocatable 16-slot Sprite Engine (entry points,
       active slot table offsets, collision status table, parameter block, and definitions).
       Informs the TAP exporter to bundle `sp_engine` (3072 bytes at address 50000) and
       preloaded sprite definitions (`sp_data` at address 53072).
   - `INCLUDE TILEMAP` (or `INCLUDE TILE`):
       Injects all standard equates for the 16x16 Tile Blitter Engine (entry points,
       parameter block memory offsets, and blit flag bitmasks).
       Informs the TAP exporter to bundle `tl_engine` (414 bytes at address 64000) and
       screen tilemap matrix data (`tilemap` at address 40000).

2. Sprite Engine Memory Map & Symbolic Equates (Default Base: 50000 / #C350):
   +----------------------+---------+--------+-------------------------------------------------+
   | Symbol Equate        | Decimal | Hex    | Purpose / Calling Convention                    |
   +----------------------+---------+--------+-------------------------------------------------+
   | SP_BASE              | 50000   | #C350  | Relocatable base address of sprite engine       |
   | SP_INIT              | 50000   | #C350  | Initialize sprite engine, clear slots & buffers |
   | SP_UPDATE            | 50003   | #C353  | Update velocities, animations & collisions      |
   | SP_DRAW              | 50006   | #C356  | Restore dirty rects & draw active sprites       |
   | SP_STEP              | 50009   | #C359  | Single-frame step (CALL SP_UPDATE + SP_DRAW)    |
   | SP_SET_ATTR          | 50012   | #C35C  | Set slot attribute via SP_PARAM_BLOCK           |
   | SP_GET_ATTR          | 50015   | #C35F  | Read slot attribute into SP_PARAM_BLOCK         |
   | SP_DEF_SPRITE        | 50018   | #C362  | Register graphic definition in memory           |
   | SP_CLEAR_ALL         | 50021   | #C365  | Deactivate all 16 slots & wipe sprite graphics  |
   | SP_ACTIVE_TABLE      | 50040   | #C378  | 16 active slot structs (16 slots * 32 bytes)    |
   | SP_COLLISION_TABLE   | 50552   | #C578  | 16 collision status bytes (1 byte per slot)     |
   | SP_PARAM_BLOCK       | 50568   | #C588  | Scratch parameter exchange area (32 bytes)      |
   | SP_RESTORE_BUFFERS   | 50600   | #C5A8  | Internal background dirty-rect restore buffers  |
   | SP_DEFS              | 53072   | #CF50  | Sprite definition graphic bank (64 defs * 128B) |
   +----------------------+---------+--------+-------------------------------------------------+

   Active Slot Table Field Offsets (SLOT_SIZE = 32 bytes per slot):
   Each slot in `SP_ACTIVE_TABLE` spans 32 bytes (`slot_address = SP_ACTIVE_TABLE + slot_id * 32`):
     - `SLOT_STATUS`     (Offset +0)  : Status flag (0 = Inactive, 1 = Active/Visible).
     - `SLOT_DEF_ID`     (Offset +1)  : Definition archetype ID (0..63).
     - `SLOT_X`          (Offset +2)  : Horizontal pixel coordinate (0..255).
     - `SLOT_Y`          (Offset +3)  : Vertical pixel coordinate (0..191).
     - `SLOT_INK`        (Offset +7)  : Spectrum INK color / attribute byte (0..7).
     - `SLOT_FRAME`      (Offset +10) : Current animation frame index (0..3).
     - `SLOT_DIR`        (Offset +13) : Trajectory heading (0=Right, 64=Down, 128=Left, 192=Up).
     - `SLOT_PATH_MODE`  (Offset +14) : Movement mode (0=Manual, 1=Linear, 2=Bounce, 3=Circle).
     - `SLOT_SPEED`      (Offset +15) : Movement velocity in pixels per frame.
     - `SLOT_PATH_PARAM` (Offset +18) : Trajectory radius or angle increment parameter.
     - `SLOT_REBOUND`    (Offset +20) : Boundary rebound (0=Destroy/clamp, 1=Bounce off edges).
     - `SLOT_COLLISION`  (Offset +23) : Slot collision trigger flag.

   Collision Status Table Bitmasks (SP_COLLISION_TABLE + slot_id):
     - Bit 0 (%00000001): Sprite-to-Sprite bounding box overlap.
     - Bit 1 (%00000010): Collision with user-defined graphics (UDGs).
     - Bit 2 (%00000100): Background tile / solid block collision.
     - Bit 3 (%00001000): Foreground INK color collision.
     - Bit 4 (%00010000): Background PAPER color collision.
     - Bit 5 (%00100000): Reached target coordinate / destination waypoint.

3. Tilemap Engine Memory Map & Symbolic Equates (Default Base: 64000 / #FA00):
   +----------------------+---------+--------+-------------------------------------------------+
   | Symbol Equate        | Decimal | Hex    | Purpose / Calling Convention                    |
   +----------------------+---------+--------+-------------------------------------------------+
   | TL_BASE              | 64000   | #FA00  | Relocatable base address of tile engine         |
   | TL_PLACE             | 64000   | #FA00  | Blit 16x16 tile to screen from parameter block  |
   | TL_CLEAR             | 64003   | #FA03  | Clear tile display / screen region              |
   | TL_ROW               | 64006   | #FA06  | Target screen character row (0..22, 1 byte)     |
   | TL_COL               | 64007   | #FA07  | Target screen character column (0..30, 1 byte)  |
   | TL_TILE_ADDR         | 64008   | #FA08  | 16-bit address of 16x16 tile graphic data       |
   | TL_ATTR              | 64010   | #FA0A  | Spectrum attribute byte (FLASH*128+PAPER*8+INK) |
   | TL_FLAGS             | 64011   | #FA0B  | Blitting options flags byte                     |
   | TL_TILEMAP_BASE      | 64012   | #FA0C  | 16-bit pointer to tile matrix data              |
   | TL_NUM_TILES         | 64014   | #FA0E  | 16-bit total count of defined tiles             |
   | TL_MAP_DATA          | 40000   | #9C40  | Default base memory address for tilemap grid    |
   +----------------------+---------+--------+-------------------------------------------------+

   Tile Blitter Flag Bitmasks (TL_FLAGS):
     - `TL_FLAG_OVER`     (1 / %00000001): OVER 1 mode (XOR blit with background pixels).
     - `TL_FLAG_INVERSE`  (2 / %00000010): INVERSE 1 mode (invert pixel bitmap bits).
     - `TL_FLAG_NO_ATTR`  (4 / %00000100): Preserve background attributes (don't overwrite color).
     - `TL_FLAG_PACKED`   (8 / %00001000): Packed tile format decompression mode.

4. Assembly Programming Examples:

   Example A: Initializing and Blitting a 16x16 Tile via TL_PLACE
   --------------------------------------------------------------
   ; Assembled with Sinclair ZEUS Assembler
   INCLUDE TILEMAP
   ORG 32768

   START:
       ; Set screen row 4, column 8
       LD A, 4
       LD (TL_ROW), A
       LD A, 8
       LD (TL_COL), A

       ; Point to tile bitmap data (32 bytes bitmap + 4 bytes attribute)
       LD HL, TILE_GRASS
       LD (TL_TILE_ADDR), HL

       ; Set attribute: FLASH 0, BRIGHT 1, PAPER 0, INK 4 (Green) -> 0x44
       LD A, #44
       LD (TL_ATTR), A

       ; Normal blit (flags = 0)
       XOR A
       LD (TL_FLAGS), A

       ; Blit the 16x16 tile to the screen!
       CALL TL_PLACE
       RET

   TILE_GRASS:
       ; 32 bytes of 16x16 pixel graphic data:
       DEFB #AA, #55, #AA, #55, #AA, #55, #AA, #55
       DEFB #55, #AA, #55, #AA, #55, #AA, #55, #AA
       DEFB #AA, #55, #AA, #55, #AA, #55, #AA, #55
       DEFB #55, #AA, #55, #AA, #55, #AA, #55, #AA
       ; 4 attribute bytes (top-left, top-right, bottom-left, bottom-right):
       DEFB #44, #44, #44, #44


   Example B: Initializing and Moving an Animated Sprite via SP_STEP
   -----------------------------------------------------------------
   ; Assembled with Sinclair ZEUS Assembler
   INCLUDE SPRITES
   ORG 32768

   INIT_GAME:
       ; 1. Initialize sprite engine (clears slots and restoration buffers)
       CALL SP_INIT

       ; 2. Configure Sprite Slot 0 as an active player sprite
       LD IX, SP_ACTIVE_TABLE          ; IX points to Slot 0
       LD (IX + SLOT_STATUS), 1        ; Make slot active
       LD (IX + SLOT_DEF_ID), 0        ; Use Definition 0
       LD (IX + SLOT_X), 120           ; X = 120 pixels
       LD (IX + SLOT_Y), 80            ; Y = 80 pixels
       LD (IX + SLOT_INK), 6           ; INK 6 (Yellow)
       LD (IX + SLOT_FRAME), 0         ; Start on frame 0
       LD (IX + SLOT_PATH_MODE), 0     ; Manual movement mode

   GAME_LOOP:
       ; Wait for vertical blank (50Hz interrupt)
       HALT

       ; Move player to the right by 1 pixel
       INC (IX + SLOT_X)

       ; Step the sprite engine: erases old position, updates animation,
       ; draws at new position, and checks collisions!
       CALL SP_STEP

       ; Check for collision with background scenery (Bit 2 of collision table)
       LD A, (SP_COLLISION_TABLE)      ; Read Slot 0 collision byte
       AND 4                           ; Test Bit 2 (Solid Block collision)
       JR NZ, HIT_WALL

       ; Loop until SPACE key pressed (Port 0x7FFE, bit 0)
       LD BC, #7FFE
       IN A, (C)
       RRA
       JR C, GAME_LOOP

   HIT_WALL:
       RET


   Example C: Complete Integrated Game Loop (Tilemap + Sprite Engine)
   -----------------------------------------------------------------
   INCLUDE SPRITES
   INCLUDE TILEMAP
   ORG 32768

   MAIN:
       ; Clear screen and initialize engines
       CALL TL_CLEAR
       CALL SP_INIT

       ; Draw background tile border
       LD A, 2
       LD (TL_ROW), A
       LD A, 4
       LD (TL_COL), A
       LD HL, TILE_DATA
       LD (TL_TILE_ADDR), HL
       LD A, #47                       ; BRIGHT 1, WHITE
       LD (TL_ATTR), A
       XOR A
       LD (TL_FLAGS), A
       CALL TL_PLACE

       ; Setup animated bouncing enemy sprite (Slot 1)
       LD IX, SP_ACTIVE_TABLE + 32     ; Slot 1 address
       LD (IX + SLOT_STATUS), 1
       LD (IX + SLOT_DEF_ID), 1
       LD (IX + SLOT_X), 64
       LD (IX + SLOT_Y), 64
       LD (IX + SLOT_INK), 2           ; INK 2 (Red)
       LD (IX + SLOT_DIR), 32          ; Diagonal heading
       LD (IX + SLOT_SPEED), 2         ; 2 pixels per frame
       LD (IX + SLOT_PATH_MODE), 2     ; Bounce trajectory mode
       LD (IX + SLOT_REBOUND), 1       ; Rebound off screen walls

   FRAME_LOOP:
       HALT                            ; Synchronize to 50Hz frame
       CALL SP_STEP                    ; Engine handles redraw & bounce physics!

       ; Check key press 'Q' (Port 0xFBFE, bit 0) to quit
       LD BC, #FBFE
       IN A, (C)
       RRA
       JR C, FRAME_LOOP

       RET

   TILE_DATA:
       DEFS 32, #FF                    ; Solid block
       DEFS 4, #47


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 13. SECTION 12: SINCLAIR LOGO TURTLE GRAPHICS SUBSYSTEM (LOGO / END LOGO)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Target Extension:    Sinclair LOGO Turtle Graphics Subsystem (LOGO / END LOGO)
Compilation Support: [INTERPRETER ONLY]
                     (The interactive turtle graphics engine and LOGO interpreter execute
                     directly within the BasinPy runtime environment).

BasinPy Extended embeds a full implementation of Sinclair LOGO, bringing the classic
educational turtle graphics language to the Sinclair ZX Spectrum environment.

13.1 Interactive LOGO Environment & Command Structure
-----------------------------------------------------
LOGO can be launched as an interactive session or executed inline:
  - Command Syntax:
      LOGO                     : Enters interactive LOGO mode.
      LOGO <command>           : Executes single LOGO statement.
      LOGO ... END LOGO        : Inline LOGO script inside BASIC.
      END LOGO / BYE           : Exits LOGO and returns to BasinPy BASIC.

13.2 Turtle Movement & Orientation Commands
-------------------------------------------
The turtle begins at screen center (X=128, Y=96) facing North (0 degrees):
  - FORWARD <dist> / FD <dist> : Moves turtle forward by dist pixels, drawing a line if pen down.
  - BACK <dist> / BK <dist>    : Moves turtle backward by dist pixels.
  - LEFT <angle> / LT <angle>  : Rotates turtle counter-clockwise by angle degrees.
  - RIGHT <angle> / RT <angle> : Rotates turtle clockwise by angle degrees.
  - HOME                       : Moves turtle back to center (128, 96) heading 0 degrees.
  - CLEARSCREEN / CS           : Clears graphics screen and homes turtle.

13.3 Pen Controls, Procedures & Loops
-------------------------------------
  - PENUP / PU                 : Lifts pen; subsequent moves do not draw.
  - PENDOWN / PD               : Lowers pen; subsequent moves draw lines.
  - HIDETURTLE / HT            : Hides the on-screen turtle cursor.
  - SHOWTURTLE / ST            : Displays the on-screen turtle cursor.
  - SETPENCOLOR <c> / SETPC <c>: Sets pen color to Spectrum color index (0 to 7).
  - REPEAT <count> [ <stmts> ] : Executes enclosed statements count times.
  - TO <name> [<args>] ... END : Defines reusable custom LOGO procedures.
Example:
  LOGO
  CS SETPC 2
  REPEAT 4 [ FD 50 RT 90 ]
  END LOGO


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 14. SECTION 13: SINCLAIR MICRO-PROLOG SUBSYSTEM (PROLOG / END PROLOG)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Target Extension:    Sinclair Micro-PROLOG Subsystem (PROLOG / END PROLOG)
Compilation Support: [INTERPRETER ONLY]
                     (Micro-PROLOG declarative resolution, unification, and dynamic Horn clauses
                     execute within BasinPy's embedded Prolog interpreter engine).

BasinPy Extended features an authentic implementation of Sinclair Micro-PROLOG 1.0,
the fifth-generation logic programming language originally developed by Logic
Programming Associates (LPA) for the Sinclair ZX Spectrum 48K.

14.1 Micro-PROLOG 1.0 Environment & Syntax Rules
------------------------------------------------
Micro-PROLOG represents relationships, facts, and rules as symbolic Horn clauses:
  - Command Syntax:
      PROLOG                   : Enters interactive Micro-PROLOG shell (prompt '&.').
      PROLOG <command>         : Executes single PROLOG expression.
      PROLOG ... END PROLOG    : Inline PROLOG logic program in BASIC.
      END PROLOG / BYE         : Exits Micro-PROLOG and returns to BASIC.

14.2 Declarative Facts, Horn Clauses & Database Commands
--------------------------------------------------------
  - Facts:
      ((parent john mary))
      ((likes mary reading))
  - Horn Clauses (Rules):
      ((grandparent ?x ?z) (parent ?x ?y) (parent ?y ?z))
  - Database Management Commands:
      * ADD ((clause))         : Inserts new fact or rule into resident database.
      * DELETE <relation> [n]  : Deletes specified clause or entire relation.
      * LIST [<relation>]      : Displays all clauses or clauses for specified relation.

14.3 Interactive Queries & Pattern Matching
-------------------------------------------
Queries are evaluated using Robinson's resolution and unification:
  - Query Syntax:
      (? (relation arg1 ?var))
  - Examples:
      (? (parent ?who mary))   --> reports matching bindings: 'john'
      ALL (?x ?y) (parent ?x ?y) --> lists all solutions


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 15. SECTION 14: INTERACTIVE VISUAL EDITORS & MEDIA CONVERTERS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Target Extension:    BasinPy Interactive Visual Editors & Media Converters
Compilation Support: [INTERPRETER ONLY] / [DATA GENERATOR & ASSET BUNDLER]
                     (Visual editors for UDG, Music, JPG image conversion, Paint, Sprites,
                     and Tilemaps are interactive workstation studios. Their generated assets,
                     DATA lines, and binary output files compile directly into standalone .TAP
                     containers via BasinPy's multi-pass compiler pipeline).

BasinPy Extended provides built-in visual interactive editors for User Defined
Graphics (UDG), AY-3-8912 polyphonic music composition, and high-fidelity
bitmap image conversion directly into Sinclair ZX Spectrum SCREEN$ format.

15.1 Interactive UDG Visual Character Matrix Editor (UDGED Command)
-------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy incorporates an interactive 8x8 character matrix visual editor:
  - Command Syntax:
      UDGED                    : Launches UDG editor with current character.
      UDGED "A"                : Launches UDG editor targeting specific letter (A to U).
  - Visual Workspace:
      * Left: Enlarged 8x8 interactive editing grid with active cursor indicator.
      * Right: Live 1x native and 4x enlarged previews rendered in current INK/PAPER.
      * Status: Displays character letter, memory address, and hex byte values.
  - Interactive Hotkeys & Navigation:
      * Arrow Keys / Keypad (8/2/4/6): Navigate cursor across the 8x8 matrix.
      * SPACE                  : Toggles pixel under cursor between ON and OFF.
      * Keys A through U       : Instantly selects corresponding UDG character slot.
      * C                      : Clears entire 8x8 matrix to blank.
      * I                      : Inverts all pixels in the active character.
      * H                      : Flips character horizontally.
      * V                      : Flips character vertically.
      * R                      : Rotates character 90 degrees clockwise.
      * S + Arrow Key          : Shifts pixel data Up, Down, Left, or Right.
      * ENTER / ESC            : Saves character bytes to memory (65368+) and exits.

15.2 Interactive AY-3-8912 Music Tracker & Polyphonic Sequencer (MUSIC Command)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy features an interactive polyphonic sound synthesizer and 16-step music tracker:
  - Invocation:
      MUSIC                    : Launches interactive AY-3-8912 music tracker.
  - Features & Controls:
      * 3-Channel Polyphony (Channels A, B, and C) with independent volume and pitch.
      * Tone Period sliders (0 to 4095) with musical note and octave identification.
      * Noise Period generator (0 to 31) for percussion and sound effects.
      * 16 Envelope Generator waveforms (sawtooth, triangle, decay, repeat).
      * Interactive Piano Keyboard: Click or use QWERTY keys to audition notes live.
      * Pattern Sequencer: Create and test multi-channel melodic sequences.
  - On-Screen Architecture Toggle:
      * A prominent header button toggles between three sound architectures:
          [128K PLAY]  : Outputs native Sinclair 128K `PLAY` string syntax.
          [FULLER]     : Outputs 48K Fuller Box AY port writes (ports 63 and 95).
          [128K OUT]   : Outputs 128K AY port writes (ports 65533 and 49149).
      * Toggle via mouse click on the badge or by pressing key 'M'.
  - Isolated Note Auditioning & Auto-Release:
      * Auditioning notes in the tracker activates an automatic 300 ms release countdown timer,
        ensuring notes release cleanly without continuous buzzing or sticking notes.
  - BASIC Code Generation:
      * Pressing 'X' exports the tracker patterns directly into the resident BASIC program at
        line 9000 using the selected architecture syntax:
          - 128K PLAY:
              9000 REM *** 3-CHANNEL AY SOUND TRACKER MUSIC ***
              9010 RESTORE 9100: FOR s=0 TO 15: READ a$,b$,c$: PLAY a$,b$,c$: NEXT s: RETURN
              9100 DATA "&4","&4","&4" ...
          - FULLER 48K OUT:
              9000 REM *** 3-CHANNEL AY SOUND TRACKER MUSIC (FULLER BOX) ***
              9010 RESTORE 9100: FOR s=0 TO 15: FOR r=0 TO 14: READ v: OUT 63,r: OUT 95,v: NEXT r: NEXT s: RETURN
          - 128K OUT:
              9000 REM *** 3-CHANNEL AY SOUND TRACKER MUSIC (128K AY OUT) ***
              9010 RESTORE 9100: FOR s=0 TO 15: FOR r=0 TO 14: READ v: OUT 65533,r: OUT 49149,v: NEXT r: NEXT s: RETURN
      * ESC : Exits music tracker and returns to the BASIC editor.

15.3 Interactive Sound Effects Generator & Synthesizer Studio (SFX, SFXED, SOUNDED Commands)
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy incorporates a dedicated interactive Sound Effects (SFX) studio for creating retro arcade
explosions, lasers, jumps, powerups, and ambient sounds:
  - Invocation:
      SFX                      : Launches interactive SFX generator studio.
      SFXED                    : Synonym for SFX.
      SOUNDED                  : Synonym for SFX.
  - Interactive Parameter Controls:
      1. Waveform     : Pulse / Tone, White Noise, Mixed Tone & Noise.
      2. Frequency    : Base pitch / frequency (0 to 4095).
      3. Duration     : Effect duration in frames / milliseconds.
      4. Pitch Sweep  : Linear / exponential pitch slide (Up / Down / Vibrato).
      5. Envelope     : Attack / Decay / Sustain envelope curves (0 to 15).
      6. Modulation   : Frequency modulation / warble rate.
      7. Noise Period : White noise hardness / filter (0 to 31).
      8. Output Engine: Selectable audio destination engine.
  - 3-Way Output Engine Toggle:
      * Supports three selectable audio playback and export targets:
          [48K BEEP]   : Standard 48K single-bit beeper (port 254 / 0xFE).
          [FULLER AY]  : Fuller Box AY-3-8912 sound unit (ports 63 & 95).
          [128K AY]    : Sinclair 128K AY-3-8912 sound unit (ports 65533 & 49149).
      * Toggle via clicking the on-screen badge, pressing key 'O', or adjusting parameter 8.
  - Real-Time Auditioning:
      * Pressing SPACE auditions the currently synthesized sound effect instantly through
        the selected audio hardware engine.
  - BASIC Code Generation:
      * Pressing 'X' exports the configured sound effect routines directly to line 4000
        in the resident BASIC program using authentic syntax:
          - 48K BEEP:
              4000 REM *** SOUND EFFECTS (48K BEEP) ***
              4010 REM EFFECT 1: LASER
              4020 FOR f=35 TO -15 STEP -3: BEEP 0.006,f: NEXT f: RETURN
              4030 REM EFFECT 2: EXPLODE
              4040 FOR n=1 TO 12: BEEP 0.007,RND*30-15: NEXT n: RETURN
          - FULLER AY:
              4000 REM *** SOUND EFFECTS (FULLER AY - PORTS 63/95) ***
              4010 REM EFFECT 1: LASER
              4020 OUT 63,7: OUT 95,62: OUT 63,8: OUT 95,15: ...
          - 128K AY:
              4000 REM *** SOUND EFFECTS (128K AY - PORTS 65533/49149) ***
              4010 REM EFFECT 1: LASER
              4020 OUT 65533,7: OUT 49149,62: OUT 65533,8: OUT 49149,15: ...
      * ESC : Exits SFX editor and returns to BASIC.

15.4 Cheetah SpecDrum 8-Bit DAC Interactive Drum Machine (DRUMS, DRUM, SPECDRUM Commands)
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy provides an authentic rhythm workstation emulating the famous Cheetah SpecDrum 8-bit DAC
percussion hardware:
  - Invocation:
      DRUMS                    : Launches interactive 8-pad rhythm drum sequencer.
      DRUM                     : Without arguments launches drum editor; in BASIC: DRUM <sample_id>.
      SPECDRUM                 : Synonym for DRUMS.
  - Features & Controls:
      * 8-Pad Interactive Drum Bank:
          Pad 1 / Key 1 / Q : Bass Drum / Kick
          Pad 2 / Key 2 / W : Snare Drum
          Pad 3 / Key 3 / E : Low Tom
          Pad 4 / Key 4 / R : Mid Tom
          Pad 5 / Key 5 / T : High Tom
          Pad 6 / Key 6 / Y : Closed Hi-Hat
          Pad 7 / Key 7 / U : Open Hi-Hat
          Pad 8 / Key 8 / I : Crash Cymbal / Clap
      * Click pads with the mouse or press hotkeys '1'..'8' / 'Q'..'I' to audition samples live.
      * Multiple Drum Kits: Choose between Standard, Electro, Afro, and Latin sample banks.
      * Step Sequencer: 16-step / 256-beat programmable rhythmic grid with tempo adjustment.
  - Complete AY Sound Silence & Hardware Isolation:
      * Launching the drum machine explicitly silences the AY-3-8912 sound chip via `fuller_ay_chip.reset()`.
      * All audio output routes exclusively through the Cheetah SpecDrum 8-bit DAC on port 223 (0xDF).
  - BASIC Code Generation:
      * Pressing 'X' exports the rhythm sequence directly to line 5000 in the resident BASIC program:
          5000 REM *** 256-BEAT DRUM MACHINE SEQUENCE ***
          5010 RESTORE 5100: FOR b=1 TO 64: READ d1,d2
          5020 IF d1>0 THEN DRUM d1
          5030 IF d2>0 THEN DRUM d2
          5040 PAUSE 12: NEXT b: RETURN
          5100 DATA 1,3, 6,0, 2,7 ...
      * ESC : Exits drum editor and returns to BASIC.

15.5 Image to Spectrum SCREEN$ Converter: JPG, PNG, BMP (JPG Command)
---------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy allows importing modern graphic images and converting them into authentic
Sinclair ZX Spectrum 6912-byte display screens with intelligent color quantization:
  - Command Syntax:
      JPG "photo.jpg"          : Converts photo.jpg to photo.scr and displays it.
      JPG "graphic.png"        : Supports PNG, BMP, and JPEG image formats.
  - Conversion Pipeline:
      1. Scales image to 256x192 resolution maintaining aspect ratio.
      2. Quantizes color palette to the 15 standard Sinclair colors (8 Normal + 7 Bright).
      3. Resolves attribute clash constraints (allocating max 2 colors per 8x8 block).
      4. Applies Floyd-Steinberg or Atkinson error-diffusion dithering.
      5. Automatically saves the resulting 6912-byte `.scr` file to disk.
      6. Automatically inserts BASIC loader lines 6000–6030 into the resident program:
           6000 REM *** ZX SPECTRUM SCREEN$ DISPLAY ***
           6010 BORDER 0: PAPER 0: INK 7: CLS
           6020 LOAD "photo.scr" SCREEN$
           6030 PAUSE 0: STOP
      7. Instantly displays the converted graphic on the BasinPy CRT canvas!


15.6 Interactive ZX Paint Studio: Deluxe Paint / The Artist (PAINT, ARTIST, DPAINT)
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy incorporates a full-featured, professional ZX Spectrum screen paint program
modeled on the dual-pane workflow of Electronic Arts' Deluxe Paint (Amiga) and
Melbourne House's The Artist (ZX Spectrum):

  - Invocation Commands:
      PAINT                     : Launches ZX Paint Studio on the resident screen canvas.
      PAINT "myscreen.scr"      : Launches studio and loads the specified .scr image.
      PAINT "photo.jpg"         : Launches studio and imports/converts the given JPEG picture.
      ARTIST                    : Alias for PAINT.
      DPAINT                    : Alias for PAINT.

  - Dual-Pane Sinclair Look & Feel:
      * Left Panel (Toolbox): Uses BasinPy's listing area for command buttons, icons,
        palette swatches, brush selectors, and input toggles styled with an authentic
        Sinclair ZX Spectrum aesthetic (including authentic 4-color rainbow stripe).
      * Right Area (Canvas): The entire 256x192 Spectrum canvas remains un-obscured
        for pixel-perfect editing with live rubber-band shape previews and tool overlays.

  - Comprehensive Bitmap Drawing Tools:
      1.  Pencil: Single-pixel freehand drawing.
      2.  Brush: Multi-pixel drawing with 10 selectable shapes (1x1, 2x2, 3x3, 5x5,
          Cross, Slash, Backslash, Vertical Bar, Horizontal Bar, Checkerboard Dither).
      3.  Line: Bresenham line drawer with live rubber-band preview on canvas.
      4.  Ray: Continuous connected line segment drawing.
      5.  Rect: Hollow rectangle outline with live rubber-band preview.
      6.  FillBox: Solid filled rectangle with live rubber-band preview.
      7.  Circle: Midpoint Bresenham circle outline with rubber-band preview.
      8.  FillCir: Solid filled circle with rubber-band preview.
      9.  Ellipse: Midpoint ellipse outline with rubber-band preview.
      10. Bucket: Fast 4-way BFS flood-fill algorithm.
      11. Spray: Airbrush random-dispersion pixel spraying.
      12. Text: Sinclair 8x8 ROM font character stamp directly onto the canvas.
      13. Eraser: Clears pixels (PAPER color) under the selected brush shape.
      14. Color: Attribute wash tool updates INK/PAPER/BRIGHT/FLASH without touching pixels.
      15. Pick: Eyedropper samples INK, PAPER, BRIGHT, and FLASH from hovered cell.
      16. Clear / Invert: Fast screen clearing (CLS) or full-screen pixel inversion.

  - Attribute & Color Modes:
      * INK (0..7) & PAPER (0..7): Interactive swatch selectors with active indicators.
      * BRIGHT & FLASH: One-click toggles.
      * SWAP: Instant swap between INK and PAPER colors.
      * Attribute Modes (Cycle with 'A'):
          - AUTO: Draws pixels and assigns current INK, PAPER, BRIGHT, FLASH to cells.
          - PIXEL_ONLY: Alters bitmap pixels while preserving existing cell attributes.
          - COLOR_ONLY: Paints cell attributes without altering the underlying bitmap.

  - Multi-Level Undo / Redo:
      * Full 16-level snapshot history storing bitmap and all attribute arrays.
      * Hotkeys: 'U' (Undo) and 'R' (Redo), plus dedicated toolbox buttons.

  - Peripheral & Format Support:
      * Native .SCR format (6912 bytes) saving and loading across all peripherals:
          - Host filesystem (.scr in current or ZX directory)
          - Tape (.tap cassette images)
          - Microdrive (m:1; cartridge storage)
          - RAM disk (! memory arrays)
          - Floppy disk (d1: +3 / Opus / Beta disk images)
      * JPG Import: Loads JPEG images, scales to 256x192, optimizes INK/PAPER per cell
        using Euclidean RGB distance minimization, and loads directly into the canvas.

  - Selectable Input Devices:
      * Mouse: Supports AMX Mouse and Kempston Mouse with smooth canvas tracking.
      * Joystick: Selectable Kempston, Cursor / Protek, Sinclair 1, and Sinclair 2.
      * Keyboard: QAOP + Space or Arrow Keys + Enter/Space (Shift moves 4px per step).
