      * Number Keys 1..8: Select INK 0..7 (Shift + 1..8 selects PAPER 0..7).

  - ESC Exit Flow:
      * Pressing ESC displays a safe modal dialog inside the toolbox:
        "Save screen before returning to BASIC? [Y]es [N]o [C]ancel"
      * [Y]es prompts for filename, saves, and returns cleanly to BASIC.
      * [N]o immediately returns to BASIC, preserving all canvas artwork intact.
      * [C]ancel closes the dialog and resumes painting.


15.7 Interactive 16x16 Dual-Engine Sprite Designer & Animation Studio (EDSPRITES, SPRITED, SPRITE EDIT)
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Target Extension:    BasinPy Extended Dual-Engine Sprite Subsystem (48K Mono & Next Hardware Color)
Compilation Support: [INTERPRETER / 48K TAP & NEXTBASIC COMPATIBLE]
                     (Built-in code generator outputs standard Sinclair 48K BASIC DATA statements
                     and loader routines at lines 5000+ which compile directly into standalone
                     machine code .TAP containers, or native NextBASIC hardware sprite definitions).

BasinPy features an integrated, professional-grade 16x16 dual-engine sprite design studio
supporting both classic Sinclair ZX Spectrum 48K monochrome machine-code sprites and modern
ZX Spectrum Next hardware color sprites:

  - Invocation Commands:
      `EDSPRITES [id | "ZX" | "NEXT"]` : Launches sprite editor targeting specified sprite ID
                                         or opens directly in designated engine mode.
      `EDSPRITE [id | "ZX" | "NEXT"]`  : Shorthand alias for EDSPRITES.
      `SPRITED [id | "ZX" | "NEXT"]`   : Shorthand alias for EDSPRITES.
      `SPRITE EDIT [id]`               : Equivalent syntax from within the SPRITE command family.
      If arguments are omitted, defaults to active engine mode and sprite 0.

  - Dual-Engine Selection & Mode Toggle:
      * Live Toggle: Press `M` key or click the top badge `[ZX]` / `[NEXT]` to switch instantly
        between 48K Monochrome and Next Hardware Color editing modes.
      * Global Engine Command: `SET SPRITES ZX` (for 48K) or `SET SPRITES NEXT` (for Next HW).

  - ZX Mode (48K Monochrome 16x16 Sprites):
      * 16 sprite slots with 4 animation frames each (32 bytes per frame, 128 bytes per definition).
      * 4-Frame Animation Strip: Frames 1, 2, 3, and 4 displayed side-by-side along the bottom.
      * Real-Time Looped Animation Preview Box: 5th box displays continuous animated sequence with
        adjustable playback speed (FPS).
      * Motion & Trajectory Attributes: Heading direction (8 compass points), speed (0..7 px/frame),
        path modes (Linear, Circle, Box, Wave-V, Wave-H), rebound bounce frames, and INK color (0..7).
      * Code Generation (`G` key): Inserts clean Sinclair 48K BASIC loader code starting at line 5000
        (`SPRITE INIT 50000`, `SPRITE DEF s FROM 5200`, `SPRITE POS...`, `DATA` lines).

  - NEXT Mode (Next Hardware Color 16x16 Sprites):
      * 64 hardware sprite patterns (256 bytes per pattern, 8-bit palette indices 0..255).
      * 5 Selectable Color Depths: 16, 32, 64, 128, and 256 colors (`D` key or click depth buttons).
      * Interactive 9-Bit RGB Palette Bar: Displays true Next RGB colors (`next_rgb_to_888`) with
        quick page selectors (`<` and `>`). Select colors by clicking or pressing `K`.
      * Next Hardware Transparency: Color index `227` (`0xE3` / Magenta) key color (`T` key).
      * Hardware Attributes: Position `(X, Y)` and Palette Offset `(0..15)`.
      * NextBASIC Code Generation (`G` key): Inserts native Next hardware sprite definitions starting
        at line 5000 (`SPRITE CLEAR`, `SPRITE DEF p FROM 5200`, `DATA ...`, `SPRITE ...`).

  - Interactive Controls & Keyboard Shortcuts:
      * Arrow Keys / Mouse Drag      : Navigate cursor / draw across 16x16 editing grid.
      * SPACE / Left Click           : Draw pixel with active color / INK.
      * Right Click                  : Erase pixel (sets to 0 in ZX mode; transparent 0xE3 in Next mode).
      * 1, 2, 3, 4                   : Selects Frame 1..4 (ZX mode) or Pattern offset (Next mode).
      * [ and ] (PageUp / PageDown)  : Selects previous or next Sprite / Pattern ID.
      * M                            : Toggles engine mode between `[ZX]` and `[NEXT]`.
      * D                            : Cycles Next color depth (16 -> 32 -> 64 -> 128 -> 256).
      * K                            : Cycles color / palette selection.
      * T                            : Selects transparent color (0xE3 / 227) in Next mode.
      * C                            : Clears active 16x16 grid.
      * I                            : Inverts active pattern / frame.
      * H / F                        : Flips horizontally.
      * V                            : Flips vertically.
      * R / O                        : Rotates 90 degrees clockwise.
      * Y                            : Copies active frame to the next frame slot.
      * G                            : Generates clean executable BASIC code into program memory!
      * Q / X / ESC                  : Synchronizes engine RAM and exits editor.

  - Automated BASIC Code Generation (G / [GEN]):
      Pressing `G` or clicking `[GEN]` automatically constructs production-ready BASIC loader
      routines starting at line 5000 and inserts them directly into the resident program:
      In ZX Mode (Sinclair 48K):
        5000 REM === SPRITE ENGINE AUTO-LOADER ===
        5005 SPRITE INIT 50000: REM Init Engine at BASE 50000
        5010 SPRITE DEF 0 FROM 5200: REM Load Sprite 0 Def
        5015 SPRITE POS 0, 120, 80: SPRITE MOVE 0, 2, 2: SPRITE INK 0, 6: SPRITE ON 0, 1, 0
        5020 RETURN: REM End of Sprite Loader Subroutine
        5200 REM --- Sprite 0 Data (4 Frames x 32 Bytes) ---
      In NEXT Mode (NextBASIC Hardware Sprites):
        5000 REM === NEXT SPRITE AUTO-LOADER ===
        5005 SPRITE CLEAR: REM Reset all hardware sprites
        5010 SPRITE DEF 0 FROM 5200: REM Load 256 bytes into pattern 0
        5015 SPRITE 0, 120, 80, 0: SPRITE ON 0, 0: REM Place and show sprite 0
        5020 RETURN: REM End of Next Sprite Subroutine
        5200 REM --- Pattern 0 Hardware Data (256 Bytes) ---


15.8 Interactive 16x16 Tilemap Matrix & Room Editor (EDTILES, TILED)
--------------------------------------------------------------------
Target Extension:    BasinPy Extended Custom Subsystem
Compilation Support: [INTERPRETER ONLY]
                     (Saved .map binary banks and .scr screen files are bundled into standalone
                     machine code .TAP containers via BasinPy's multi-pass compiler).

BasinPy includes a dedicated 16x16 Tilemap Matrix and Room Designer modeled on classic
arcade and NES tile engines:

  - Invocation Commands:
      `EDTILES [tile_id]`         : Launches tilemap editor targeting specified tile (0..191).
      `EDTILE [tile_id]`          : Shorthand alias for EDTILES.
      `TILED [tile_id]`           : Shorthand alias for EDTILES.
      If `tile_id` is omitted, defaults to Tile 0.

  - Studio Workspace Architecture (256x192 CRT Display):
      * Upper Area (Tile Editor & Toolbox):
          - Left: 16x16 enlarged drawing grid (6x scale) for editing individual tile bitmaps.
          - Right Palette: 8 INK color swatches, 8 PAPER color swatches, and `[BRIGHT]` toggle.
          - Transform Tools: `[CLR]` (Clear), `[INV]` (Invert), `[FLPH]` (Flip Horizontal),
            `[FLPV]` (Flip Vertical), and `[ROT]` (Rotate 90 degrees clockwise).
          - File Management Buttons:
              * `[SAVE MAP]`: Exports resident 192-tile bank to a `.map` binary file.
              * `[SAVE SCN]`: Exports full 16x12 tile screen to standard 6912-byte `.scr` format.
              * `[GRAB SCR]`: Imports raw `.scr` screen dump and decomposes it into 192 tiles!
              * `[LOAD MAP]`: Loads external `.map` tile file into memory.
      * Lower Area (8x4 Tile Matrix Browser):
          - Displays 32 live tile thumbnails simultaneously formatted as an 8-column by 4-row matrix.
          - Spans 6 selectable pages (32 tiles * 6 pages = 192 tiles total, perfectly matching
            a full 256x192 Spectrum screen composed of 16x12 16x16 tiles).
          - Clicking any thumbnail immediately loads that tile into the upper editing canvas.
          - Page Navigation: `[PREV]` and `[NEXT]` buttons cycle through pages 1 to 6.

  - Interactive Controls & Keyboard Shortcuts:
      * Arrow Keys / Keypad (8/2/4/6): Navigate cursor across the 16x16 tile grid.
      * SPACE / ENTER / Left Click   : Toggle pixel under cursor between INK and PAPER.
      * Left Click & Drag            : Continuous freehand pixel drawing.
      * [ and ] (or - and =)         : Previous or next tile (Tile 0 to 191).
      * PageUp / PageDown            : Previous or next 32-tile page (Pages 1 to 6).
      * Number Keys 1..8             : Set active INK color (0 to 7).
      * C                            : Clears the active tile to blank.
      * V                            : Inverts all pixels in the active tile.
      * H                            : Flips active tile horizontally.
      * F                            : Flips active tile vertically.
      * R                            : Rotates active tile 90 degrees clockwise.
      * S                            : Opens dialog to save tilemap as `.map` file.
      * Shift + S                    : Opens dialog to save screen as `.scr` / `.scn` file.
      * G                            : Grabs 192 tiles from an existing `.scr` screen file.
      * L                            : Loads `.map` tile bank from disk.
      * Q / ESC                      : Synchronizes live tilemap memory and returns to BASIC.

  - File Format & Memory Integration:
      * Standard 16x16 Tile Memory Layout:
          - Each tile occupies 32 bytes of bitmap pixel data (two 8x8 character cells wide by
            two character cells high) plus 4 attribute bytes (top-left, top-right, bottom-left,
            bottom-right) = 36 bytes per tile (or 256 bytes per uncompressed bank tile).
      * Seamless BASIC Integration:
          - Saved tiles can be blitted directly into game screens from Sinclair BASIC using
            `PLACE row, col, TILE(x)` or loaded at runtime using `LOAD TILEMAP "level1.map"`.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 18. SECTION 17: ENHANCED CONFORMING TAPE (.TAP) SUBSYSTEM & AUTOSTART EXECUTION
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Target Extension:    Conforming Tape (.TAP) Subsystem & Autostart Execution
Compilation Support: [48K TAPE FORMAT COMPATIBLE]
                     (Conforms strictly to standard Sinclair ZX Spectrum .TAP cassette specifications,
                     supporting multi-block loading, autostart line parameters, and code injection).

BasinPy Extended includes an authentic, production-grade Sinclair ZX Spectrum
tape container engine conforming to the official `.TAP` file format specification.

18.1 Conforming Sinclair ZX Tape Container Format & Block Descriptors
---------------------------------------------------------------------
A `.TAP` tape file consists of alternating 19-byte Header blocks and Data blocks:
  - Header Block (19 bytes: 2 bytes length + 1 byte flag 0x00 + 17 bytes payload + 1 byte parity):
      * Byte 0: Block Type:
          0 = Program (BASIC source with autostart parameter)
          1 = Number Array (DIM var())
          2 = Character Array (DIM var$())
          3 = Bytes / Code (Machine code, screen dump, or raw memory)
      * Bytes 1..10: Filename (padded with spaces to 10 characters)
      * Bytes 11..12: Data block length (bytes, little-endian)
      * Bytes 13..14: Parameter 1:
          For Program (Type 0): Autostart line number (or >= 32768 if no autostart)
          For Code (Type 3): Load start memory address (e.g. 16384 for SCREEN$, 32768 for Code)
      * Bytes 15..16: Parameter 2:
          For Program (Type 0): Length of program without variables
          For Code (Type 3): Unused (default 32768)
  - Data Block (Flag 0xFF + Payload + Parity):
      Contains the tokenized BASIC code, raw byte array, or compiled Z80 machine code.

18.2 Case-Insensitive Multi-Block Tape Parsing (.tap and .TAP)
--------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy automatically handles case variations in tape file extensions across Windows,
macOS, and Linux. Both `.tap` and `.TAP` files are routed to the binary tape parser.
When loading a multi-block tape (e.g. `MANIC.TAP` or `Dig.tap`):
  1. The Type 0 header is parsed and autostart line extracted.
  2. The Sinclair BASIC token stream is decoded using BasinPy's Sinclair detokenizer.
  3. All program lines are populated into resident memory.
  4. Subsequent Type 3 CODE blocks (e.g. machine code sprites, game logic) are
     automatically loaded into target memory locations (e.g. 22784, 24500, 32768).

18.3 Binary Machine Code Pre-loading & Automatic Execution Line Targeting
-------------------------------------------------------------------------
When loading commercial Spectrum games with embedded autostart parameters:
  - If the header specifies an autostart line (e.g. Line 10 in `MANIC.TAP`), BasinPy
    automatically triggers execution starting at that exact line.
  - Line targeting executes `CLEAR`, sets screen colors, loads embedded code blocks,
    and runs the `RANDOMIZE USR` machine code entry point, seamlessly launching
    the Z80 title screen and game loop!

18.4 Fast Tape Loading Audio Chirp Simulation
---------------------------------------------
During program execution (such as `LOAD "" CODE` inside a BASIC loader), BasinPy
simulates the authentic red/cyan pilot tone and blue/yellow data stripes with
a brisk 0.5s audio chirp, giving the authentic retro feel without imposing
long stalls.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 19. SECTION 18: SINCLAIR FORTH NATIVE COMPILER & .ZXF CONTAINER SUBSYSTEM
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Target Extension:    Sinclair Forth Native Compiler & .ZXF Container Subsystem
Compilation Support: [NATIVE Z80 MACHINE CODE] / [.ZXF CONTAINER COMPATIBLE]
                     (Compiles Forth words directly into native Z80 machine code targeting Spectrum RAM.
                     Executable via RANDOMIZE USR and distributable via standalone .zxf containers).

BasinPy Extended features a high-performance native Sinclair Forth machine code
compiler subsystem. Unlike traditional Forth environments that run as threaded-code
interpreters (such as Abersoft Forth / fig-FORTH 1.1 detailed in Section 4), the
Sinclair Forth compiler emits direct, pure Z80 machine code instructions (PUSH, POP,
EX DE,HL, ADD HL,DE, CALL, JP, RET, etc.) that execute at full native Z80 CPU speed
via SinclairZ80CPU.

19.1 Native Forth to Z80 Machine Code Architecture
--------------------------------------------------
The Sinclair Forth compiler generates true Z80 binary instructions targeting
Spectrum RAM (default origin address 49152 / 0xC000):
  - Stack Architecture:
      * Top of Stack (TOS) is cached in the Z80 HL register pair for maximum speed.
      * Secondary stack operands are pushed and popped to/from the machine stack (SP)
        or handled through DE and BC.
      * Binary operations (e.g. `+`, `-`, `AND`, `OR`) pop the second parameter into DE,
        compute the result into HL, and leave the new TOS in HL.
  - Calling Convention & Execution Safety:
      * Compiled routines conclude with a native Z80 `RET` instruction.
      * Sinclair BASIC system variables, the machine stack pointer, and the BASIC
        runtime environment are completely preserved across Forth calls.
      * Cycle-accurate execution runs inside the `SinclairZ80CPU` core with full 50Hz
        frame interrupts and ULA video sync.

19.2 Block & Immediate Execution: FORTH ... END FORTH and FORTH <statements>
----------------------------------------------------------------------------
Sinclair Forth statements can be embedded directly into Sinclair BASIC programs or
executed immediately from the command line:
  - Multi-Line Program Blocks:
      A BASIC program can contain multi-line Forth code blocks delimited by
      `FORTH` and `END FORTH`:
        10 REM Sinclair Forth Prime Number Sieve
        20 FORTH
        30 : SQUARE DUP * ;
        40 : PRINTSQ 0 DO I SQUARE . LOOP CR ;
        50 10 PRINTSQ
        60 END FORTH
        70 PRINT "Forth execution finished."
  - Single-Line Statements in BASIC:
      Single-line Forth commands can be mixed anywhere inside BASIC code:
        100 FORTH 10 20 + . CR
        110 FORTH 255 254 OUT
  - Immediate Mode Command Line:
      Forth statements can be entered directly at the BasinPy prompt:
        FORTH 5 5 * . CR
        FORTH : STAR 42 EMIT ; 20 0 DO STAR LOOP CR

19.3 1st Run Compilation Caching & RANDOMIZE USR Execution
----------------------------------------------------------
To ensure maximum responsiveness, the Forth subsystem implements an intelligent
1st Run Compilation Caching engine:
  - First-Run Compilation:
      When a `FORTH ... END FORTH` block or immediate statement is encountered for
      the first time, the compiler parses the token stream, generates native Z80
      machine code bytes, writes them directly into Spectrum memory at the origin
      address (0xC000 / 49152), and marks the block as compiled (`compiled = True`).
  - Fast-Path Execution:
      Subsequent executions of the same Forth block bypass the compiler entirely.
      The engine jumps straight to the pre-compiled binary entry point, achieving
      instantaneous execution with zero compilation overhead.
  - Direct RANDOMIZE USR Execution:
      Because Forth compiles to standard Z80 machine code, compiled words and blocks
      can be invoked at any time using standard Sinclair BASIC USR calls:
        RANDOMIZE USR 49152
        LET result = USR 49152

19.4 Native .ZXF File Format (SAVE "name", FORTH & LOAD "name", FORTH)
---------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy introduces the `.ZXF` container format for preserving and distributing
compiled Sinclair Forth machine code binaries without requiring source recompilation:
  - Container Specification:
      * Bytes 0..3   : ASCII Magic identifier `ZXF\x01` (Sinclair Forth version 1)
      * Bytes 4..5   : Origin / Load memory address (16-bit little-endian, default 49152)
      * Bytes 6..7   : Entry point execution address (16-bit little-endian)
      * Bytes 8..9   : Code payload byte length (16-bit little-endian)
      * Bytes 10..15 : Reserved header bytes (6 null bytes)
      * Offset 16+   : Pure Z80 executable machine code payload
  - Saving Forth Binaries:
      SAVE "myprog", FORTH
      SAVE "myprog.zxf"
      Exports the resident compiled Forth machine code into a portable `.zxf` file.
  - Loading Forth Binaries:
      LOAD "myprog", FORTH
      LOAD "myprog.zxf"
      Loads the `.zxf` payload directly into Spectrum RAM at the specified origin
      address and configures the entry point for immediate `RANDOMIZE USR` execution.

19.5 Complete Dictionary Reference
----------------------------------
The Sinclair Forth compiler includes a comprehensive dictionary of standard Forth-79 /
Forth-83 words, arithmetic operations, stack manipulators, control structures, and
Sinclair ZX Spectrum hardware primitives:

  - Stack Manipulation:
      DUP          ( n -- n n )           Duplicates top item on stack.
      DROP         ( n -- )               Discards top item from stack.
      SWAP         ( n1 n2 -- n2 n1 )     Swaps top two items on stack.
      OVER         ( n1 n2 -- n1 n2 n1 )  Copies second item to top of stack.
      ROT          ( n1 n2 n3 -- n2 n3 n1) Rotates third item to top of stack.
      ?DUP         ( n -- n [n] )         Duplicates n only if non-zero.
      2DUP         ( d -- d d )           Duplicates top pair of items.
      2DROP        ( d -- )               Discards top pair of items.
      2SWAP        ( d1 d2 -- d2 d1 )     Swaps top two pairs of items.
      NIP          ( n1 n2 -- n2 )        Removes second item from stack.
      TUCK         ( n1 n2 -- n2 n1 n2 )  Copies top item below second item.

  - Arithmetic & Mathematics:
      +            ( n1 n2 -- sum )       16-bit addition.
      -            ( n1 n2 -- diff )      16-bit subtraction (n1 - n2).
      *            ( n1 n2 -- prod )      16-bit signed multiplication.
      /            ( n1 n2 -- quot )      16-bit signed division (n1 / n2).
      MOD          ( n1 n2 -- rem )       16-bit division remainder.
      /MOD         ( n1 n2 -- rem quot )  Divides n1 by n2, returns remainder and quotient.
      NEGATE       ( n -- -n )            Two's complement negation.
      ABS          ( n -- |n| )           Absolute value of n.
      MIN          ( n1 n2 -- min )       Returns minimum of n1 and n2.
      MAX          ( n1 n2 -- max )       Returns maximum of n1 and n2.
      1+           ( n -- n+1 )           Increments top item by 1.
      1-           ( n -- n-1 )           Decrements top item by 1.
      2+           ( n -- n+2 )           Increments top item by 2.
      2-           ( n -- n-2 )           Decrements top item by 2.
      2*           ( n -- n*2 )           Arithmetic left shift (multiply by 2).
      2/           ( n -- n/2 )           Arithmetic right shift (divide by 2).

  - Bitwise & Logical:
      AND          ( n1 n2 -- res )       Bitwise AND.
      OR           ( n1 n2 -- res )       Bitwise OR.
      XOR          ( n1 n2 -- res )       Bitwise Exclusive-OR.
      INVERT       ( n -- ~n )            Bitwise One's complement inversion.

  - Comparison Operators:
      =            ( n1 n2 -- flag )      True (-1) if n1 equals n2, else False (0).
      <>           ( n1 n2 -- flag )      True if n1 is not equal to n2.
      <            ( n1 n2 -- flag )      True if n1 is less than n2 (signed).
      >            ( n1 n2 -- flag )      True if n1 is greater than n2 (signed).
      <=           ( n1 n2 -- flag )      True if n1 is less than or equal to n2.
      >=           ( n1 n2 -- flag )      True if n1 is greater than or equal to n2.
      0=           ( n -- flag )          True if n is 0.
      0<>          ( n -- flag )          True if n is not 0.
      0<           ( n -- flag )          True if n is negative (< 0).
      0>           ( n -- flag )          True if n is positive (> 0).
      U<           ( u1 u2 -- flag )      True if u1 is less than u2 (unsigned).
      U>           ( u1 u2 -- flag )      True if u1 is greater than u2 (unsigned).

  - Memory Inspection & Modification:
      @            ( addr -- val )        Fetches 16-bit word from memory address.
      !            ( val addr -- )        Stores 16-bit word at memory address.
      C@           ( addr -- byte )       Fetches 8-bit byte from memory address.
      C!           ( byte addr -- )       Stores 8-bit byte at memory address.
      +!           ( n addr -- )          Adds n to 16-bit value at memory address.

  - Control Flow Structures:
      IF ... THEN                         Executes code if flag on stack is non-zero.
      IF ... ELSE ... THEN                Two-way conditional branch.
      DO ... LOOP                         Loops from start to limit-1.
                                          `I` pushes inner loop index.
                                          `J` pushes outer loop index.
                                          `LEAVE` exits DO-LOOP immediately.
      DO ... +LOOP                        Loops with step increment on stack.
      BEGIN ... UNTIL                     Loops until condition at UNTIL evaluates True.
      BEGIN ... WHILE ... REPEAT          While condition at WHILE is True, loops.
      BEGIN ... AGAIN                     Infinite loop.

  - Console Output & Input:
      .            ( n -- )               Prints signed decimal integer followed by space.
      U.           ( u -- )               Prints unsigned decimal integer.
      EMIT         ( char -- )            Outputs ASCII character to screen.
      SPACE                               Prints single space character.
      SPACES       ( n -- )               Prints n space characters.
      CR                                  Emits carriage return / newline.
      ." <text>"                          Prints literal text string to screen.
      KEY          ( -- char )            Waits for keyboard keypress, pushes ASCII code.
      PAGE / CLS                          Clears the ZX Spectrum CRT screen.
      DECIMAL                             Sets default numeric input/output base to 10.
      HEX                                 Sets default numeric input/output base to 16.

  - Sinclair ZX Spectrum Graphics, Sound & I/O:
      PLOT         ( x y -- )             Plots a pixel at coordinate (x, y) [0..255, 0..191].
      DRAW         ( dx dy -- )           Draws a line relative to last plot position.
      CIRCLE       ( x y r -- )           Draws a circle at center (x, y) with radius r.
      INK          ( n -- )               Sets current drawing INK color (0..7).
      PAPER        ( n -- )               Sets current background PAPER color (0..7).
      FLASH        ( n -- )               Enables (1) or disables (0) attribute FLASH.
      BRIGHT       ( n -- )               Enables (1) or disables (0) attribute BRIGHT.
      BORDER       ( n -- )               Sets ULA border color (0..7).
      BEEP         ( duration pitch -- )  Produces a tone of given duration and pitch.
      IN           ( port -- val )        Reads 8-bit byte from Z80 I/O port.
      OUT          ( val port -- )        Writes 8-bit byte to Z80 I/O port.

  - Execution & Return:
      EXECUTE      ( xt -- )              Executes word whose execution token is on stack.
      EXIT                                Exits current definition immediately.

19.6 Colon Definitions, Recursion & User Variables (: ... ;)
------------------------------------------------------------
Users can define custom Forth words, state variables, and recursive procedures:
  - Colon Definitions:
      `: <name> <words...> ;` compiles a new word into the dictionary:
        : DOUBLE 2* ;
        : QUADRUPLE DOUBLE DOUBLE ;
  - Variables & Constants:
      `VARIABLE <name>` allocates a 2-byte storage slot and creates a word that
      pushes its address onto the stack:
        VARIABLE SCORE
        0 SCORE !
        10 SCORE +!
        SCORE @ . CR
      `n CONSTANT <name>` creates a word that pushes the constant value `n`:
        16384 CONSTANT SCREEN
        SCREEN . CR
  - Recursive Words:
      The `RECURSE` word allows a definition to call itself recursively:
        : FACTORIAL
          DUP 2 < IF DROP 1 ELSE DUP 1- RECURSE * THEN ;
        5 FACTORIAL . CR    ( prints 120 )

19.7 Directory Cataloging (DIR & CAT .zxf Detection)
----------------------------------------------------
The file system catalog commands (`DIR` and `CAT`) automatically detect and identify
Sinclair Forth `.zxf` files:
  - Output display:
      `mycode.zxf    [FORTH]   Origin: #C000   Length: 412 bytes`
  - Allows instant identification of Forth machine code programs alongside standard
    `.TAP`, `.ZXB`, and CP/M disk images.


19.8 Listing Compiled Forth & Assembler Blocks: LIST CODE
---------------------------------------------------------
The LIST CODE command provides a dedicated diagnostic listing of all compiled
machine code blocks resident in memory, including inline Sinclair Forth blocks,
inline Z80 assembly blocks, and active Zeus Assembler buffers.

  - Command Syntax:
      LIST CODE
    Can be entered directly at the K > prompt, inside Zeus interactive mode, or
    as an executable statement in BASIC programs.

  - Dual-Pane List Window Display:
      When LIST CODE is executed, the Left Panel List Area (listing_rect) switches
      from displaying standard BASIC program lines to displaying a dedicated,
      color-coded compiled code summary table:
      * Header: Compiled Code Blocks [LIST CODE] (Type LIST to return)
      * Legend: [TYPE] Line/Origin  Address (Hex)  Length (Bytes)
      * Forth Blocks: Rendered in Teal ([FORTH] Line <num>: Addr <dec> (#<hex>)  Len: <bytes> B)
        with exact memory range (Range: #<start>..#<end>) and source code preview.
      * Z80 Assembly Blocks: Rendered in Blue ([Z80] Line <num>: Addr <dec> (#<hex>)  Len: <bytes> B)
        with address range and source instruction preview.
      * Zeus Assembler Buffer: Rendered in Orange/Crimson ([ZEUS] Buffer (<n> lines): Addr <dec> (#<hex>)  Len: <bytes> B)
        with origin address and assembled code length.
      * Footer Summary: Displays the total number of compiled blocks and total byte count.
      * Status Box: The bottom-left status tag displays LIST CODE.

  - Sinclair CRT Canvas Feedback:
      A concise textual summary is simultaneously printed to the Spectrum CRT canvas
      area (canvas_print), informing the user of the address and length of every
      resident block and confirming that full details are visible in the List Area.

  - Automatic On-Demand Compilation:
      If the current BASIC program contains uncompiled FORTH ... END FORTH or
      Z80 ... END Z80 blocks, LIST CODE automatically parses and pre-compiles
      them into native machine code so their memory addresses and byte lengths are
      calculated and reported immediately, even before the program is executed.

  - Navigation & Returning to Program Code:
      * Scrolling: Use the mouse wheel, scroll bar, or keyboard cursor keys within
        the List Area to browse through code blocks.
      * Return: Type LIST (or LIST <line>), VARS, EDIT, or press the ESC
        key to immediately close the code blocks view and return to the standard
        program source listing.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 20. SECTION 19: CRYSTAL COMPUTING COMPATIBLE Z80 MACHINE CODE MONITOR & DISASSEMBLER
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Target Extension:    Crystal Computing Compatible Z80 Machine Code Monitor & Disassembler
Compilation Support: [INTERPRETER ONLY] / [NATIVE Z80 DEBUGGER]
                     (Interactive machine code monitor, disassembler, and single-step debugger.
                     Runs entirely in the Left Panel list window with zero VRAM intrusion, interacting
                     directly with SinclairZ80CPU registers and 64KB memory space).

BasinPy Extended includes an authentic, professional-grade Z80 Machine Code Monitor
and Disassembler. Inspired by and compatible with the classic Crystal Computing Z80
Monitor for the Sinclair ZX Spectrum, this subsystem provides an integrated suite of
tools for inspecting, debugging, disassembling, single-stepping, and assembling native
Z80 machine code programs in real time.

20.1 Architecture & Crystal Computing Compatibility
---------------------------------------------------
The monitor adopts the syntax, single-letter command structure, and operational feel
of the legendary Crystal Computing monitor while integrating deeply with BasinPy's
`SinclairZeusAssembler` and `SinclairZ80CPU` cycle-accurate emulation cores:
  - Full compatibility with classic Crystal commands: `D` (Disassemble), `M` (Memory),
    `R` (Registers), `S` / `T` (Step/Trace), `G` (Go), `A` (Assemble), `F` (Fill),
    `C` (Copy), and `FIND` (Search).
  - Extended with modern debugging enhancements: step-over subroutine execution (`O` /
    `OVER`), persistent address pointers, multi-format numeric parsing, and full
    symbolic label resolution from Zeus assembly projects.

20.2 List Window Output Isolation & Zero-VRAM Intrusion
-------------------------------------------------------
A paramount engineering requirement for retro-system development is display isolation:
  - Left Panel List Window Output:
      All monitor outputs—including disassembly listings, memory dumps, register status
      grids, step traces, and command responses—are rendered exclusively in the Left
      Panel List Window (`listing_rect`).
  - Zero-VRAM Intrusion:
      The ZX Spectrum CRT display (Right Panel), including the 256x192 pixel bitmap
      (0x4000..0x57FF), the 768-byte attribute matrix (0x5800..0x5AFF), and the ULA
      border are 100% isolated and never overwritten by debug text.
  - Pixel-Accurate Visual Debugging:
      Developers can set breakpoints inside sprite plot routines, screen wipes, or
      scrolling engines, single-step instruction by instruction, and observe every
      individual pixel and attribute change directly on the CRT canvas in real time.

20.3 Monitor Invocation: MONITOR [addr], MON [addr], Zeus M, & Breakpoint Trap
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The monitor can be entered through multiple avenues:
  - From Sinclair BASIC Prompt:
      Type `MONITOR [addr]` or `MON [addr]` from the command line:
        MONITOR          : Enters monitor at current PC (defaults to 32768 / 0x8000).
        MONITOR #C000    : Enters monitor and sets inspection pointer to 0xC000.
        MON 49152        : Shorthand alias for MONITOR 49152.
  - From Sinclair Zeus Assembler:
      Type `M [addr]` or `MON [addr]` directly from the Zeus assembler prompt (`Z >`).
  - Automatic Breakpoint Trap:
      Whenever machine code is executed (via `G`, `RUN`, `RANDOMIZE USR`, or Zeus `R`),
      hitting an active breakpoint halts CPU execution immediately, transfers control
      to the Monitor, and sets the prompt to `M >`. The hit address and register state
      are printed automatically.
  - Prompt Status Indicator:
      When active, the lower command prompt displays `M >`, clearly signaling that the
      system is awaiting Monitor commands.

20.4 Multi-Format Value & Expression Parser (#, $, 0x, %, ', labels)
--------------------------------------------------------------------
All monitor commands accept numeric arguments in any standard computing format:
  - Hexadecimal:
      Prefix `#`      : `#C000`, `#FF`, `#1234`
      Prefix `$`      : `$C000`, `$FF`
      Prefix `0x`     : `0xC000`, `0xFF`
      Prefix `&H`     : `&HC000`, `&HFF`
      Suffix `H`      : `C000H`, `0FFH`
      Bare Hex        : `C000`, `B000` (auto-detected when digits A-F are present or
                        in address argument positions).
  - Binary:
      Prefix `%`      : `%11000000`, `%10101010`
      Prefix `0b`     : `0b11000000`
      Suffix `B`      : `10101010B`
  - Decimal:
      Standard integers: `49152`, `32768`, `255`, `0`
  - Character Literals:
      Single-quoted ASCII characters: `'A'` (evaluates to 65), `'*'` (evaluates to 42).
  - Symbolic Labels & Arithmetic Offsets:
      Any label defined in the resident Zeus Assembler environment can be referenced:
        `START`, `LOOP + 4`, `TABLE - 2`

20.5 Register Inspection & Modification: R, R <reg> <val>, <reg>=<val>
----------------------------------------------------------------------
The `R` command provides comprehensive inspection and live editing of all Z80 registers:
  - Register Display (Command `R`):
      Displays a crystal-clear 3-row register status panel in the List Window:
        AF=0044  BC=0000  DE=0000  HL=C000
        AF'=0000 BC'=0000 DE'=0000 HL'=0000 IX=0000 IY=5C3A
        PC=C000  SP=FF20  I=3F R=12 IM=1 IFF=1  Flags: [..H..NC]
  - Register Modification Syntax:
      Registers can be updated using either space-separated or assignment syntax:
        R PC #C000       or   PC=#C000       : Sets Program Counter to 0xC000.
        R HL 32768       or   HL=32768       : Sets HL register pair.
        R A #FF          or   A=255          : Sets 8-bit accumulator A.
        R SP #FF00       or   SP=#FF00       : Sets Machine Stack Pointer.
  - Supported Registers:
      * 16-Bit Pairs   : AF, BC, DE, HL, IX, IY, SP, PC, AF', BC', DE', HL'
      * 8-Bit Registers: A, F, B, C, D, E, H, L, IXH, IXL, IYH, IYL, I, R, IM, IFF
  - Flags Manipulation:
      Individual condition flags can be inspected and toggled:
        * Zero Flag       : `Z=1`, `Z=0`, `NZ`
        * Sign Flag       : `S=1`, `S=0`
        * Carry Flag      : `CF=1`, `CY=1`, `NC` (Carry Flag is explicitly designated
                            `CF` or `CY` to prevent conflict with 8-bit register `C`).
        * Half-Carry Flag : `HF=1`, `HF=0`
        * Parity/Overflow : `PV=1`, `PE` (even), `PO` (odd)
        * Add/Subtract    : `N=1`, `N=0`

20.6 Memory Inspection & Modification: M [addr], M <addr> <bytes...>
--------------------------------------------------------------------
The `M` command allows viewing and editing raw memory bytes anywhere across the 64KB
address space:
  - Memory Dump:
      `M [addr]`
      Displays 64 bytes of memory formatted as 4 rows of 16 bytes. Each row includes
      the 16-bit hexadecimal start address, 16 two-digit hex values, and an authentic
      ASCII character representation:
        C000: 21 00 58 11 00 58 01 00 03 36 47 ED B0 C9 00 00  !.X..X...6G.....
        C010: 3E 02 CD 01 16 21 20 C0 CD 1E C0 C9 48 65 6C 6C  >..! ..!..Hello
        C020: 6F 20 57 6F 72 6C 64 21 0D 00 00 00 00 00 00 00  o World!........
        C030: 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00  ................
      Executing `M` without arguments automatically dumps the next consecutive 64 bytes.
  - Memory Deposit / Direct Editing:
      `M <addr> <byte1> [byte2 ... bn]`
      Deposits one or more bytes directly into memory starting at `<addr>`:
        M #C000 #3E #07 #D3 #FE #C9
        M #5800 7 7 7 7
        M #C010 'H' 'e' 'l' 'l' 'o' 0

20.7 Full Z80 Disassembly Engine: D [addr], DIS [addr]
------------------------------------------------------
The `D` (or `DIS`) command disassembles native machine code into clean, readable Z80
mnemonics:
  - Command Syntax:
      `D [addr]`  or  `DIS [addr]`
      Disassembles 10 instructions starting at `[addr]`. If `[addr]` is omitted, it
      continues from the address following the last disassembled instruction.
  - Display Layout:
      Each disassembled line displays status markers, memory address, hex opcode bytes,
      and formatted Z80 instruction mnemonics:
        > C000  21 00 58     LD HL, 22528
        * C003  11 01 58     LD DE, 22529
          C006  01 FF 02     LD BC, 767
          C009  36 47        LD (HL), 71
          C00B  ED B0        LDIR
          C00D  C9           RET
  - Visual Indicators:
      * Current PC (`>`) : Marks the instruction currently pointed to by the CPU
                           Program Counter.
      * Breakpoint (`*`) : Indicates that an active breakpoint is armed at that address.
  - Complete Z80 Opcode Support:
      Full coverage of base opcodes, CB-prefixed bitwise/shifts, ED-prefixed extended
      instructions, DD/FD index instructions (IX/IY with signed displacements `+d` / `-d`),
      and undocumented opcodes.

20.8 Interactive & Single-Line Assembler: A [addr]
-------------------------------------------------
BasinPy provides direct Z80 assembly directly from within the Monitor:
  - Single-Line Assembly:
      `A <addr> <instruction>`
      Assembles a single instruction directly into memory at `<addr>`:
        A #C000 LD A, 2
        A #C002 OUT (254), A
        A #C004 RET
  - Interactive Assembly Mode:
      `A <addr>`
      Enters an interactive assembly loop. The prompt displays the target memory
      address:
        M > A #C000
        A:C000 > LD HL, #5800
        A:C003 > LD (HL), #47
        A:C005 > RET
        A:C006 >
      As each instruction is entered, it is immediately assembled into memory, the
      target address is automatically incremented by the length of the generated opcode,
      and the prompt advances. Pressing ENTER on a blank line exits interactive
      assembly and returns to `M >`.

20.9 Execution Control & Breakpoints: G, RUN, S, T, O, OVER, B, BC, BL
----------------------------------------------------------------------
The Monitor offers fine-grained execution and debugging controls:
  - Go / Run (`G [addr]` / `RUN [addr]`):
      Runs the CPU at full emulated speed starting from `[addr]` (or current PC) until:
        * A breakpoint address is encountered.
        * A Z80 unhandled exception or halt occurs.
        * The routine reaches its final `RET` and returns cleanly.
  - Single Step / Trace (`S [n]` / `T [n]`):
      Executes exactly `n` machine code instructions (default 1). After each step:
        * The CPU executes the instruction at PC.
        * The updated register status panel is displayed.
        * The next instruction at the new PC is disassembled.
  - Step Over (`O` / `OVER`):
      Steps over subroutine calls and repeating block instructions without tracing
      through their internals:
        * Subroutines: `CALL <addr>`, conditional `CALL cc, <addr>`, `RST n`.
        * Block Operations: `LDIR`, `LDDR`, `CPIR`, `CPDR`, `INIR`, `OTIR`.
      When `OVER` is issued at a call or block instruction, a temporary internal
      breakpoint is armed at the instruction immediately following the call, and
      execution proceeds at full speed. Control returns to the Monitor prompt when
      the subroutine or block transfer completes!
  - Breakpoint Management:
      * `B <addr>`       : Arms an active breakpoint at `<addr>` (e.g. `B #C00B`).
      * `BC <addr>`      : Clears the breakpoint at `<addr>`.
      * `BC *` or `BC`   : Clears all active breakpoints.
      * `BL`             : Lists all currently armed breakpoints with memory addresses.

20.10 Memory Block Utilities: FILL (F), COPY (C), FIND
------------------------------------------------------
High-speed memory manipulation tools for bulk data handling:
  - Memory Fill (`F <start> <end> <val>`):
      Fills the address range from `<start>` to `<end>` inclusive with byte `<val>`:
        F #5800 #5AFF #47     (Clears entire attribute screen to White on Black)
        F #8000 #8FFF #00     (Zeros out a 4KB memory buffer)
  - Memory Copy (`C <src> <dest> <len>`):
      Copies `<len>` contiguous bytes from `<src>` address to `<dest>` address:
        C #C000 #D000 512     (Copies 512 bytes from 0xC000 to 0xD000)
      Correctly handles overlapping memory blocks using non-destructive direction logic
      identical to Z80 `LDIR` (forward) and `LDDR` (backward) transfers.
  - Memory Search (`FIND <start> <end> <bytes...>`):
      Scans memory between `<start>` and `<end>` for an exact sequence of bytes or
      text characters:
        FIND #4000 #FFFF #ED #B0         (Finds all LDIR instructions in memory)
        FIND #8000 #C000 'H' 'e' 'l' 'l' (Finds text string "Hell")
      Outputs every memory address where the matching pattern was located.

20.11 Navigation & Mode Switching: ZEUS / Z, BASIC / EXIT / QUIT / BYE
----------------------------------------------------------------------
Seamless transitions between BasinPy environments:
  - Switch to Zeus Assembler:
      `ZEUS` or `Z` transitions directly to the Sinclair Zeus Assembler mode (`Z >`),
      preserving all memory, registers, and symbol tables.
  - Return to Sinclair BASIC:
      `BASIC`, `EXIT`, `QUIT`, or `BYE` returns smoothly to the BasinPy Sinclair
      BASIC prompt (`>`). All resident BASIC programs, variables, and VRAM remain
      fully intact.



--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 21. SECTION 20: ZX SPECTRANET & SPECTRANEXT NETWORK SUBSYSTEM
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Target Extension:    ZX Spectranet & Spectranext Network Subsystem
Compilation Support: [INTERPRETER ONLY]
                     (Ethernet and WiFi network emulation, TNFS, IRC, Gopher, VT100 terminal, and
                     extended BASIC % network commands execute within the BasinPy host runtime).

BasinPy Extended incorporates comprehensive network emulation bringing authentic
Ethernet and WiFi connectivity to the Sinclair ZX Spectrum. Combining full support
for the classic ZX Spectranet (Wiznet W5100 Ethernet interface) and the modern
ZX Spectrum Next (ESP8266 / ESP32 WiFi UART coprocessor), BasinPy enables Sinclair
BASIC programs, Z80 machine code, and retro applications to communicate seamlessly
with local networks and the global Internet via host Python sockets and DNS.

21.1 Architecture & Host Internet Integration
---------------------------------------------
The network subsystem bridges retro ZX Spectrum network protocols directly to the
host machine's real-time Internet connection:
  - Spectranet W5100 Hardware Emulation:
      Faithful emulation of the Wiznet W5100 hardwired TCP/IP chip mapped into the
      ZX Spectrum's memory space, providing 4 independent hardware socket channels,
      direct socket control, and CPLD memory bank switching.
  - Spectranext Virtual ESP8266 WiFi Coprocessor:
      Full bidirectional UART emulation supporting standard Hayes AT WiFi commands,
      transparent socket bridging, and dynamic Baud rate communication.
  - Transparent Host Internet Access:
      Domain name queries (DNS), TCP socket connections, and UDP packet streams
      resolve directly through host operating system network stacks with zero external
      drivers or complex bridge TAP adapters required.
  - Retro Application Suite:
      Built-in native network applications accessible directly from BASIC:
      TNFS network drive mounting, full-screen TNFS server & browser, IRC chat client,
      Gopher information browser, and VT100/ANSI terminal emulator.

21.2 Spectranet Hardware Specification & Register Map
-----------------------------------------------------
The Spectranet hardware interface is mapped directly into ZX Spectrum I/O ports and
the lower 16KB address space:
  - CPLD Paging Control Port: Port #003B (59)
      Controls memory paging and trap execution:
      * Bit 0: Trap Disable (0 = CPLD traps enabled, 1 = traps disabled)
      * Bit 1: Page A Disable (0 = Page A W5100 mapped at 0x1000..0x1FFF, 1 = disabled)
      * Bit 2: Page B Disable (0 = Page B RAM mapped at 0x2000..0x2FFF, 1 = disabled)
      * Bits 5..7: Page B RAM Bank Select (0..7, selecting one of eight 4KB SRAM banks)
  - Page A Memory Window: 0x1000..0x1FFF (4KB)
      Direct access to Wiznet W5100 internal registers and socket buffers:
      * 0x1000: Mode Register (MR)
      * 0x1001..0x1004: Gateway Address Register (GAR)
      * 0x1005..0x1008: Subnet Mask Register (SUBR)
      * 0x1009..0x100E: Source Hardware MAC Address Register (SHAR)
      * 0x100F..0x1012: Source IP Address Register (SIPR)
      * 0x1400..0x17FF: Socket 0..3 Control, Status, Port, and TX/RX Buffer Registers
  - Page B Memory Window: 0x2000..0x2FFF (4KB)
      Provides 32KB of paged static RAM organized as eight 4KB banks (banks 0..7).
  - CPLD Trap Vector: CALL 0x3FF8..0x3FFF
      The Spectranet CPLD hardware traps Z80 `CALL 0x3FF8`..`0x3FFF` instructions.
      When executed by machine code, the trap vector inspects register `C` to identify
      the high-level HGF (Hardware Generated Function) socket operation:
      * C = 0x01: HGF_SOCKET         - Allocates a network socket descriptor in A
      * C = 0x03: HGF_CONNECT        - Connects socket to remote IP/Port
      * C = 0x06: HGF_SEND           - Transmits data buffer over socket
      * C = 0x07: HGF_RECV           - Receives pending incoming socket data into buffer
      * C = 0x08: HGF_CLOSE          - Closes active socket descriptor
      * C = 0x0A: HGF_GETHOSTBYNAME  - Resolves domain name string at (HL) to 4-byte IP at (DE)
      * C = 0x0B: HGF_IFCONFIG       - Configures or queries adapter IP settings
      * C = 0x0C: HGF_DHCP           - Initiates DHCP lease configuration
      Upon trap completion, the carry flag is cleared (NC = success), return parameters
      are stored in CPU registers, and the CPU resumes cleanly via a hardware `RET`.

21.3 Spectranext Hardware Specification & Hayes AT Engine
---------------------------------------------------------
The ZX Spectrum Next network architecture utilizes an onboard ESP8266 or ESP32
coprocessor communicating over high-speed hardware UART ports:
  - UART Data Port: Port #143B (5179)
      * OUT #143B, val : Transmits a character byte to the ESP coprocessor TX FIFO.
      * IN #143B       : Reads the next received character byte from the ESP RX FIFO.
  - UART Control / Status Port: Port #153B (5435)
      * Read Status Bits:
          - Bit 0: TX Ready (1 = FIFO ready to accept outgoing characters)
          - Bit 1: RX Ready (1 = FIFO contains incoming received characters)
      * Write Control: Configures UART FIFO flow control and line resets.
  - Hayes AT Command Interpreter:
      The virtual ESP coprocessor interprets standard AT command sequences:
      * `AT`                           : Basic ping, returns OK
      * `AT+CWMODE=1`                  : Sets station / client Wi-Fi mode
      * `AT+CWJAP="SSID","PASS"`       : Connects to Wi-Fi access point
      * `AT+CWJAP?`                    : Returns active connected network SSID
      * `AT+CIPSTATUS`                 : Returns current socket connection status
      * `AT+CIPSTART=0,"TCP","host",pt`: Opens TCP socket stream connection to remote host
      * `AT+CIPSEND=0,length`          : Initiates payload transmission of specified length
      * `AT+CIPCLOSE=0`                : Closes active socket connection

21.4 Sinclair BASIC Stream I/O over Network Channels
----------------------------------------------------
BasinPy extends Sinclair ZX Spectrum stream addressing with native network device channels,
enabling direct socket communication using standard `OPEN #`, `PRINT #`, `INPUT #`,
and `CLOSE #` statements:
  - Syntax:
      `OPEN #channel, "n>tcp:host:port"`
      `OPEN #channel, "n>udp:host:port"`
      `OPEN #channel, "net:host:port"`
  - Channel Operations:
      * `PRINT #channel; "GET / HTTP/1.0", CHR$ 13, CHR$ 10`
          Transmits text or binary data directly to the connected remote host.
      * `INPUT #channel, response$`
          Reads a line of incoming response data from the network stream.
      * `INKEY$ #channel`
          Inspects the next available incoming character without blocking.
      * `CLOSE #channel`
          Gracefully shuts down the socket stream and releases the channel descriptor.
  - Example - Fetching a Web Page in Pure BASIC:
      10 OPEN #4, "n>tcp:example.com:80"
      20 PRINT #4; "GET / HTTP/1.0"; CHR$ 13; CHR$ 10; "Host: example.com"; CHR$ 13; CHR$ 10; CHR$ 13; CHR$ 10
      30 IF INKEY$ #4 = "" THEN GO TO 30
      40 INPUT #4, a$: PRINT a$: IF a$ <> "" THEN GO TO 40
      50 CLOSE #4

21.5 Spectranet Extended BASIC % Commands
-------------------------------------------
BasinPy provides full support for the standard Spectranet `%` prefix syntax directly
from the Sinclair BASIC prompt and inside stored BASIC programs. For maximum ease of use,
all Spectranet commands may also be entered directly without the `%` prefix (e.g., `MOUNT`,
`FS`, `PING`, `DHCP`, `SETHW`), and network suite commands like `TNFS`, `IRC`, `GOPHER` / `GOPER`,
`TERM`, and `NET` can be typed either bare or with the `%` prefix (e.g., `%TNFS`, `%IRC`,
`%GOPHER`, `%NET`):
  - Remote Filesystem Mounting:
      `%mount <drive>, "<host>" [, "<path>"]`
      Mounts a remote TNFS server as a local network drive (drives 0..3):
        %mount 0, "tnfs.zxnet.co.uk", "/"
        %mount 1, "192.168.1.50", "/speccy"
  - Unmounting Filesystems:
      `%umount [drive]`
      Unmounts the specified network drive (default drive 0):
        %umount 0
  - Filesystem Status:
      `%fs`
      Lists all currently mounted TNFS network drives, hostnames, and paths.
  - Cataloging Network Drives:
      `%cat [drive]`
      Catalogs files and directories resident on the specified remote TNFS drive:
        %cat 0
  - Directory Navigation:
      `%cd "<path>"`
      Changes the active working directory on the primary mounted network drive:
        %cd "/games/adventures"
  - Network File Download:
      `%wget "<url>"`
      Downloads a remote file over HTTP/TCP directly to local storage:
        %wget "http://zxnet.co.uk/games/manic.tap"
  - Host Connectivity Check:
      `%ping "<host>"`
      Sends an ICMP/TCP echo request to verify connectivity and display network latency:
        %ping "8.8.8.8"
        %ping "google.com"
  - DHCP & Network Configuration:
      `%dhcp on`  or  `%dhcp off`
      Enables or disables automatic IP configuration via DHCP.
  - Adapter Status:
      `%ifconfig`  or  `%netstat`
      Displays the active network configuration (IP, Subnet, Gateway, DNS, MAC).

21.6 Network Hardware & Interface Configuration: NET, IFCONFIG, NETCFG
----------------------------------------------------------------------
Dedicated commands for configuring and viewing hardware network parameters:
  - View Network Configuration:
      `NET STATUS`  or  `IFCONFIG`  or  `NETCFG`
      Displays full hardware adapter details:
        --- BasinPy Network Status ---
        Hardware: Spectranet + Next WiFi
        MAC:      00:03:19:00:00:01
        IP:       192.168.1.100
        Mask:     255.255.255.0
        Gateway:  192.168.1.1
        DNS:      8.8.8.8
        DHCP:     Enabled
        Spectranet: W5100 + Traps Active
        Next WiFi:  Ports #143B/#153B Active
  - Modify Adapter Settings:
      `NET SET "<key>", "<value>"`
      Configures specific network parameters and automatically synchronizes the W5100
      hardware registers:
        NET SET "IP", "192.168.1.200"
        NET SET "GATEWAY", "192.168.1.254"
        NET SET "DNS", "1.1.1.1"
        NET SET "DHCP", "OFF"
  - Network Ping Utility:
      `NET PING "<host>"`
      Tests reachability of a remote IP address or domain name:
        NET PING "1.1.1.1"

21.7 TNFS (Total Network File System) Host Server & Browser: TNFS
-----------------------------------------------------------------
TNFS is the premier network filesystem protocol for 8-bit retro computers, operating
over UDP port 16384. BasinPy includes both a built-in TNFS host server and a full-featured
interactive visual client:
  - Interactive Visual TNFS Browser:
      `TNFS [host] [port]`
      Launches the full-screen visual file browser (defaults to `tnfs.zxnet.co.uk:16384`):
        TNFS
        TNFS "192.168.1.50"
        TNFS "tnfs.bytemaniacos.com", 16384
  - Browser Controls:
      * Up Arrow / Q        : Move cursor up one file
      * Down Arrow / A      : Move cursor down one file
      * Page Up / Page Down : Scroll listing by full page (16 items)
      * Enter               : Open subdirectory, or download and instantly auto-run `.tap` / `.bas`
      * U / Backspace       : Navigate to parent directory (Up)
      * M                   : Mount current remote directory as drive 0 (`%fs`)
      * Esc                 : Exit browser and return cleanly to Sinclair BASIC prompt
  - Built-in Local TNFS Server:
      `TNFS SERVER [port] [folder]`
      Starts an integrated, multi-threaded TNFS file server on the host machine:
        TNFS SERVER
        TNFS SERVER 16384 "C:\zx\games"
      The server immediately begins servicing incoming UDP requests from BasinPy,
      physical ZX Spectrum Next machines, or other emulators on your local network!

21.8 Spectrum IRC Chat Client: IRC
----------------------------------
BasinPy includes a native, full-featured Internet Relay Chat client formatted for the
classic ZX Spectrum display:
  - Command Syntax:
      `IRC [host] [port] [channel] [nick]`
      Connects to an IRC network (defaults to `irc.libera.chat:6667`, `#basinpy`, `SpeccyUser`):
        IRC
        IRC "irc.libera.chat", 6667, "#spectrum", "RetroCoder"
  - User Interface:
      * 21-line scrolling chat history display with authentic timestamping and colored nicks.
      * Status header displaying connected server, port, and active channel.
      * Live command input bar at the bottom with cursor and text editing.
  - Interactive Controls:
      * Type chat messages and press Enter to send directly to the channel.
      * IRC Commands supported:
          - `/JOIN <channel>` : Switch or join a new IRC channel
          - `/NICK <newnick>` : Change current nickname
          - `/ME <action>`   : Send CTCP action message
          - `/QUIT` or Esc   : Disconnect and exit back to Sinclair BASIC

21.9 Spectrum Gopher Information Browser: GOPHER / GOPER
--------------------------------------------------------
Browse Gopherspace directly from BasinPy using the built-in RFC 1436 Gopher client:
  - Command Syntax:
      `GOPHER [host] [port] [selector]`  (or `GOPER`)
      Connects to a Gopher server (defaults to `gopher.floodgap.com:70`, `/`):
        GOPHER
        GOPER
        GOPHER "gopher.floodgap.com"
        GOPHER "sdf.org", 70, "/"
  - Navigation & Controls:
      * Displays formatted menus with item numbers, folder markers `[DIR]`, and text markers `[TXT]`.
      * Up Arrow / Q        : Scroll page up
      * Down Arrow / A      : Scroll page down
      * Space               : Jump down half page
      * 0..9 digits + Enter : Select and navigate to numbered menu item
      * U / B / Backspace   : Navigate back to previous menu in history
      * Esc                 : Exit Gopher browser and return to Sinclair BASIC

21.10 Spectrum VT100 / ANSI Terminal Emulator: TERM
---------------------------------------------------
Connect to BBS systems, MUDs, telnet hosts, and Unix shells with the built-in
VT100 / ANSI terminal emulator:
  - Command Syntax:
      `TERM [host] [port]`
      Opens a live telnet terminal session (defaults to `telehack.com:23`):
        TERM
        TERM "telehack.com", 23
        TERM "bbs.fozztexx.com", 23
  - Features:
      * Automatic Telnet IAC (Interpret As Command) negotiation and option suppression.
      * Full ANSI/VT100 CSI escape sequence engine: cursor positioning, colors, text
        attributes, and screen clearing (`\033[2J`).
      * 32x24 character terminal matrix with automatic line wrapping and vertical scrolling.
      * Bidirectional keyboard streaming: Enter, Backspace, arrow keys (`\033[A`..`\033[D`),
        and ASCII characters sent immediately.
      * Esc exits terminal emulation and restores the Sinclair BASIC prompt.


================================================================================
 22. SECTION 21: SINCLAIR ZX INTERFACE 2 ROM CARTRIDGE & MULTIFACE POKE ENGINE
================================================================================

Target Extension:    Sinclair ZX Interface 2 ROM Cartridge & Multiface POKE Engine
Compilation Support: [INTERPRETER ONLY] / [HARDWARE ROM EMULATION]
                     (Interface 2 16KB ROM cartridge mapping, Multiface non-destructive break,
                     and real-time memory PEEK/POKE engines execute directly within the emulator).

22.1 Interface 2 Hardware Architecture & Cartridge ROM Mapping
--------------------------------------------------------------
The Sinclair ZX Interface 2 was released in September 1983, adding dual joystick
ports and an authentic ROM cartridge slot to the ZX Spectrum 16K/48K.

In hardware, inserting a ROM cartridge asserts the `/ROMCS` line on the expansion
bus, which disables the internal 16KB Sinclair 48K BASIC ROM and maps the cartridge
ROM directly into memory space:
    Address Range: 0x0000 .. 0x3FFF (0 to 16383, 16384 bytes)

BasinPy accurately emulates this bus override:
  - When a cartridge is inserted or loaded, memory addresses 0x0000..0x3FFF are
    swapped to the cartridge contents.
  - BasinPy maintains an untouched, pristine backup of the authentic Sinclair 48K
    BASIC ROM (`basic_rom_backup`).
  - Ejecting the cartridge (`CARTRIDGE EJECT`) restores the Sinclair 48K BASIC ROM
    without loss of data or integrity.
  - System variables and character fonts are managed consistently so BASIC can
    resume whenever the cartridge is removed.


22.2 Cartridge File Formats (.rom and .bin) & 8KB/16KB Mirroring
----------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy supports standard Interface 2 cartridge image files with `.rom` and `.bin`
extensions:
  - 16KB Cartridges (16,384 bytes):
      Standard 16KB commercial cartridge titles (e.g. Jetpac, Pssst, Tranz Am,
      Cookie) map directly across 0x0000..0x3FFF.
  - 8KB Cartridges (<= 8,192 bytes) & Address Mirroring:
      Titles distributed on 8KB ROM chips (such as Planetoids and Space Raiders)
      did not connect address line A13 on the Interface 2 PCB. BasinPy replicates
      this authentic hardware mirroring by mapping the 8KB image to 0x0000..0x1FFF
      and repeating it across 0x2000..0x3FFF.


22.3 Loading & Saving Cartridges: LOAD and SAVE
----------------------------------------------
Cartridge files can be loaded and saved from both physical host disk storage and
the internal BasinPy RAM Disk:

1. Loading Cartridge ROMs:
   - Explicit syntax:
       LOAD "game.rom", ROM
       LOAD "game.bin", ROM
   - Automatic extension recognition:
       LOAD "jetpac.rom"
       LOAD "planetoids.bin"
   - Loading automatically installs the cartridge data into 0x0000..0x3FFF and
     triggers a system reset (`RESET`) to boot and execute the cartridge instantly.
   - RAM Disk loading:
       LOAD !"cartridge", ROM
       Transfers 16KB cartridge image from RAM disk directly into cartridge ROM.

2. Saving Cartridge ROMs:
   - Explicit syntax:
       SAVE "cartridge.rom", ROM
       SAVE "dump.bin", ROM
   - RAM Disk saving:
       SAVE !"mycart", ROM
   - Saves 16,384 bytes representing the current cartridge ROM area (0x0000..0x3FFF).

3. File Directory:
   - The `DIR` command automatically includes `.rom` and `.bin` cartridge images
     alongside `.tap`, `.zxb`, `.mdr`, and disk files.


22.4 Zeus Machine Code Integration & Cartridge Swapping
-------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy enables developers to build custom ROM cartridges directly from the
built-in Zeus Z80 Assembler:

1. Assembling Directly into Cartridge:
   - Enter Zeus Assembler: `Z80`
   - Enter your assembly source code (with `ORG 0` or standard entry points).
   - Type `C` or `CARTRIDGE`:
       Assembles the Zeus buffer and immediately copies the resulting machine
       code into the Interface 2 Cartridge ROM slot (0x0000..0x3FFF).
   - Type `RESET` to boot and run your cartridge from address 0x0000!

2. Direct Cartridge Swap Command:
   - `CARTRIDGE SWAP`:
       Transfers the current Zeus assembled machine code into 0x0000..0x3FFF.
   - `CARTRIDGE SWAP <addr> [, <len>]`:
       Copies arbitrary machine code already in RAM (starting at `<addr>`) into
       the Cartridge ROM slot.
       Example:
         CARTRIDGE SWAP 32768, 8192
   - `CARTRIDGE EJECT`:
       Ejects the cartridge and restores the Sinclair 48K BASIC ROM.
   - `CARTRIDGE`:
       Displays cartridge status, active file name, and occupied byte count.


22.5 System Reset & Cartridge Boot: RESET Command
-------------------------------------------------
The `RESET` (or `BOOT`) command performs a full hardware reset of the Z80 CPU:
  - Resets all Z80 registers: AF=0, BC=0, DE=0, HL=0, IX=0, IY=0.
  - Resets stack pointer: SP=0xFFFE.
  - Sets interrupt mode: IM 1, IFF1=0, IFF2=0.
  - Sets program counter: PC=0x0000.

Boot Behavior:
  - If a cartridge is inserted:
      Clears screen VRAM and border, boots immediately from address 0x0000,
      and runs the cartridge machine code.
  - If no cartridge is inserted:
      Clears memory and variables (`perform_new`), clears the screen, restores
      the Sinclair copyright message (`(C) 1982 Sinclair Research Ltd`), and
      returns to the Sinclair BASIC prompt.


22.6 Multiface Non-Destructive Break Engine
-------------------------------------------
BasinPy includes a non-destructive Multiface POKE engine modeled after the Romantic
Robot Multiface 1, 128, and 3 peripherals.

1. Non-Destructive ESC Break:
   - While any machine code (via `USR`, `RESET`, Zeus `R`, or monitor `G`) or BASIC
     program is executing, pressing `ESC` immediately interrupts execution.
   - Machine code enters a suspended state with a status message:
       `[MULTIFACE BREAK at PC #XXXX] - CONT to resume`

2. Zero Corruption Guarantee:
   - Unlike standard emulator stops, the Multiface Break guarantees ZERO corruption:
       * Z80 Registers: `AF`, `BC`, `DE`, `HL`, `IX`, `IY`, `PC`, `SP`, and shadow
         registers (`AF'`, `BC'`, `DE'`, `HL'`) remain 100% unaltered.
       * Hardware Flags & Interrupt State: `IFF1`, `IFF2`, `IM` preserved.
       * Memory & Stack: Machine code call frames and stack bytes remain intact.
       * Screen & VRAM: Display memory (0x4000..0x57FF) and attribute memory
         (0x5800..0x5AFF) remain preserved.


22.7 Multiface POKE Engine: Multi-Byte & String Literals
--------------------------------------------------------
While machine code or a BASIC game is suspended, trainers, cheats, and memory
modifications can be applied with the extended `POKE` command:

1. Single-Byte POKE:
     POKE 32768, 201

2. Multi-Byte POKE:
     Enter multiple comma-separated byte values to write consecutive addresses:
     Syntax:
       `POKE <start_addr>, <val1>, <val2>, <val3>, ...`
     Example:
       POKE 23296, 0, 0, 0, 201
     Writes bytes sequentially starting from address 23296.

3. String Literals in POKE:
     Embed ASCII text strings directly inside POKE statements:
     Example:
       POKE 30000, "INFINITE LIVES", 0
     Each character's ASCII byte value is written to consecutive memory addresses,
     followed by the null terminator byte 0.


22.8 Multiface PEEK & PEKK Inspection Commands
----------------------------------------------
Inspect memory at any time from the command prompt or inside programs:

1. Single Address PEEK:
     Syntax:
       `PEEK <addr>`
     Example:
       PEEK 23296
     Output format:
       `PEEK 23296 (0x5B00) = 201 (0xC9) 'E'`
     Displays decimal address, hexadecimal address, decimal byte value, hexadecimal
     byte value, and printable ASCII character.

2. Block Dump with PEEK or PEKK:
     Syntax:
       `PEEK <addr>, <count>`  or  `PEKK <addr>, <count>`
     Example:
       PEEK 16384, 64
     Output format:
       Produces an authentic 16-byte-per-line formatted hexadecimal and ASCII dump:
       4000: FF 00 FF 00 AA 55 AA 55 01 02 03 04 05 06 07 08  ..U.U..........


22.9 Machine Code & BASIC Resumption: CONT / CONTINUE
-----------------------------------------------------
Once memory modifications or inspections are finished, resume execution seamlessly:
  - Command:
      `CONT`  or  `CONTINUE`
  - Resumption Behavior:
      * If machine code was suspended by a Multiface break (`can_continue_mc`),
         execution resumes immediately from the exact preserved `PC` address with
         all registers and stack intact.
      * If the machine code was called from a Sinclair BASIC program (e.g. `RANDOMIZE USR 32768`),
         when the machine code eventually executes `RET`, execution seamlessly
         cascades into resuming the parent BASIC program!
      * If an interrupted BASIC program was suspended (`can_continue`), it resumes
         at the interrupted line and statement index.


================================================================================
 23. SECTION 22: SINCLAIR ZX FILE COMMANDER & MULTI-MEDIA MANAGER
================================================================================

Target Extension:    Sinclair ZX File Commander & Multi-Media Manager
Compilation Support: [INTERPRETER ONLY]
                     (Two-panel file manager, disk browsing, and tape/cartridge manipulation
                     rendered on the 32x24 CRT display with mouse and Lotus menu controls).

BasinPy Extended features an authentic Sinclair ZX Spectrum 2-panel file
commander (styled in the tradition of classic PC Norton Commander and Volkov
Commander) rendered directly within the Sinclair Spectrum 256x192 CRT display
window (32 columns by 24 rows).

The file commander provides unified cross-media navigation, batch inspection,
and file operations across all physical and virtual media supported by the
Sinclair ZX Spectrum ecosystem, complete with AMX mouse pointer interaction
and a classic Lotus 1-2-3 hierarchical command line.


23.1 Classic 2-Panel Norton Commander Display Architecture
----------------------------------------------------------
The commander divides the ZX Spectrum 32x24 character screen into two
independent navigation panels (Left Panel: columns 0..14; Right Panel:
columns 16..30) separated by vertical border lines (column 15 and column 31).

- Visual Palette & Color Scheme:
  * Norton Commander Classic Blue: Screen background Paper 1 (Blue), Bright 1,
    with White (Ink 7) and Cyan (Ink 6) borders and file listings.
  * Active Panel Header: Highlighted in Paper 5 (Cyan), Ink 0 (Black) displaying
    the active drive/media designation and current container path.
  * Passive Panel Header: Displayed in Paper 1 (Blue), Ink 7 (White).
  * Selected Cursor Row: Displayed in inverse video (Paper 7 White, Ink 0 Black)
    showing filename, file size, and file type.
  * Panel Layout:
    Row 0: Quick-mount buttons ([DISK], [MDR], [TAPE], [NET]) and Menu ([/:Men])
    Row 1: Panel media headers ([L:<MEDIA>] and [R:<MEDIA>])
    Row 2: Header column titles (Name, Size, Type)
    Rows 3..18: File entry list with automatic scrolling window
    Row 19: Panel footer summary displaying total file count and free space
    Row 20: Command status and notification line (e.g. "Copied HELLO to MDR1")
    Row 21: Command prompt line showing current path and input buffer
    Row 22: Function action bar: [F3:Inf] [F5:Cpy] [F6:Mov] [F7:Del]
    Row 23: Help & Exit button: [/:Menu] [TAB:Panel] [F10:Quit]


23.2 Multi-Media Support: Tape, Microdrive, Floppy Disk, RAM Disk, Host, Network
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The file commander unifies diverse physical storage media into a consistent
catalog and payload abstraction:

1. Host Filesystem (HOST):
   - Accesses standard host files in BasinPy ZX folder (`get_zx_folder()`) and
     the current working directory (`os.getcwd()`).
   - Displays real host file sizes, extensions, and modification attributes.

2. 128K RAM Silicon Disk (RAM):
   - Accesses BasinPy's high-speed in-memory RAM disk (`ram_disk`).
   - Displays program type (BASIC, CODE, DATA), memory length, start address,
     and autorun line number.

3. MGT +D / DISCiPLE Floppy Disk (DSK1 & DSK2):
   - Accesses 720KB/800KB `.dsk` and `.fdi` MGT floppy disk drives 1 and 2.
   - Decodes MGT directory sectors, sector allocation bitmaps, file types
     (BASIC, CODE, SCREEN, DATA), execution addresses, and remaining free sectors.

4. Sinclair ZX Interface 1 Microdrive (MDR1..MDR8):
   - Accesses up to 8 virtual Interface 1 Microdrive cartridges (`.mdr` images).
   - Traverses 254-sector cartridge headers, checksums, file record flags,
     and sector chains.

5. Sinclair ZX Tape Containers (TAPE):
   - Browses blocks inside `.tap` tape containers.
   - Inspects block headers (Program, Number Array, Character Array, Code/Screen$),
     header parameters (length, start address, variable length), and raw data blocks.

6. Network Stations & Spectranet TNFS (NET):
   - Browses Interface 1 ZX Net stations (`net_station{id}.net`) and connects
     to remote Spectranet TNFS (Total Network File System) hosts for downloading
     and uploading files.


23.3 File Operations: Copy, Move, Erase/Delete, Details & Hex/ASCII Dump
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
File operations seamlessly translate between different media structures:

- File Details / Hex Dump (F3 / 'I' / Info):
  Pops up an authentic modal overlay displaying:
  * Full filename, file type, file size in bytes.
  * Start memory address (hex & decimal) and BASIC autorun line (if present).
  * 16-to-32 byte formatted hexadecimal and printable ASCII memory dump.
  * Press any key, click outside, or press Space/Enter to dismiss.

- Cross-Media File Copy (F5 / 'C' / Copy):
  Copies the highlighted file from the active panel to the destination media
  in the passive panel.
  * Payloads are dynamically translated (e.g. tape block to RAM disk entry,
    RAM file to MGT disk file, host binary to Microdrive sectors).
  * Prompts for target destination filename (pre-filled with source name).

- Cross-Media File Move (F6 / 'M' / Move):
  Transfers the selected file to the opposite panel and deletes it from the
  origin media once written successfully.

- Erase / Delete File (F7 / 'D' / Delete):
  Opens an authentic confirmation dialog ("Delete <name>? [Y] Yes [N] No").
  Permanently removes the file from the media when confirmed.


23.4 Interface 1 Net & Spectranet TNFS Remote File Transfers
------------------------------------------------------------
The commander provides integrated network access:

- Interface 1 ZX Net:
  * Selecting `NET` media scans the network folder for active ZX Net station
    packets (`net_station1.net` .. `net_station64.net`).
  * Copying or moving files to a Station entry writes the packet into the
    station's mailbox for transmission to remote network clients.

- Spectranet TNFS:
  * Mounting TNFS connects through the built-in `TNFSClient` protocol engine.
  * Allows browsing remote file directories on internet or local TNFS servers.
  * Remote files can be downloaded directly into RAM disk, Microdrive, or Floppy,
    or uploaded from Spectrum media to the remote server.


23.5 On-Screen Toolbar Buttons & Media Mounting
-----------------------------------------------
Row 0 provides instant clickable buttons:
  - `[DISK]`: Cycles the active panel through Floppy Drive 1 (`DSK1`) and Drive 2 (`DSK2`).
  - `[MDR]`: Cycles the active panel through Microdrive cartridges (`MDR1`..`MDR8`).
  - `[TAPE]`: Mounts the tape container browser for `.tap` files.
  - `[NET]`: Mounts the network browser (Interface 1 Net and Spectranet TNFS).
  - `[/:Men]`: Activates the Lotus 1-2-3 style top menu bar.

Clicking on the panel headers `[L:<MEDIA>]` or `[R:<MEDIA>]` opens a full media
picker dialog allowing instant selection of Host, RAM, DSK1, DSK2, MDR1..MDR8,
TAPE, or NET.


23.6 AMX Mouse Integration & Graphical Interaction
--------------------------------------------------
The commander features full hardware AMX Mouse integration:
- AMX Mouse Pointer:
  Automatically turns on the hardware AMX pointer sprite on the Spectrum CRT
  display upon entering the commander.
- Panel Switching:
  Clicking anywhere inside the left or right file list activates that panel.
- File Selection & Inspection:
  * Single-clicking an entry selects it and moves the highlight bar.
  * Double-clicking an already-selected file immediately opens the File Details /
    Hex Dump modal inspection window.
- Bottom Action Bar:
  Clicking `[F3:Inf]`, `[F5:Cpy]`, `[F6:Mov]`, `[F7:Del]`, or `[F10:Quit]`
  executes the corresponding operation.


23.7 Lotus 1-2-3 Style Keyboard Command System (/)
--------------------------------------------------
Pressing the `/` (slash) key activates a classic Lotus 1-2-3 command menu across
the top two rows of the Spectrum screen:

- Menu Bar Structure:
  Row 0: `/ Files Disk Microdrive Tape Network View Quit`
  Row 1: Dynamic explanatory sentence describing the highlighted command:
    * Files:      "Copy, Move, Erase or View file details"
    * Disk:       "Mount MGT Floppy Disk 1 or 2"
    * Microdrive: "Select Sinclair Microdrive 1 to 8"
    * Tape:       "Mount Sinclair Tape (.TAP) container"
    * Network:    "Access IF1 Net or Spectranet TNFS"
    * View:       "Hexadecimal and ASCII file inspection"
    * Quit:       "Exit Sinclair ZX File Commander"

- Navigation & Shortcuts:
  * Arrow Keys: `LEFT` and `RIGHT` navigate horizontally across menu items;
    `ENTER` executes the highlighted action.
  * Single-Letter Shortcuts: Pressing `F`, `D`, `M`, `T`, `N`, `V`, or `Q`
    instantly triggers that action.
  * Exit Menu: Pressing `ESC` or `BREAK` dismisses the menu bar and returns to
    the standard 2-panel file list.


23.8 BASIC Commands & Immediate Mode Invocation
-----------------------------------------------
The Sinclair ZX File Commander can be launched from the BasinPy command prompt
or executed as a statement within Sinclair BASIC programs:

Syntax:
  `COMMANDER [left_media [, right_media]]`
  `NC [left_media [, right_media]]`
  `FC [left_media [, right_media]]`
  `FILEMAN [left_media [, right_media]]`
  `PANEL [left_media [, right_media]]`

Examples:
  `NC`                     - Open Commander with default panels (HOST and RAM)
  `COMMANDER "DSK1", "RAM"`- Open Commander with Floppy Drive 1 on Left and RAM Disk on Right
  `NC "MDR1", "DSK1"`      - Open Commander with Microdrive 1 on Left and Floppy on Right
  `FC "TAPE", "RAM"`       - Open Commander with Tape container on Left and RAM Disk on Right

Exiting the commander (via `F10`, `ESC`, or `Quit`) cleanly restores the previous
Sinclair Spectrum CRT screen buffer, border color, and AMX mouse pointer visibility.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 24. SECTION 23: BASINPY HARDWARE-STYLE SPRITE SUBSYSTEM (BASIC & ASSEMBLY)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Target Extension:    BasinPy Extended Custom Subsystem
Compilation Support: [CUSTOM COMPILER TRANSPILATION] / [48K STANDALONE TAP COMPATIBLE]
                     (High-level SPRITE statements are transpiled by BasinPy's multi-pass
                     compiler into Sinclair BASIC POKE, PEEK, and USR calls. Preloaded
                     sprite graphics are extracted into a compact sp_data binary block.
                     The program compiles to native Z80 machine code targeting address 24500
                     via Boriel's ZXBC, and bundles the relocatable Z80 sprite engine at
                     address 50000 into a self-contained, autostarting 48K ZX Spectrum .TAP file).

BasinPy Extended includes an authentic, hardware-accelerated 16-slot Sprite Engine
modeled after classic arcade sprite chips (e.g. Texas Instruments TMS9918 / Konami).
