================================================================================

Target Extension:    Boriel's ZX Basic (ZXBC) Native Compiler Toolchain
Compilation Support: [BORIEL ZXBC COMPATIBLE] / [NATIVE Z80 MACHINE CODE]
                     (Full compilation of high-level BASIC into optimized Z80 machine
                     code binaries and standalone autostarting .TAP containers via the
                     integrated zxbc compiler toolchain).

38.1 What is Boriel's ZX Basic? (The High-Performance Native Z80 Compiler)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
For decades, Sinclair ZX Spectrum developers faced a stark dilemma: write in Sinclair
BASIC for rapid development but suffer severe execution slowness, or write in raw Z80
assembly language for blistering speed at the cost of steep complexity and tedious
debugging.

Boriel's ZX Basic (created by Jose Rodriguez, aka Boriel) completely revolutionized
Spectrum software development by bridging this divide. ZXBC is a modern, optimizing,
cross-platform compiler that translates structured BASIC source code directly into
lean, cycle-efficient Z80 machine code. Programs compiled with Boriel Basic run
hundreds of times faster than interpreted Sinclair BASIC, approaching the speed
of hand-crafted assembly while retaining the readability, modularity, and expressiveness
of high-level structured programming.

Key Architectural Strengths of Boriel's ZX Basic:
  - 100% Native Z80 Machine Code: Generates standalone binary executables (.bin, .tap,
    .tzx) with zero runtime interpreter overhead.
  - Modern Structured Programming: Full support for named procedures (`SUB`), typed
    functions (`FUNCTION`), local variable scoping, recursion, and structured control flow.
  - Multi-Precision Static Typing: 8-bit (`BYTE`/`UBYTE`), 16-bit (`INTEGER`/`UINTEGER`),
    32-bit (`LONG`/`ULONG`), 32-bit fixed point (`FIXED`), standard 40-bit floating point
    (`FLOAT`), and dynamic strings (`STRING`).
  - Ultra-Fast Parameter Passing: `FASTCALL` convention passes arguments directly in CPU
    registers (A, HL, DE) for zero-stack overhead.
  - Direct Hardware & Memory Access: Inline assembly blocks (`ASM ... END ASM`), typed
    pointers, memory address operators (`@`), direct port I/O, and hardware register access.
  - Memory Efficiency: Dead code elimination, peephole optimization, and configurable
    heap and stack positioning for maximum free RAM.


38.2 Writing and Organizing Boriel Basic Code in BasinPy (.zxb Source Files)
---------------------------------------------------------------------------
BasinPy provides first-class support for Boriel's ZX Basic right out of the box.
Developers can write, test, debug, and compile Boriel Basic applications within a
single unified environment.

1. Source File Format (.zxb):
   Boriel Basic source files in BasinPy are saved with the `.zxb` extension (Basin ASCII
   BASIC). Unlike legacy tokenized Spectrum programs, `.zxb` files are human-readable,
   UTF-8 clean text files without mandatory line numbers.
   - Traditional line numbers (e.g. `10 PRINT "HELLO"`) are optional and can be used for
     backwards compatibility, but modern Boriel code relies entirely on named subroutines,
     functions, and label identifiers.

2. Syntax Highlighting & Validation in BasinPy:
   BasinPy's real-time parser automatically detects Boriel keywords (`SUB`, `FUNCTION`,
   `END SUB`, `AS BYTE`, `FASTCALL`, `ASM`, etc.) and tags them with the `[BORIEL]`
   compliance badge in royal purple (`RGB(140, 60, 200)`).
   - Use `SYNTAX` or `COMPLIANCE` in BasinPy to audit your code before compilation.

3. Compiling Inside BasinPy:
   - Command: `COMPILE [target_file.tap]`
     BasinPy invokes its built-in `zxbasic_compiler.py` and the resident Boriel compiler
     located in `/zxbasic`.
   - The compiler performs lexical analysis, parses the Abstract Syntax Tree (AST),
     applies optimization passes, generates Z80 assembly, runs the assembler, and
     packages the binary into a conforming Sinclair `.TAP` tape container equipped
     with an automated BASIC autoloader (`10 RANDOMIZE USR 24500`).
   - The resulting `.TAP` file runs instantly inside BasinPy, on standard Spectrum emulators
     (Fuse, SpecEmu, Retro Virtual Machine), or on physical ZX Spectrum hardware (via DivMMC,
     SMD, or audio cassette tape).


38.3 Procedures and Functions: SUB, FUNCTION, END SUB, END FUNCTION
-------------------------------------------------------------------
Boriel Basic eliminates the spaghetti code of legacy `GO SUB` and `RETURN` by introducing
true structured procedures and functions.

1. Subroutines (`SUB`):
   Subroutines execute a block of statements and do not return a value.
   - Syntax:
     ```basic
     SUB DrawBorder(x AS UBYTE, y AS UBYTE, w AS UBYTE, h AS UBYTE)
         DIM col AS UBYTE
         FOR col = x TO x + w
             PLOT col, y
             PLOT col, y + h
         NEXT col
     END SUB
     ```
   - Invocation:
     Subroutines can be called directly by name or with the optional `CALL` keyword:
     ```basic
     DrawBorder(10, 10, 100, 50)
     CALL DrawBorder(10, 10, 100, 50)
     ```

2. Functions (`FUNCTION`):
   Functions calculate and return a typed value using `RETURN expression`.
   - Syntax:
     ```basic
     FUNCTION Distance(x1 AS INTEGER, y1 AS INTEGER, x2 AS INTEGER, y2 AS INTEGER) AS UINTEGER
         DIM dx AS INTEGER = x2 - x1
         DIM dy AS INTEGER = y2 - y1
         RETURN SQR(dx * dx + dy * dy)
     END FUNCTION
     ```
   - Invocation in Expressions:
     Functions can be used anywhere an expression is expected:
     ```basic
     DIM dist AS UINTEGER
     dist = Distance(playerX, playerY, enemyX, enemyY)
     IF dist < 16 THEN PlayerHit()
     ```


38.4 Parameters and Calling Conventions: BYVAL, BYREF, FASTCALL, STDCALL
------------------------------------------------------------------------
Boriel Basic gives developers total control over how data moves across function boundaries:

1. `BYVAL` (Pass by Value):
   Copies the argument value into the parameter. Changes made to the parameter inside the
   subroutine do not affect the caller's original variable. This is the default for numeric
   scalars.
   ```basic
   SUB Increment(BYVAL n AS UBYTE)
       n = n + 1: REM Only local copy is incremented
   END SUB
   ```

2. `BYREF` (Pass by Reference):
   Passes the memory address of the caller's variable. Any modification to the parameter
   directly mutates the caller's variable in place. Ideal for returning multiple results
   or avoiding the overhead of copying large data blocks.
   ```basic
   SUB SwapValues(BYREF a AS INTEGER, BYREF b AS INTEGER)
       DIM temp AS INTEGER = a
       a = b
       b = temp
   END SUB
   ```

3. `FASTCALL` (Register-Based Calling):
   `FASTCALL` instructs the compiler to pass the first parameter directly in Z80 CPU
   registers rather than pushing it onto the stack:
     - 8-bit arguments (`BYTE`, `UBYTE`): Passed in register `A`.
     - 16-bit arguments (`INTEGER`, `UINTEGER`, Pointers): Passed in register `HL`.
     - 32-bit arguments (`LONG`, `ULONG`): Passed in registers `DE:HL`.
   This produces blistering execution speeds, often saving dozens of T-states per call.
   ```basic
   FUNCTION FASTCALL FastSqr(val AS UINTEGER) AS UINTEGER
       RETURN val * val
   END FUNCTION
   ```

4. `STDCALL`:
   Standard C-style calling convention where all arguments are pushed onto the machine stack
   from right to left. Used when interfacing with standard assembly routines.


38.5 Static Variable Types & Memory Layout
------------------------------------------
Unlike standard Sinclair BASIC where all numbers are stored as 5-byte (40-bit) floating
point values, Boriel Basic allows explicit static typing. Selecting the appropriate type
drastically accelerates calculations and minimizes memory consumption:

| Data Type  | Size (Bytes) | Range / Description                                      | Typical Use Case                  |
|------------|--------------|----------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------|
| `BYTE`     | 1 byte       | -128 to +127 (signed 8-bit)                              | Screen deltas, small offsets      |
| `UBYTE`    | 1 byte       | 0 to 255 (unsigned 8-bit)                                | Screen coordinates (X/Y), INK/ATTR|
| `INTEGER`  | 2 bytes      | -32,768 to +32,767 (signed 16-bit, little-endian)        | Physics velocity, grid math       |
| `UINTEGER` | 2 bytes      | 0 to 65,535 (unsigned 16-bit, little-endian)             | Memory addresses, scores, counters|
| `LONG`     | 4 bytes      | -2,147,483,648 to +2,147,483,647 (signed 32-bit)         | Large totals, astronomical math   |
| `ULONG`    | 4 bytes      | 0 to 4,294,967,295 (unsigned 32-bit)                     | High-precision game timers        |
| `FIXED`    | 4 bytes      | 16.16 signed fixed point (-32768.9999 to +32767.9999)    | High-speed fractional physics     |
| `FLOAT`    | 5 bytes      | Official Sinclair ZX Spectrum 40-bit floating point format| Scientific & trig functions       |
| `STRING`   | Dynamic      | Variable-length string (2-byte length header + ASCII)    | Text, messages, filenames         |

Declarations and Initialization:
```basic
DIM x AS UBYTE = 128
DIM score AS UINTEGER = 0
DIM pi AS FLOAT = 3.14159
CONST MAX_LIVES AS UBYTE = 3
```


38.6 Pointers, Memory Buffers, Arrays & Introspection
-----------------------------------------------------
Boriel Basic gives developers low-level C-like memory control while keeping BASIC syntax:

1. The Address-Of Operator (`@`):
   Prefixing a variable or array with `@` returns its 16-bit memory address:
   ```basic
   DIM buffer(255) AS UBYTE
   DIM bufAddr AS UINTEGER
   bufAddr = @buffer(0)
   ```

2. Typed Pointers:
   Pointers store the memory address of a typed variable. Dereferencing is done via `*`:
   ```basic
   DIM ptr AS UBYTE POINTER
   ptr = @x
   *ptr = 255: REM Sets variable x to 255
   ```

3. Memory Introspection Functions:
   - `SIZEOF(variable | type)`: Returns the memory footprint in bytes.
     ```basic
     PRINT "Size of INTEGER: "; SIZEOF(INTEGER); " bytes"   : REM Prints 2
     ```
   - `CAST(type, expression)`: Performs explicit type conversion between incompatible types.
     ```basic
     DIM val AS INTEGER = -50
     DIM rawByte AS UBYTE = CAST(UBYTE, val)
     ```
   - `LBOUND(array)` and `UBOUND(array)`: Return lower and upper indices of array dimensions.


38.7 Modern Structured Control Flow
-----------------------------------
Boriel Basic features modern, readable control structures that completely replace messy
`GO TO` and `IF...THEN line` jumps:

1. `IF ... THEN ... ELSEIF ... ELSE ... END IF`:
   Multi-branch conditionals with clean nesting:
   ```basic
   IF key$ = "Q" THEN
       MoveUp()
   ELSEIF key$ = "A" THEN
       MoveDown()
   ELSEIF key$ = "O" THEN
       MoveLeft()
   ELSEIF key$ = "P" THEN
       MoveRight()
   ELSE
       Idle()
   END IF
   ```

2. `DO WHILE / UNTIL ... LOOP WHILE / UNTIL`:
   General-purpose loops with pre-test or post-test conditions:
   ```basic
   DO WHILE lives > 0
       UpdateGame()
       RenderFrame()
   LOOP
   ```
   Post-test loop (always executes at least once):
   ```basic
   DO
       key$ = INKEY$
   LOOP UNTIL key$ <> ""
   ```

3. `EXIT` and `CONTINUE`:
   - `EXIT FOR`, `EXIT DO`, `EXIT SUB`, `EXIT FUNCTION`: Immediately exits the active loop or procedure.
   - `CONTINUE FOR`, `CONTINUE DO`: Jumps directly to the next iteration of the loop.


38.8 Bitwise Expressions & Arithmetic Optimizations
---------------------------------------------------
Boriel Basic compiles bitwise operations into fast, single Z80 instructions:
  - `SHL`: Shift bits left (multiplies by powers of 2): `a = b SHL 3` (equivalent to `b * 8`)
  - `SHR`: Shift bits right (divides by powers of 2): `a = b SHR 1` (equivalent to `b / 2`)
  - `XOR`: Bitwise exclusive OR: `a = b XOR 0xFF` (inverts all bits)
  - `BAND` or `&`: Bitwise AND: `a = b BAND 0x07` (extracts lower 3 bits)
  - `BOR` or `|`: Bitwise OR: `a = b BOR 0x80` (sets high bit)
  - `BNOT` or `~`: Bitwise NOT (one's complement)


38.9 Inline Assembly & Hardware Access (ASM ... END ASM)
--------------------------------------------------------
When absolute maximum speed is required (e.g. inner rendering loops or custom interrupt
handlers), Boriel Basic allows embedding pure Z80 assembly directly into your code:

1. Basic Inline Assembly Syntax:
   ```basic
   SUB FastFill(addr AS UINTEGER, count AS UINTEGER, val AS UBYTE)
       ASM
           LD HL, (._addr)     ; Load address parameter into HL
           LD BC, (._count)    ; Load byte count into BC
           LD A, (._val)       ; Load fill value into accumulator
       FillLoop:
           LD (HL), A          ; Write byte
           INC HL              ; Advance pointer
           DEC BC              ; Decrement counter
           LD A, B
           OR C
           JR NZ, FillLoop     ; Loop until BC == 0
       END ASM
   END SUB
   ```

2. Accessing Boriel Variables from Assembly:
   Global and local variables are accessible inside `ASM` blocks by prefixing their names
   with `._` (e.g. `._x`, `._score`, `._buffer`).

3. Preserving Registers:
   When writing inline assembly, you can freely use `AF`, `BC`, `DE`, and `HL`. If your
   routine alters `IX` or `IY`, you must preserve them using `PUSH IX` / `POP IX` to
   maintain runtime system stability.


38.10 Preprocessor Directives, Optimization Flags & Compiler Commands
---------------------------------------------------------------------
Boriel's compiler includes a C-compatible preprocessor and rich optimization options:

1. Preprocessor Directives:
   - `#include "filename.bas"` : Merges external library files at compile time.
   - `#define IDENTIFIER val`  : Defines compile-time constants or macros.
   - `#ifdef IDENTIFIER` / `#ifndef` / `#else` / `#endif`: Conditional compilation.
   - `#pragma output [option]` : Configures binary output options (e.g. starting address).

2. Compiler Command-Line Options:
   When invoked via BasinPy or terminal:
   - `-O2` / `-O3`: Enables aggressive dead-code elimination and register peephole optimizations.
   - `--sinclair`: Enforces strict Sinclair BASIC syntax compatibility for legacy programs.
   - `--strict`: Disallows implicit type conversions for robust, type-safe programming.
   - `--org=addr`: Sets execution origin address (default is 24500 / 0x5FB4).
   - `--heap-size=bytes`: Reserves dynamic memory allocation heap space for `ALLOC()` and `FREE()`.
   - `--tap`: Packages machine code into an autostarting `.TAP` tape file.


38.11 Step-by-Step Tutorial: Developing an Arcade Action Game with Boriel ZX Basic
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Here is a complete, working Boriel Basic arcade action game: "Star Defender". It showcases
structured subroutines, static integer typing, high-speed keyboard polling, particle effects,
and custom UDG graphics:

```basic
REM ====================================================================
REM STAR DEFENDER - A Fast 50Hz Arcade Game in Boriel's ZX Basic
REM Compile with: COMPILE "stardefender.tap"
REM ====================================================================

#include <keys.bas>

CONST SCREEN_W AS UBYTE = 32
CONST SCREEN_H AS UBYTE = 24
CONST BORDER_COL AS UBYTE = 0

DIM playerX AS UBYTE = 16
DIM playerY AS UBYTE = 20
DIM laserX AS UBYTE = 0
DIM laserY AS UBYTE = 0
DIM laserActive AS UBYTE = 0
DIM alienX AS UBYTE = 1
DIM alienY AS UBYTE = 2
DIM alienDir AS BYTE = 1
DIM score AS UINTEGER = 0
DIM lives AS UBYTE = 3

SUB FASTCALL InitDisplay()
    BORDER BORDER_COL
    PAPER 0: INK 7: BRIGHT 1: CLS
    PRINT AT 0, 0; INK 6; "SCORE: "; score; "  LIVES: "; lives
    PRINT AT 23, 0; INK 1; "================================"
END SUB

SUB FASTCALL DrawPlayer(x AS UBYTE, y AS UBYTE)
    PRINT AT y, x; INK 4; "<A>"
END SUB

SUB FASTCALL ClearPlayer(x AS UBYTE, y AS UBYTE)
    PRINT AT y, x; "   "
END SUB

SUB FASTCALL FireLaser()
    IF laserActive = 0 THEN
        laserActive = 1
        laserX = playerX + 1
        laserY = playerY - 1
        BEEP 0.005, 30: REM Short laser zap
    END IF
END SUB

SUB UpdateLaser()
    IF laserActive = 1 THEN
        PRINT AT laserY, laserX; " "
        IF laserY > 1 THEN
            laserY = laserY - 1
            PRINT AT laserY, laserX; INK 5; "|"
        ELSE
            laserActive = 0
        END IF
    END IF
END SUB

SUB UpdateAlien()
    PRINT AT alienY, alienX; " "
    alienX = alienX + alienDir
    IF alienX >= 29 OR alienX <= 1 THEN
        alienDir = -alienDir
        alienY = alienY + 1
        IF alienY >= 20 THEN
            lives = lives - 1
            alienY = 2
            BEEP 0.05, -10
        END IF
    END IF
    PRINT AT alienY, alienX; INK 2; "{*}"
END SUB

SUB CheckCollisions()
    IF laserActive = 1 THEN
        IF laserX >= alienX AND laserX <= alienX + 2 AND laserY = alienY THEN
            REM Alien hit!
            PRINT AT alienY, alienX; INK 6; "***"
            BEEP 0.02, 10
            BEEP 0.02, 20
            score = score + 100
            laserActive = 0
            PRINT AT laserY, laserX; " "
            alienX = 1: alienY = 2
            PRINT AT 0, 7; INK 6; score
        END IF
    END IF
END SUB

REM --- Main Game Loop ---
InitDisplay()

DO WHILE lives > 0
    ClearPlayer(playerX, playerY)
    
    IF INKEY$ = "o" OR INKEY$ = "O" THEN
        IF playerX > 1 THEN playerX = playerX - 1
    END IF
    IF INKEY$ = "p" OR INKEY$ = "P" THEN
        IF playerX < 28 THEN playerX = playerX + 1
    END IF
    IF INKEY$ = " " THEN
        FireLaser()
    END IF
    
    DrawPlayer(playerX, playerY)
    UpdateLaser()
    UpdateAlien()
    CheckCollisions()
    
    REM Frame pacing delay
    PAUSE 1
LOOP

CLS
PRINT AT 10, 8; INK 2; FLASH 1; "G A M E   O V E R"
PRINT AT 12, 6; INK 7; "FINAL SCORE: "; score
PAUSE 200
```

================================================================================
 40. SECTION 39: THE MICROSTAR PROFESSIONAL APPLICATION SUITE: COMPREHENSIVE USER GUIDES
================================================================================

Target Extension:    Microstar (MicroPro) Professional CP/M Application Suite
Compilation Support: [INTERPRETER ONLY] / [NATIVE Z80 CP/M 2.2 & PLUS EMULATION]
                     (Complete functional implementations of WordStar 3.0, CalcStar 1.4,
                     DataStar, and SuperSort running in BasinPy's authentic 51x24 CRT
                     display environment with terminal emulation and disk persistence).

39.1 WordStar 3.0 (WS.COM) Professional Word Processor User Guide
------------------------------------------------------------------
39.1.1 Overview & Architecture on the ZX Spectrum +3 CP/M:
WordStar, created by Rob Barnaby and Seymour Rubinstein of MicroPro International in
1979, was the undisputed king of word processors throughout the 8-bit CP/M era. It was
the program that proved personal microcomputers were viable commercial tools capable of
serious office work.

In BasinPy's CP/M subsystem, WordStar is available as `WS.COM` (or `WORDSTAR.COM`).
It runs in the authentic Sinclair ZX Spectrum +3 high-density 51-column by 24-row
display mode using the specialized 5x8 font. It supports all standard CP/M disk
drives (A: through P:), handles text files with standard CRLF and 0x1A EOF terminators,
and correctly manages WordStar's high-bit character encoding (used historically for
soft-spaces and word-wrap points).

Launching WordStar:
From the CP/M command prompt (`A>`):
  - `A> WS`                    (Opens WordStar to the Opening Menu)
  - `A> WS LETTER.TXT`         (Opens LETTER.TXT directly for editing)
  - `A> WORDSTAR MEMO.DOC`     (Alias syntax)


39.1.2 Display Layout:
WordStar divides the 51x24 CRT screen into four distinct functional regions:
  1. Top Status Line (Row 0):
     Displays critical document status information:
     ```
     B:REPORT.TXT     PAGE 1  LINE 14  COL 22  INSERT  ON
     ```
     - Current Drive and Filename.
     - Active Page number (calculated dynamically based on lines per page).
     - Active Line number and Column cursor position.
     - Insert Mode status (`INSERT ON` or `OVERWRITE`).
  2. The Ruler Line (Row 1):
     Visually marks margins and tab positions:
     ```
     L----!----!----!----!----!----!----!----!----R
     ```
     - `L` marks the Left Margin column.
     - `!` marks active Tab Stops (every 5 columns by default).
     - `R` marks the Right Margin column.
     - Hyphens (`-`) represent regular text columns.
  3. The 21-Line Viewport (Rows 2..22):
     The primary editing area where text is displayed and edited in real time.
     Soft line wraps are indicated by a space in the rightmost column; hard carriage
     returns (pressed by Enter) are marked by a `<` symbol in the margin.
  4. The Bottom Menu / Help Line (Row 23):
     Displays context-sensitive shortcuts, operational prompts, and error messages.


39.1.3 The No-File Opening Menu:
When started without a filename, WordStar displays the Opening Menu:
  ```
  <<<  N O - F I L E   M E N U  >>>
  --- Preliminary Commands ---        --- File Commands ---
  L  Change logged drive              P  Print a file
  F  File directory off (ON)          E  Rename a file
  H  Set help level                   O  Copy a file
  --- Commands to Open a File ---     Y  Delete a file
  D  Open a Document file             --- System Commands ---
  N  Open a Non-Document file         X  Exit to CP/M
  ```
Opening Menu Command Reference:
  - `D` (Open Document File): Opens a formatted text document with word wrap, justification,
    and automatic pagination enabled. Ideal for letters, essays, and manuals.
  - `N` (Open Non-Document File): Opens a raw text file without word wrap or soft-spaces.
    Essential for writing programming source code (BASIC, Z80 assembly, Forth, Pascal, C)
    and CP/M batch scripts (`.SUB`).
  - `L` (Change Logged Drive): Prompts for a drive letter (e.g. `B:`) to switch default storage.
  - `F` (Toggle File Directory): Shows or hides the catalog of files on the current drive.
  - `P` (Print a File): Sends a document to the ZX Spectrum printer stream or print file.
  - `E` (Rename a File): Prompts for original filename and new filename.
  - `O` (Copy a File): Duplicates an existing file to another name or disk drive.
  - `Y` (Delete a File): Permanently removes a file from disk.
  - `X` (Exit to CP/M): Closes WordStar and returns cleanly to the CP/M `A>` prompt.


39.1.4 The WordStar Diamond & Cursor Navigation:
WordStar was engineered for touch-typists. It revolutionized word processing by allowing
typists to navigate anywhere in a document without lifting their hands from the home row
to reach dedicated arrow keys.

Hold down the `Control` key (or `Ctrl`) and press:
  - The Classic Navigation Diamond:
      `^E`  Cursor UP one line
      `^X`  Cursor DOWN one line
      `^S`  Cursor LEFT one character
      `^D`  Cursor RIGHT one character
  - Word & Screen Movement:
      `^A`  Jump word LEFT
      `^F`  Jump word RIGHT
      `^R`  Page UP (scroll up one full screen)
      `^C`  Page DOWN (scroll down one full screen)
      `^W`  Scroll screen DOWN one line (cursor remains on text)
      `^Z`  Scroll screen UP one line (cursor remains on text)
  - Quick Cursor Commands (`^Q` Prefix):
      `^QS` Jump to the BEGINNING of the current line (Column 1)
      `^QD` Jump to the END of the current line
      `^QR` Jump to the very TOP of the document (Page 1, Line 1)
      `^QC` Jump to the very BOTTOM of the document (End of text)
      `^QB` Jump to the Start of the marked block
      `^QK` Jump to the End of the marked block
      `^QP` Jump to the previous cursor position (backtrack)


39.1.5 Text Insertion, Deletion & Word Wrap:
  - Text Insertion: Simply type text at the cursor. If `INSERT ON` is active, existing
    characters are pushed to the right. If `OVERWRITE` is active, typing replaces characters.
  - Toggle Insert Mode: Press `^V` (or the `Insert` key).
  - Deletion Commands:
      `^G`  Delete character directly under the cursor.
      `^H`  (or `Backspace`): Delete character to the left of the cursor.
      `^T`  Delete the word to the right of the cursor.
      `^Y`  Delete the ENTIRE current line.
      `^QY` Delete from the cursor to the END of the current line.


39.1.6 Block Operations (^K Prefix):
WordStar's block engine allows moving, copying, deleting, and saving arbitrary blocks
of text:
  - `^KB` (Mark Block Begin): Drops a marker at the start of the desired block.
  - `^KK` (Mark Block End): Drops a marker at the end of the block.
  - `^KC` (Copy Block): Duplicates the marked block to the current cursor position.
  - `^KV` (Move Block): Cuts the marked block and pastes it at the current cursor position.
  - `^KY` (Delete Block): Deletes the entire marked block.
  - `^KW` (Write Block to Disk): Prompts for a filename and saves only the marked block to disk.
  - `^KR` (Read File into Document): Prompts for a filename and inserts its entire contents
    directly at the current cursor position.


39.1.7 Search and Replace Operations (^Q Prefix):
  - `^QF` (Find String):
    Prompts: `FIND? <text>`. Prompts for search options:
      `G`  Global search (from top of document)
      `U`  Ignore case (case-insensitive)
      `B`  Search backward
  - `^QA` (Find and Replace):
    Prompts: `FIND? <target>` and `REPLACE WITH? <replacement>`.
    Options:
      `N`  Replace without asking confirmation
      `G`  Global replacement throughout entire document
  - `^L` (Find / Replace Again): Repeats the most recent search or replace operation.


39.1.8 Page Formatting, Margins & Paragraph Reform (^O Prefix):
  - `^OC` (Center Line): Automatically centers the current text line between left and right margins.
  - `^OW` (Toggle Word Wrap): Toggles automatic wrapping of words at the right margin.
  - `^OJ` (Toggle Justification): Toggles soft-space padding to align right margins.
  - `^OL` (Set Left Margin): Prompts for column number (e.g. `1` or `5`).
  - `^OR` (Set Right Margin): Prompts for column number (e.g. `50` or `75`).
  - `^B` (Reform Paragraph): The essential formatting command! When text has been added or
    deleted from an existing paragraph, place the cursor at the top of the paragraph and
    press `^B`. WordStar re-flows and re-wraps the entire paragraph according to the current
    ruler margins down to the next blank line.


39.1.9 Dot Commands Reference:
Dot commands allow formatting document layout and printing by placing a period (`.`) in
the very first column of a line followed by a two-letter directive:
  - `.he <Header Text>` : Prints a running header at the top of every subsequent page.
  - `.fo <Footer Text>` : Prints a running footer at the bottom of every subsequent page.
  - `.pa`               : Forces an unconditional page break.
  - `.op`               : Omit page numbers from being printed automatically.
  - `.pn <n>`           : Sets the page number sequence to start at integer `n`.
  - `.lm <n>`           : Sets left margin to column `n`.
  - `.rm <n>`           : Sets right margin to column `n`.
  - `.pl <n>`           : Sets total page length in lines (default is 66 lines for standard 11" paper).
  - `.mt <n>`           : Margin Top: number of blank lines at top of page (default 3).
  - `.mb <n>`           : Margin Bottom: number of blank lines at bottom of page (default 8).


39.1.10 File Saving, Document Exiting & Printing:
All document exit and save operations use the `^K` prefix:
  - `^KS` (Save and Stay): Saves the document to disk and keeps the cursor exactly where it is.
    Always save frequently during long writing sessions!
  - `^KD` (Save and Return to Menu): Saves changes and returns to the WordStar Opening Menu.
  - `^KX` (Save and Exit to CP/M): Saves changes and exits WordStar completely back to the CP/M prompt.
  - `^KQ` (Abandon Document): Abandons all changes made since the last save and returns to the menu.


39.1.11 Practical Tutorial: Writing and Printing a Professional Business Document:
1. From CP/M prompt, type: `WS MEMO.TXT`
2. At the top of the blank document, enter the header dot commands:
   ```text
   .he SINCLAIR RESEARCH LTD - INTERNAL MEMO
   .fo Confidential - Page #
   ```
3. Type the memo header:
   ```text
   MEMORANDUM
   DATE:    23rd September 1986
   TO:      All Engineering Staff
   FROM:    Clive Sinclair
   SUBJECT: ZX Spectrum +3 CP/M Software Release
   ```
4. Place cursor on `MEMORANDUM` and press `^OC` to center the title.
5. Write the body text. Let WordStar wrap lines automatically without pressing Enter.
6. When finished, press `^B` at the beginning of each paragraph to ensure tidy margins.
7. Press `^KS` to save your work, then press `^KX` to return to CP/M. Your document is saved!



39.2 CalcStar 1.4 (CS.COM) Financial Spreadsheet User Guide
------------------------------------------------------------
39.2.1 Overview & Architecture:
CalcStar 1.4, published by MicroPro International, was the premier financial modeling
and spreadsheet system developed specifically for CP/M microcomputers. It brought the
computational power of VisiCalc and SuperCalc into the integrated MicroPro office family.

In BasinPy, CalcStar runs as `CS.COM` (or `CALCSTAR.COM`) in authentic 51x24 CRT mode.
It provides a massive 2D matrix of **26 Columns (labeled A through Z) by 255 Rows (numbered
1 through 255)**, yielding over 6,600 individual calculation cells!

Launching CalcStar:
  - `A> CS`                    (Opens CalcStar with an empty spreadsheet)
  - `A> CS BUDGET.CAL`         (Opens existing spreadsheet file BUDGET.CAL)
  - `A> CALCSTAR EXPENSES.CAL` (Alias syntax)


39.2.2 The Worksheet Display Screen & Coordinates Grid:
The CalcStar screen is organized into informative zones:
  - Row 0 (Status & Coordinate Bar):
    Displays the active cell coordinate, formatting mode, and current cell contents:
    ```
    B:BUDGET.CAL    CELL: C14    FORMAT: $ (F2)    VAL: 1450.00
    ```
  - Row 1 (Formula & Prompt Bar):
    Displays the raw formula underlying the active cell (e.g. `FORMULA: +B14*1.15`) or
    input prompts when executing slash commands.
  - Row 2 (Column Headers Bar):
    Displays column letters aligned above each column: `|    A    |    B    |    C    |`
  - Rows 3..22 (Worksheet Grid Area):
    Displays 20 visible rows of numbers, labels, and computed values. Row numbers (1..20, etc.)
    are clearly displayed along the left edge.
  - Row 23 (Help & Message Bar):
    Displays command reminders (`Type / for Commands, ? for Help`).


39.2.3 Cell Navigation & Direct Coordinate Jumps:
Moving around the spreadsheet is fast and intuitive:
  - The WordStar Diamond: Hold `Control` and press `^E` (Up), `^X` (Down), `^S` (Left), `^D` (Right).
  - Arrow Keys: Standard arrow keys move the cell highlight one step in any direction.
  - Page Scrolling:
      `^R`  Page UP (scroll up 20 rows)
      `^C`  Page DOWN (scroll down 20 rows)
      `^A`  Page LEFT (scroll columns left)
      `^F`  Page RIGHT (scroll columns right)
  - Direct Jump (`/G` Goto Command):
      Type `/G` followed by the target coordinate (e.g. `/G M50` or `/G A1`) and press Enter.
      The highlight instantly jumps directly to that cell.


39.2.4 Data Entry: Labels, Numeric Constants, and Formulas:
CalcStar automatically identifies what you are typing:
  1. Text Labels:
     If the first character typed is a letter, quotation mark (`"`), or space, CalcStar
     treats the entry as a descriptive text label (e.g. `Jan`, `Total Expenses`, `Rent`).
  2. Numeric Constants:
     If the first character is a digit (0..9) or minus sign (`-`), CalcStar parses it
     as a numeric value (e.g. `1200`, `45.50`, `-150`).
  3. Formulas:
     To enter a mathematical formula, **begin with a plus sign (`+`) or equals (`=`)**:
     ```
     +A1+B1
     +C5*0.175
     +(D10-E10)/D10
     ```


39.2.5 Formula Syntax, Operators & Relative Cell References:
CalcStar supports standard algebraic rules with full parenthetical precedence:
  - Arithmetic Operators:
      `+`  Addition
      `-`  Subtraction
      `*`  Multiplication
      `/`  Division
      `^`  Exponentiation (power)
  - Automatic Coordinate Relocation:
    When formulas are copied across cells with `/C`, CalcStar automatically updates cell
    references relatively. For example, copying `+A1+B1` from row 1 to row 2 automatically
    becomes `+A2+B2`!


39.2.6 Built-in Mathematical & Statistical Functions Reference:
CalcStar includes a comprehensive library of built-in computational functions:
  - `SUM(range)`      : Computes the sum of all numeric cells in a range (e.g. `SUM(B2:B12)`).
  - `AVG(range)`      : Computes the arithmetic mean (average) of cells in a range.
  - `CNT(range)`      : Returns the count of non-empty numeric cells in a range.
  - `MIN(range)`      : Returns the smallest value found within a range.
  - `MAX(range)`      : Returns the largest value found within a range.
  - `ABS(expr)`       : Returns the absolute (positive) value of an expression.
  - `INT(expr)`       : Truncates a value to its integer component.
  - `SQRT(expr)`      : Calculates the positive square root.
  - `ROUND(expr, n)`  : Rounds an expression to `n` decimal places.
  - `IF(cond, v1, v2)` : Evaluates `cond`; returns `v1` if true, or `v2` if false.
  - `NPV(rate, range)`: Calculates Net Present Value for a series of future cash flows.


39.2.7 The Slash (/) Command System Reference:
Pressing `/` opens CalcStar's command prompt. Enter the letter of the desired command:
  - `/F` (Format Cell / Global):
      `G`    General: standard default display.
      `$`    Currency: displays amounts with currency symbol and commas (e.g. `$1,450.00`).
      `F0`..`F4` Fixed decimals: forces exact decimal places (e.g. `F2` forces 2 decimals).
      `I`    Integer: rounds and displays as whole number.
      `*`    Bar Chart: displays a horizontal bar of asterisks proportional to the value.
      `L`    Left-align text label.
      `R`    Right-align text label.
      `C`    Center text label.
  - `/C` (Copy Range): Prompts for source range (e.g. `B2:B12`) and destination start cell
    (e.g. `C2`). Formulas are automatically adjusted.
  - `/M` (Move): Moves contents of a cell, row, or column to another coordinate.
  - `/I` (Insert):
      `/IR`  Inserts a blank row at the cursor, pushing lower rows down.
      `/IC`  Inserts a blank column at the cursor, pushing right columns right.
  - `/D` (Delete):
      `/DR`  Deletes the current row, shifting lower rows up.
      `/DC`  Deletes the current column, shifting right columns left.
  - `/W` (Column Width): Prompts for column and width in characters (3 to 25 columns).
  - `/B` (Blank Cell): Erases data, labels, and formulas from the active cell.
  - `/A` (Auto-Calculate): Toggles automatic spreadsheet re-calculation on or off.
  - `/R` (Recalculate): Manually evaluates all formulas across the entire worksheet.
  - `/S` (Save): Saves the active spreadsheet to disk as a `.CAL` file.
  - `/L` (Load): Loads an existing `.CAL` spreadsheet from disk.
  - `/P` (Print): Prints the worksheet grid to the printer (`LST:`) or a disk file.
  - `/Q` or `/X` (Quit): Exits CalcStar and returns to CP/M.


39.2.8 Practical Tutorial: Constructing an Annual Business Budget Model:
1. Start CalcStar: `A> CS BUDGET.CAL`
2. Enter Column Headers:
   - Cell A1: `Category`
   - Cell B1: `Qtr 1`
   - Cell C1: `Qtr 2`
   - Cell D1: `Qtr 3`
   - Cell E1: `Qtr 4`
   - Cell F1: `Annual Total`
3. Enter Expense Rows:
   - Cell A2: `Rent`        | B2: `1000` | C2: `1000` | D2: `1000` | E2: `1000`
   - Cell A3: `Salaries`    | B3: `4500` | C3: `4600` | D3: `4700` | E3: `4800`
   - Cell A4: `Utilities`   | B4: `350`  | C4: `300`  | D4: `280`  | E4: `400`
   - Cell A5: `Marketing`   | B5: `800`  | C5: `950`  | D5: `1100` | E5: `1200`
4. Enter Total Formula in F2:
   - Navigate to cell F2 and type: `+B2+C2+D2+E2`
   - Use `/C` to copy cell F2 to range `F3:F5`.
5. Enter Quarterly Summary Totals in Row 7:
   - Cell A7: `Total Exp`
   - Cell B7: `SUM(B2:B5)`
   - Copy cell B7 to range `C7:F7`.
6. Format as Currency:
   - Type `/F`, select `$`, and apply to range `B2:F7`.
7. Save Worksheet: Type `/S`, accept `BUDGET.CAL`, and press Enter. Done!



39.3 DataStar 1.4 (DS.COM & FORMSORT.COM) Forms Database Management User Guide
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
39.3.1 Overview & Architecture:
DataStar, developed by MicroPro, was the first visual, screen-forms oriented database
system created for CP/M microcomputers. Rather than requiring complex programming in
dBASE or assembly, DataStar empowered users to visually design input forms on the screen,
define data validation rules, enter and search records, and sort files for reporting.

In BasinPy, DataStar runs as `DS.COM` (or `DATASTAR.COM`) accompanied by the sorting
utility `FORMSORT.COM`. Data is persisted in standard CP/M `.DTA` files.

Launching DataStar:
  - `A> DS`                    (Opens DataStar forms system)
  - `A> DS CLIENTS.DTA`        (Opens database file CLIENTS.DTA)
  - `A> FORMSORT CLIENTS.DTA`  (Launches index sorter)


39.3.2 Screen Form Design & Attribute Masks:
DataStar separates database structure into distinct field definitions. Each field has a
name, screen coordinate, length, and validation mask:
  - Field Data Types & Masks:
      `A` (Alphanumeric) : Any characters (letters, numbers, symbols).
      `N` (Numeric)      : Numbers only (0..9, minus, decimal point).
      `$` (Currency)     : Financial values with automatic decimal alignment.
      `D` (Date)         : Date verification (DD/MM/YY format).
      `C` (Calculated)   : Evaluates mathematical expressions in real time!
                           Example: `TOTAL = QTY * PRICE`
      `K` (Primary Key)  : Indexed key field for rapid binary search.
      `R` (Required)     : Enforces non-blank entry before record can be written.


39.3.3 Visual Operating Modes:
DataStar operates in three primary viewing modes:
  1. Form View (`FORM`):
     The primary interactive screen. Fields are presented with borders and descriptive
     prompts. The cursor moves from field to field via `Tab` or `Enter`.
  2. Tabular List / Browse View (`LIST`):
     Displays records in a multi-line tabular format similar to a spreadsheet. Ideal for
     scanning through customer lists, part catalogs, or inventory.
  3. Seek / Query Mode (`SEEK`):
     Allows entering a target key (e.g. Customer ID `1042` or Name `Smith`). DataStar
     searches the index and instantly displays the matching record.


39.3.4 Record Management:
  - Adding a New Record:
    Navigate to the last record or select `New Record`. Fill in each field. Press `^B`
    (or press Enter on the final field) to write the record to disk.
  - Navigating Records:
      `^R`  Previous record
      `^C`  Next record
      `^QR` First record in database
      `^QC` Last record in database
  - Modifying Records:
    Navigate to the desired record, use arrow keys or `Tab` to reach the target field,
    edit the value, and press `^B` to update.
  - Deleting Records:
    Press `^Y` on a record to mark it as deleted.


39.3.5 High-Speed Sorting with FORMSORT.COM:
`FORMSORT.COM` is DataStar's companion high-speed disk sorting engine:
  - Syntax:
      `A> FORMSORT filename.DTA [KEY1 [A|D]] [KEY2 [A|D]]`
  - Examples:
      `A> FORMSORT CLIENTS.DTA ZIP A`           (Sorts by Zip Code Ascending)
      `A> FORMSORT ORDERS.DTA DATE D TOTAL D`   (Sorts by Date Descending, then Total Descending)
  - `FORMSORT` executes high-speed Z80 merge-sort passes on disk, generating a perfectly
    ordered database file ready for sequential browsing or report generation.


39.3.6 Integration with WordStar for Automated MailMerge Letters:
DataStar databases integrate directly with WordStar. By exporting `.DTA` files as comma-
delimited records, WordStar's MailMerge engine can read customer names, addresses, and
amounts into personalized letters, invoices, and envelopes automatically!


39.3.7 Practical Tutorial: Designing an Inventory & Invoicing Database:
1. Launch DataStar: `A> DS INVENTORY.DTA`
2. Define the schema fields:
   - Field 1: `PART_NO`  (Length: 6, Type: Alphanumeric, Key: Yes, Required: Yes)
   - Field 2: `DESC`     (Length: 20, Type: Alphanumeric, Required: Yes)
   - Field 3: `QTY`      (Length: 5, Type: Numeric)
   - Field 4: `UNIT_PR`  (Length: 8, Type: Currency)
   - Field 5: `VALUATION`(Length: 10, Type: Calculated: `+QTY*UNIT_PR`)
3. Save Form Definition and start adding records:
   - Part: `1001`, Desc: `Z80A CPU 3.5MHz`, Qty: `50`, Unit Price: `4.50` -> Valuation: `$225.00`
   - Part: `1002`, Desc: `4116 16K DRAM`, Qty: `200`, Unit Price: `1.25` -> Valuation: `$250.00`
   - Part: `1003`, Desc: `ULA 6C001E-7`, Qty: `25`, Unit Price: `12.00` -> Valuation: `$300.00`
4. Press `^B` after each record. Notice the `VALUATION` field is computed automatically!
5. Press `^KX` to save the database and return to CP/M.

================================================================================
 41. SECTION 40: BUNDLED CP/M TRANSIENT UTILITIES & SYSTEM PROGRAMS GUIDE
================================================================================

Target Extension:    Sinclair ZX Spectrum +3 CP/M Plus Bundled Utilities
Compilation Support: [INTERPRETER ONLY] / [NATIVE Z80 CP/M 2.2 & PLUS EMULATION]
                     (Complete operational guide for all bundled CP/M command-line
                     transient utilities and built-in CCP operating system commands).

BasinPy's CP/M subsystem comes pre-equipped with an extensive library of system utilities,
development tools, and maintenance programs matching the official Locomotive Software /
Amstrad Sinclair ZX Spectrum +3 CP/M Plus distribution. This section provides an
exhaustive, practical user guide for every utility program included on the system disks.


40.1 STAT.COM - File & Disk Status Utility
------------------------------------------
Purpose: Displays remaining storage capacity, reports file sizes and attributes, alters
file protection modes, and inspects logical-to-physical device assignments.

1. Disk Free Space Queries:
   - `A> STAT`
     Reports remaining free storage across all currently mounted active drives:
     ```text
     A: R/W, Space: 142k
     B: R/W, Space: 684k
     M: R/W, Space: 58k
     ```
   - `A> STAT B:`
     Reports storage capacity and free space specifically for Drive B:.

2. Detailed File Inspection:
   - `A> STAT *.*`
     Lists all files in the current user area with record count, byte size, allocation
     block count, and access mode:
     ```text
      Recs  Bytes  Blocks  Attributes  Name
        32     4k       1     R/W      A:STAT.COM
        96    12k       1     R/W      A:PIP.COM
       120    15k       2     R/W      A:MALLARD.COM
     ```
   - `A> STAT *.BAS` : Reports statistics for all BASIC files.

3. File Protection & Attribute Management:
   - `A> STAT filename $R/O`
     Marks the file as **Read-Only**. Protects vital system binaries and data from
     accidental deletion (`ERA`) or overwrite.
   - `A> STAT filename $R/W`
     Restores the file to normal **Read/Write** status.
   - `A> STAT filename $SYS`
     Sets the **System** attribute: hides the file from standard `DIR` directory listings.
   - `A> STAT filename $DIR`
     Restores the file to the standard **Directory** attribute (visible to `DIR`).

4. Device Assignment & IOBYTE Inspection:
   - `A> STAT DEV:`
     Displays the current logical-to-physical device mappings for Console (`CON:`),
     Printer (`LST:`), Reader (`RDR:`), and Punch (`PUN:`).
   - `A> STAT VAL:`
     Displays all valid physical options supported by the BIOS.


40.2 PIP.COM - Peripheral Interchange Program
---------------------------------------------
Purpose: The universal file transfer, file duplication, file concatenation, and device
redirection utility for CP/M.

1. Syntax:
   `PIP destination=source [switches]`
   Remember the golden rule of PIP: **Destination on the left, Source on the right!**

2. Common File Copy Operations:
   - Copy a single file across drives:
     `A> PIP B:=A:PROGRAM.BAS`
   - Copy all files from Drive A: to Drive B::
     `A> PIP B:=A:*.*`
   - Copy all files with verification:
     `A> PIP B:=A:*.*[V]`
     The `[V]` switch forces PIP to re-read destination sectors and calculate CRC
     checksums against source data, guaranteeing 100% error-free copies.
   - Rename a file while copying:
     `A> PIP B:BACKUP.DAT=A:ORIGINAL.DAT`

3. Concatenating (Merging) Files:
   Multiple source files separated by commas are combined into a single destination file:
   `A> PIP MERGED.TXT=PART1.TXT,PART2.TXT,PART3.TXT`

4. Operational Parameter Switches:
   - `[V]` : Verify destination data against source.
   - `[U]` : Convert all lowercase letters to uppercase during transfer.
   - `[L]` : Convert all uppercase letters to lowercase during transfer.
   - `[N]` : Add sequential line numbers to each line of text.
   - `[Z]` : Zero the high bit (8th bit) of all bytes. Essential when converting WordStar
             documents into plain ASCII text for compilers or other word processors!
   - `[Stext^Z]` : Start copying only after encountering `text`.
   - `[Qtext^Z]` : Quit copying immediately upon encountering `text`.

5. Device Redirection:
   - Print a text file directly to the printer:
     `A> PIP LST:=MYFILE.TXT`
   - Create a text file directly from keyboard input:
     `A> PIP NOTES.TXT=CON:`
     (Type your text; press `Control-Z` to close the file).


40.3 ED.COM - Context Line & Character Editor
---------------------------------------------
Purpose: The classic character and line-oriented text editor supplied with all CP/M
systems. Used to create and edit program source code, batch scripts, and data.

1. Launching ED:
   `A> ED FILENAME.TXT`
   If the file exists, ED opens it. If it does not exist, ED creates a new file.
   The prompt changes to the asterisk (`*`).

2. Memory Buffer Management:
   Unlike modern editors that load entire files into RAM at once, ED streams lines
   through an internal memory buffer:
   - `#A` : Read all lines from disk into the editing buffer.
   - `W`  : Write buffer lines out to the temporary file on disk.
   - `E`  : End editing: writes buffer and remaining lines to disk, renames original file
            to `.BAK`, saves new file, and exits to CP/M.
   - `Q`  : Quit immediately, abandoning all changes without altering original file.

3. Moving the Character Pointer:
   - `B`  : Move character pointer to the Beginning of the buffer.
   - `-B` : Move character pointer to the End of the buffer.
   - `nL` : Move pointer down `n` lines.
   - `-nL`: Move pointer up `n` lines.

4. Viewing Text:
   - `T`  : Type (display) the current line on screen.
   - `5T` : Display the next 5 lines.
   - `0T` : Display the line from column 1 up to the current pointer position.

5. Editing Text:
   - `I`  : Enter Insert Mode. Type text freely. Press `Control-Z` (`^Z`) to exit Insert Mode.
   - `D`  : Delete one character to the right of pointer.
   - `nK` : Kill (delete) `n` lines starting from pointer.
   - `Sfind^Zreplace^Z` : Substitute `find` string with `replace` string.


40.4 DDT.COM - Dynamic Debugging Tool & Z80 Disassembler
--------------------------------------------------------
Purpose: Interactive machine code debugger, memory inspector, disassembler, and single-
step execution tracer.

1. Launching DDT:
   - `A> DDT`                    (Starts DDT with an empty TPA)
   - `A> DDT GAME.COM`           (Loads GAME.COM into TPA starting at 0x0100)
   The prompt changes to a hyphen (`-`).

2. DDT Command Reference:
   - `D [start][,end]` : Display Memory Dump in hexadecimal and ASCII.
     Example: `-D 0100,0130`
   - `L [start][,end]` : Disassemble memory into Z80 assembly language mnemonics.
     Example: `-L 0100,0120`
   - `A address` : Single-line inline assembler! Allows typing Z80 mnemonics directly
     into memory:
     ```text
     -A 0100
     0100 LD C, 09
     0102 LD DE, 010A
     0105 CALL 0005
     0108 RET
     0109 [Press Enter to finish]
     ```
   - `X [reg]` : Examine and modify CPU registers. Typing `-X` displays all registers:
     ```text
     C0 Z0 M0 E0 I0 A=00 BC=0000 DE=0000 HL=0000 SP=FD00 PC=0100
     ```
     Type `-XA` to change the accumulator, or `-XPC` to alter the program counter.
   - `T [n]` : Trace (single-step) execution of `n` instructions, printing register
     state after each step.
   - `U [n]` : Untagged trace: steps through code without following subroutine `CALL`s.
   - `G [start][,stop]` : Go: executes code starting at `start` until reaching breakpoint `stop`.
   - `F start,end,byte` : Fills memory range with a constant byte value.
   - `M start,end,dest` : Moves (copies) a block of memory to `dest`.
   - `Control-C` : Exit DDT and return to CCP.


40.5 DUMP.COM - Hexadecimal & ASCII File Inspector
--------------------------------------------------
Purpose: Reads any disk file and displays a clean 16-byte-per-line hexadecimal and ASCII
listing on the screen.

1. Syntax:
   `A> DUMP filename.ext`
2. Example Output:
   ```text
   0000  C3 5C 01 00 00 00 00 00  00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00  .\..............
   0010  53 49 4E 43 4C 41 49 52  20 5A 58 20 53 50 45 43  SINCLAIR ZX SPEC
   0020  54 52 55 4D 20 2B 33 20  43 50 2F 4D 20 50 4C 55  TRUM +3 CP/M PLU
   ```
   Ideal for examining binary headers, corrupt disk sectors, and non-printable text files.


40.6 SUBMIT.COM - Batch Script Execution System
-----------------------------------------------
Purpose: Automates sequences of CP/M commands by reading a `.SUB` batch script file and
generating a temporary `$$$.SUB` command queue for execution by the CCP.

1. Writing a Batch Script:
   Create a text file with extension `.SUB` (using WordStar Non-Document mode or `ED`):
   ```text
   ; COMPILE_AND_TEST.SUB
   MALLARD $1.BAS
   STAT $1.*
   DIR B:
   ```
2. Running SUBMIT:
   `A> SUBMIT COMPILE_AND_TEST DEMO`
   - Parameter substitution: `$1` is replaced with `DEMO`.
   - `SUBMIT` creates `$$$.SUB` on the root disk.
   - The CCP automatically pops commands from `$$$.SUB` until all tasks complete.


40.7 HELP.COM - Interactive On-Line Help Facility
-------------------------------------------------
Purpose: Displays comprehensive, on-screen interactive documentation for all CP/M
commands, utilities, concepts, and system architecture.

1. Launching HELP:
   - `A> HELP`                   (Displays master topic directory)
   - `A> HELP PIP`               (Displays detailed help on PIP)
   - `A> HELP STAT OPTIONS`      (Navigates sub-topics)
2. Interactive Navigation:
   - Press `Enter` to advance to the next screen page.
   - Type a topic name or number to dive into sub-topics.
   - Type `.` to exit HELP and return to the CP/M command line.


40.8 MALLARD.COM / BASIC.COM / BAS.COM - Locomotive Mallard-80 BASIC Interpreter
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Purpose: The official BASIC programming language implementation for the Amstrad PCW and
Sinclair ZX Spectrum +3 CP/M systems, written by Locomotive Software.

1. Launching Mallard BASIC:
   - `A> MALLARD`                (Opens interactive interpreter with `Ok` prompt)
   - `A> BASIC`                  (Alias)
   - `A> BAS`                    (Alias)
   - `A> MALLARD MYPROG.BAS`     (Directly loads and executes MYPROG.BAS)
2. Core Interactive Commands:
   - `AUTO [start][,step]` : Automatically generates line numbers.
   - `RENUM [new_start][,old_start][,step]` : Renumbers program lines and updates jumps.
   - `DELETE start-end` : Deletes a range of program lines.
   - `LIST [start][-end]` : Displays program lines on screen.
   - `SAVE "filename.bas"` : Saves program to disk.
   - `LOAD "filename.bas"` : Loads program from disk into memory.
   - `RUN` : Executes the resident program.
   - `SYSTEM` : Exits Mallard BASIC and returns cleanly to CP/M!


40.9 DISCKIT.COM - Spectrum +3 Floppy Management Utility
--------------------------------------------------------
Purpose: Official Amstrad / Sinclair disk utility for initializing, duplicating, and
verifying floppy disks.

1. Launching:
   `A> DISCKIT`
2. Menu Functions:
   - 1. Format Disk: Writes fresh sector headers and 0xE5 filler bytes to all tracks.
     Supports both 180K (Single-Sided) and 720K (Double-Sided) formats.
   - 2. Copy Disk: High-speed track-by-track sector replication between drives.
   - 3. Verify Disk: Scans every sector on disk, checking CRC integrity and recording errors.
   - 4. Quit to CP/M: Exits utility.


40.10 SETKEYS.COM - Keyboard Repeat & Programmable Function Keys
----------------------------------------------------------------
Purpose: Customizes keyboard hardware repeat behavior and programs macro strings onto
function keys.

1. Launching:
   `A> SETKEYS`
2. Capabilities:
   - Configure repeat delay (time before key begins repeating).
   - Configure repeat rate (speed of continuous repetition).
   - Program macro strings into numeric keypad or function keys for instant command expansion.


40.11 SET24X80.COM & DISPLAY.COM - CRT Display Geometry & Terminal Configuration
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Purpose: Dynamically changes screen column width and terminal emulation geometry.

1. `DISPLAY` Syntax:
   - `A> DISPLAY 51` : Selects standard Sinclair +3 51-column mode using 5x8 font (Default).
   - `A> DISPLAY 32` : Selects 32-column retro Sinclair ROM display.
   - `A> DISPLAY 80` : Selects professional 80-column full-width terminal display.
2. `SET24X80.COM`:
   - `A> SET24X80`   : Directly activates 80-column by 24-row VT100 / ANSI terminal mode.


40.12 BOOT.COM & START.COM - Turnkey Startup Utilities
------------------------------------------------------
Purpose: Automated turnkey loader utilities placed on bootable disks to initialize
hardware, set terminal geometry, display welcome banners, and launch applications.


40.13 Built-in Console Command Processor (CCP) Reference
--------------------------------------------------------
The CCP is built directly into memory and executes instantaneously without disk access:
  - `DIR [filespec]`       : Displays multi-column catalog of files on active or specified drive.
  - `ERA filespec`         : Permanently deletes specified files (respects $R/O protection).
  - `REN newname=oldname`  : Renames an existing disk file.
  - `TYPE filespec`        : Displays ASCII text contents of a file on the CRT screen.
  - `USER n`               : Switches active user area (0..15) for logical file grouping.
  - `SAVE n filespec`      : Writes `n` 256-byte pages from TPA (0x0100) to a disk file.
  - `SPEC`                 : Flushes disks, restores Sinclair 48K/128K display, and exits to BASIC!

================================================================================
 42. SECTION 41: PROGRAMMING IN NEXTBASIC: THE DEFINITIVE DEVELOPER'S GUIDE
================================================================================

Target Extension:    ZX Spectrum Next (NextBASIC) & FPGA Architecture
Compilation Support: [INTERPRETER / NEXT HARDWARE EMULATION]
                     (Full emulation of Next hardware registers, Turbo clock speeds,
                     Layer 2 256-colour bitmapped screen, 9-bit RGB palettes, 128 hardware
                     sprites, 16x16/8x8 tilemaps, NextZXOS/EXDOS filesystems, and extended
                     statements and functions).

41.1 Introduction to the ZX Spectrum Next & NextBASIC
-----------------------------------------------------
The ZX Spectrum Next is the official evolutionary continuation of the Sinclair ZX Spectrum
line. Conceived by Henrique Olifiers and designed with the blessing of Rick Dickinson
(the industrial designer of the original 48K rubber-key and 128K toastrack cases), the
Next implements a modern, expanded Spectrum architecture inside an FPGA (Field-Programmable
Gate Array) core.

NextBASIC, created by Garry Lancaster (author of +3e ROMs and NextZXOS), is the resident
extended BASIC interpreter for the ZX Spectrum Next. It preserves 100% backward compatibility
with standard Sinclair ZX Spectrum 48K and 128K BASIC while providing a massive suite of
first-class commands for driving the Next's advanced FPGA hardware:
  - 4 Selectable CPU Speeds: 3.5 MHz, 7 MHz, 14 MHz, and 28 MHz.
  - Multi-Layer Graphics Engine: ULA, Layer 2 (256x192, 256 colors per pixel), LoRes,
    and Timex screen modes.
  - 9-Bit RGB Hardware Palettes: 512 total colors with 256 active entries per layer.
  - 128 Hardware Sprites: 16x16 pixels with rotation, flipping, scaling, and hardware collision.
  - Hardware Tilemaps: 40x32 (16x16 tiles) or 80x32 (8x8 tiles) with smooth hardware scrolling.
  - Extended Memory & File Handling: NextZXOS virtual filesystem with dot-commands, colon
    commands, and direct SD card access.

Entering Next Mode in BasinPy:
  - Type `SPECTRUM NEXT` or `NEXT` at the prompt.
  - BasinPy initializes the Next registers, unlocks Turbo modes, enables Layer 2 and
    hardware sprite composite engines, and updates the status bar to `ZX NEXT BASIC [K]`.


41.2 CPU Turbo Modes & Clock Management (TURBO 0..3, TURBO())
-------------------------------------------------------------
The Next's Z80 core can be clocked at four distinct frequencies dynamically under program
control:

1. The `TURBO` Statement:
   - `TURBO 0`  (or `TURBO 3.5`): 3.5 MHz (Standard classic Sinclair 48K/128K speed).
   - `TURBO 1`  (or `TURBO 7`):   7 MHz (Fast 2x clock: smooth BASIC loops).
   - `TURBO 2`  (or `TURBO 14`):  14 MHz (Turbo 4x clock: fast calculations).
   - `TURBO 3`  (or `TURBO 28`):  28 MHz (Ultra 8x maximum FPGA clock speed!).

2. The `TURBO()` Function:
   Returns the active clock speed level (0, 1, 2, or 3):
   ```basic
   PRINT "Current Turbo Speed Level: "; TURBO()
   IF TURBO() < 3 THEN TURBO 3: REM Boost to 28MHz
   ```


41.3 Display Layers Architecture & Layer 2 Bitmapped Graphics
--------------------------------------------------------------
The Next display engine uses a multi-layered composite pipeline where individual graphical
planes are combined into the final video output:
  - Layer Priority: Configurable via Next registers or `SPRITE OVER`. Default order:
    `Sprites -> Layer 2 -> ULA (Standard Screen)`.

1. Layer 2 Specifications:
   - Resolution: 256 pixels wide by 192 pixels high.
   - Colour Depth: 8 bits per pixel (256 colors). Every single pixel on the screen has
     its own independent color index! No attribute blocks, no 8x8 character cell limitations,
     and **ZERO ATTRIBUTE CLASH**!

2. Controlling Layer 2:
   - `LAYER 2, 1` : Enables Layer 2 display.
   - `LAYER 2, 0` : Disables Layer 2 display.
   - `LAYER()`    : Returns 1 if Layer 2 is active, 0 if disabled.

3. LoRes Mode:
   - `LORES 1` (or `LAYER 1, 2`): Enables the 128x96 pixel 256-color low-resolution mode.
   - Ideal for retro blocky games and fast pseudo-3D engines.


41.4 Color Palettes & 9-Bit RGB Color Depth
-------------------------------------------
The Next hardware features an authentic 9-bit RGB color palette:
  - Color Encoding: 3 bits Red (0..7), 3 bits Green (0..7), 3 bits Blue (0..7).
    This produces a master gamut of $8 	imes 8 	imes 8 = 512$ vibrant colors!
  - 8-bit Palette Indices: Memory pixels store an 8-bit index (0..255) referencing the
    active palette table.
  - Next Transparency Key: Color index **`227` (`0xE3` / Magenta)** is the standard
    transparent key color across sprites, tilemaps, and Layer 2. Any pixel drawn with
    color 227 allows the underlying layers to show through!

1. The `PALETTE` Statement:
   - Set Layer 2 Palette Entry:
     `PALETTE LAYER2, index, rgb9_value`
     Example: `PALETTE LAYER2, 1, %111000000`: Sets index 1 to bright pure red.
   - Set Sprite Palette Entry:
     `PALETTE SPRITE, index, rgb9_value`
   - Set ULA Palette Entry:
     `PALETTE ULA, index, rgb9_value`
   - Reset All Palettes:
     `PALETTE RESET`: Instantly restores default system color tables.


41.5 Hardware Sprites Programming
---------------------------------
Next hardware sprites are rendered directly by the FPGA raster engine on the fly. Up to
128 hardware sprites can move, rotate, scale, and collide simultaneously with ZERO CPU
slowdown and zero flicker!

1. Sprite Pattern Bank:
   - Up to 64 unique patterns (Patterns 0 through 63).
   - Each pattern is exactly 16x16 pixels. At 8 bits per pixel, each pattern occupies
     256 bytes ($16 	imes 16$).
   - Loading Patterns from BASIC `DATA`:
     ```basic
     SPRITE DEF pat_id FROM start_line
     ```
     Reads 256 consecutive values from `DATA` statements directly into hardware pattern memory!

2. The `SPRITE` Statement & Attributes:
   - Position & Pattern:
     `SPRITE id, x, y, pattern [, palette_offset]`
     * `id`: Sprite instance (0..127).
     * `x`: X screen coordinate (0..319; 32 is left edge of main screen).
     * `y`: Y screen coordinate (0..255; 32 is top edge of main screen).
     * `pattern`: Pattern index (0..63).
     * `palette_offset`: 0..15 (adds $16 	imes 	ext{offset}$ to color indices).
   - Visibility:
     `SPRITE ON id [, pattern]` : Activates and reveals sprite.
     `SPRITE OFF id`            : Deactivates and hides sprite.
     `SPRITE CLEAR`             : Deactivates and resets all 128 hardware sprites.
   - Movement:
     `SPRITE MOVE id, dx, dy`   : Adds relative offsets `dx` and `dy` to coordinates.
   - Flipping:
     `SPRITE FLIP id, flip_x, flip_y`: Mirrors pattern horizontally or vertically.
   - Rotation:
     `SPRITE ROTATE id, angle`  : Hardware rotates sprite (0, 90, 180, or 270 degrees).
   - Scaling:
     `SPRITE SCALE id, sx, sy`  : Hardware magnifies sprite (1x, 2x, 4x, or 8x size).

3. Hardware Collision Detection:
   The FPGA checks pixel overlap between active non-transparent pixels on every video frame:
   - `SPRITE COLLISION(id)` returns non-zero if sprite `id` collided with another sprite.


41.6 Hardware Tilemap Engine
----------------------------
The Next includes a dedicated hardware tilemap coprocessor that draws backgrounds and
scenery with instantaneous 50Hz scrolling:
  - Formats:
      * 40 columns by 32 rows of 16x16 pixel tiles.
      * 80 columns by 32 rows of 8x8 pixel tiles.
  - Statements:
      `TILEMAP init`             : Initializes tilemap hardware matrix.
      `TILEMAP CLEAR`            : Clears tilemap grid to tile 0.
      `PLACE AT row, col; TILE(n)`: Puts tile `n` at grid coordinate.
  - Smooth Scrolling:
    Configure horizontal and vertical pixel scroll offsets in Next registers for silky-smooth
    scrolling without redrawing the screen!


41.7 Extended Next Functions, Register Control & Bitwise Operators
------------------------------------------------------------------
NextBASIC includes a comprehensive set of built-in functions:
  - `REG(n)`: Reads hardware Next register `n` (0..255).
    Example: `PRINT "Core: "; REG(1)`
  - `NEXTREG n, val`: Directly writes value to Next hardware register `n`.
    Example: `NEXTREG 7, 3`: Sets 28MHz Turbo via register 7.
  - `PEEK$(addr, len)`: Reads `len` bytes from memory into a BASIC string.
  - `HEX$(num [, len])`: Converts number to hexadecimal string (e.g. `HEX$(255)` -> `"FF"`).
  - `BIN$(num [, len])`: Converts number to binary string (e.g. `BIN$(10)` -> `"1010"`).
  - `DEC$(num [, len])`: Formats number as decimal string.
  - `EXP$(str)`: Dynamic expression evaluator.
  - `MOD(a, b)`: Modulo remainder operator (`10 MOD 3` -> `1`).
  - `<<` and `>>`: Bitwise left and right shifts (`1 << 4` -> `16`).


41.8 NextZXOS, ESXDOS & EXDOS Disk Filesystem
---------------------------------------------
NextBASIC provides native disk navigation and file streaming commands:
  - Directories: `PWD`, `CD "dir"`, `MKDIR "name"`, `RMDIR "name"`.
  - File Maintenance: `COPY "a.dat" TO "b.dat"`, `RENAME "old" TO "new"`, `ERASE "file"`.
  - Dot-Commands: Fast utilities prefixed with `.`:
      `.ls`, `.cd`, `.cp`, `.mv`, `.rm`, `.cat`, `.vstat`, `.help`.
  - Colon-Commands:
      `:DIR`, `:CD`, `:ERASE`, `:TYPE`, `:REN`, `:COPY`.
  - Stream I/O:
      `OPEN #3, "SCORES.DAT"`
      `PRINT #3; score; ","; player$`
      `CLOSE #3`


41.9 Complete NextBASIC Game Project Tutorial: "Cosmic Defender Next"
---------------------------------------------------------------------
Here is a complete, working NextBASIC arcade game showcasing 28MHz Turbo speed, Layer 2
stars, 256-color hardware sprites, collision detection, and AY-3-8912 audio:

```basic
10 REM ====================================================================
20 REM COSMIC DEFENDER NEXT - Authentic 28MHz Next Hardware Action Game
30 REM ====================================================================
40 SPECTRUM NEXT
50 TURBO 3: REM Boost to 28 MHz!
60 CLS: BORDER 0
70 SPRITE CLEAR
80 REM --------------------------------------------------------------------
90 REM 1. Load Player & Alien 16x16 Patterns (256 bytes per pattern)
100 REM --------------------------------------------------------------------
110 SPRITE DEF 0 FROM 5000: REM Pattern 0: Player Ship
120 SPRITE DEF 1 FROM 5200: REM Pattern 1: Alien Invader
130 REM --------------------------------------------------------------------
140 REM 2. Initialize Game Variables
150 REM --------------------------------------------------------------------
160 LET px = 140: LET py = 180: LET p_speed = 4
170 LET ax = 60:  LET ay = 50:  LET a_dx = 2
180 LET score = 0: LET lives = 3
190 REM Place Player (Sprite 0, Pattern 0)
200 SPRITE 0, px, py, 0: SPRITE ON 0, 0
210 REM Place Alien (Sprite 1, Pattern 1)
220 SPRITE 1, ax, ay, 1: SPRITE ON 1, 1
230 PRINT AT 0, 0; INK 7; "SCORE: "; score; "  LIVES: "; lives; "  [28MHz NEXT]"
240 REM --------------------------------------------------------------------
250 REM 3. Main 50Hz Real-Time Arcade Loop
260 REM --------------------------------------------------------------------
270 IF INKEY$ = "o" OR INKEY$ = "O" THEN LET px = px - p_speed
280 IF INKEY$ = "p" OR INKEY$ = "P" THEN LET px = px + p_speed
290 IF px < 32 THEN LET px = 32
300 IF px > 270 THEN LET px = 270
310 SPRITE 0, px, py, 0
320 REM Move Alien
330 LET ax = ax + a_dx
340 IF ax < 32 OR ax > 270 THEN LET a_dx = -a_dx: LET ay = ay + 10
350 SPRITE 1, ax, ay, 1
360 REM Check Collision between Player (0) and Alien (1)
370 IF SPRITE COLLISION(0) THEN GO SUB 1000
380 PAUSE 1
390 GO TO 270
1000 REM --- Player Hit Subroutine ---
1010 SOUND 7, 55; 6, 25; 8, 15: REM Explosion sound effect
1020 LET lives = lives - 1
1030 PRINT AT 0, 24; INK 2; FLASH 1; lives
1040 PAUSE 20
1050 SOUND 8, 0: REM Silence sound
1060 IF lives <= 0 THEN GO TO 2000
1070 LET ax = 60: LET ay = 50
1080 RETURN
2000 REM --- Game Over ---
2010 SPRITE CLEAR
2020 PRINT AT 10, 10; INK 2; FLASH 1; "G A M E   O V E R"
2030 PAUSE 100
2040 STOP
5000 REM --- Pattern 0: Player Starfighter (256 bytes) ---
5010 DATA 227,227,227,227,227,227,227,255,255,227,227,227,227,227,227,227
5020 DATA 227,227,227,227,227,227,255,252,252,255,227,227,227,227,227,227
5030 DATA 227,227,227,227,227,255,252,248,248,252,255,227,227,227,227,227
5040 DATA 227,227,227,227,255,252,248,248,248,248,252,255,227,227,227,227
5050 DATA 227,227,227,255,252,248,248,248,248,248,248,252,255,227,227,227
5060 DATA 227,227,255,252,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,252,255,227,227
5070 DATA 227,255,252,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,252,255,227
5080 DATA 255,252,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,252,255
5090 DATA 255,252,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,252,255
5100 DATA 255,252,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,252,255
5110 DATA 227,255,252,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,252,255,227
5120 DATA 227,227,255,252,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,248,252,255,227,227
5130 DATA 227,227,227,255,252,248,248,248,248,248,248,252,255,227,227,227
5140 DATA 227,227,227,227,255,252,248,248,248,248,252,255,227,227,227,227
5150 DATA 227,227,227,227,227,255,224,224,224,224,255,227,227,227,227,227
5160 DATA 227,227,227,227,227,227,224,224,224,224,227,227,227,227,227,227
5200 REM --- Pattern 1: Alien Cruiser (256 bytes) ---
5210 DATA 227,227,227,227,227,227,227,224,224,227,227,227,227,227,227,227
5220 DATA 227,227,227,227,227,224,224,224,224,224,224,227,227,227,227,227
5230 DATA 227,227,227,227,224,224,252,224,224,252,224,224,227,227,227,227
5240 DATA 227,227,227,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,227,227,227
5250 DATA 227,227,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,227,227
5260 DATA 227,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,227
5270 DATA 227,224,224,227,227,224,224,224,224,224,224,227,227,224,224,227
5280 DATA 224,224,224,227,227,224,224,224,224,224,224,227,227,224,224,224
5290 DATA 224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224
5300 DATA 224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224
5310 DATA 227,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,224,227
```
